# The packages under `internal/`

**Every package states its own rationale in its package doc.** `go doc ./internal/coord` is the authority on coordination, not this file. What follows is a map, so you know which doc to read: one line per package, saying what it is for. A row that still runs longer holds decisions its package doc has not absorbed yet (#1474 folds them in package by package); read it with the doc, and record a new decision in the doc, never here. A row may not grow (`internal/commentgate`), and a new package gets a one-line row.

## The spine — what everything else is built on

| Package | What it is for |
|---|---|
| `queue` | The EventQueue contract: durable mailboxes, ephemeral ask/broadcast, dead letters; the `memory` and `jetstream` backends certified by one `queuetest` suite. |
| `coord` | Cross-process ownership: TTL leases with a monotonic fencing epoch, plus everything else a fleet has to agree on (`fleet.go`). THE DOCUMENT FAMILIES ARE GONE, both native backends left them for ordered logs, and what survived is the KEY GRAMMAR (`keys.go`), because every class the fleet still writes composes an id, a stream name or a node id into a subject token path and each of those can carry a byte a token cannot, the activation pointer (a COMPARE-AND-SET: there is no leader, so the flip is what stops two operators overwriting each other, and the sealed company document rides INSIDE its record as a JSON object, because as a key of its own a racing writer's body replaced the one the winning flip named; and its expectation is STRICT on both backends, ADR-0057: a write expecting a revision lands only while the pointer names it, and a pointer deleted or purged since the read is a race rather than nothing to have raced with, because every caller reads the pointer first and passes `ExpectAbsent` when it read none, and under the lenient rule this replaced the KV backend refused that write over the removal marker while the twin landed it, a 409 on every config write for up to three hours and a quarter; only `crewlet run -import-company` passes neither) and per-node apply status (which names the revision the node SERVES beside the one its epoch asked for, because the two differ exactly on a node that refused the fleet's revision, the epoch it last APPLIED beside the one it attempted, because a refusal says the node did not reach that epoch and never which one it did reach, and the one it BOOTS ON beside both, because the boot row moves only after an apply has succeeded and that write is local and best effort, so a node runs a revision it would not restart on for as long as a failed write takes to repair, and a reader keeping what a node needs in order to start, which the Kubernetes operator does with a revision's sealed credentials, would otherwise drop exactly what its next boot needs; `GET /fleet` reports all three per node), the completion ledger, the delivery dedupe, the custody claims (which one data node keeps each batch of a stateless node's events), the fixed-window COUNTER (`Counter.Allow`, each key carrying its window's width) in two buckets: windows up to `MaxRateWindow`, one minute, in `crewlet_rate`, the notification valve's and the Dashboard Assistant's per-person turn rate, a bucket that keeps a few multiples of that widest window, bounded by the age it actually holds; and windows up to `MaxCounterWindow`, a UTC day, in `crewlet_counter_windows`, aged three days, the remote check cells' box-minutes, since either age in the other's bucket is wrong: a day's count reaped between its charges, or a minute's record kept for days; both backends refuse a wider window rather than restart its count mid-window, the TOKEN COUNTERS (one record per scope, `org`, a seat's `agent:` or, where a company caps the assistant, a person's `person:`, with a slot per calendar window, day, ISO week, month on the company's clock, each carrying its window's LABEL, so the roll onto a new window happens inside the compare-and-swap that counts the charge and there is no reset of any kind, ADR-0019, and written by ONE compare-and-swap per node per scope however many charges the node has in flight: coalesced and decided in arrival order, each exactly as a serial order would, because every round of every seat writes the company's record and a write per round made every round in flight a contender no attempt bound covers; every compare-and-swap loop on a fleet record is bounded by its HEAD COUNT, the nodes writing the key, with a wait counted in the round trips the node measures on the bucket, never by a constant below its writers), the capacity window (ONE record that is both the operation and the exclusion, beside the per-node admissions and acknowledgements the handshake is made of), the OBJECT STORE's backend record (create-only, compared at every boot, so a node naming another store refuses to start rather than splitting the files between two) and its collector's last report (and NO LOCK of any kind on an object, since a key named only by the write that uploaded it leaves a deletion nothing to race, ADR-0027), which seats a person PAUSED (one compare-and-set record per seat, filed under the seat's ID, as the inbox it holds is named; with no age, because an expiring pause is a resume nobody chose; every node watches them, a paused seat's holder holds its inbox and its next holder takes that hold before attaching it), each human seat's CHAT READ STATE (its person's read cursors, per-room mutes and do-not-disturb, one record per seat merged forward under a compare-and-set so two tabs never lose each other's progress, kept under `iam.RecordOwner`, so the seat's next holder inherits it as it does the seat's inbox; every node serving sockets WATCHES them and pushes a changed record to that seat's own tabs, derived from the record rather than told by the node that took the write, through the one watch the pauses use (`kv/watch.go`); with no age, because an expired cursor marks a room its reader had finished as unread; and in a bucket of its OWN although its retention is the positions register's, because it is the busiest record the fleet writes and as a class there its flushes would share the one raft group and leader the trim's inputs go through) and the company's SECRETS, last-write-wins for an operator's rotation, with a version on every row and the conditional writes (`CreateSecret`, `UpdateSecret`) the engine's own key material and a rekey need, a create for a company key's mint, an update at the version a rekey read, since a plain put of a key is how two minters each derive under their own, and a plain write of what a rekey read undoes whatever landed in between. A RESOURCE NAME IS SEGMENTED for the same reason, `seat:<id>` is the two segments `seat` and the seat's id (derived from its immutable handle, ADR-0013, so a lease, a mailbox and a pause all name a seat the same way on every node), so its lease and epoch keys carry the CLASS as a subject token and `ListLive`/`PreferredResources` take a `Class` rather than a prefix string: the membership read asks the broker for one class, the presence leases, instead of every lease in the fleet, and the sweep's hints for the seats instead of every epoch the deployment has ever minted. There is deliberately NO all-classes listing and no empty segment, because both fail the same silent way, a filter matching nothing answers exactly like a class with no members, and `seat:` writes a key nothing can decode, so the lease lands and no listing ever returns it, which every node reads as a free seat. One class is the PROCESS'S rather than a seat's, a node's or a duty's: `taker:<incarnation>` (`ClassTaker`), the sandbox's word that a process still keeps the executor sessions it began taking, claimed afresh by every process, so it counts its tokens on ONE counter for the whole class (`CounterResource`, which both backends mint through), since a counter per taker would leave a record per process start in the ageless epochs bucket, and its own key is in the duty bucket, whose age removes it three hours after its last write. A lease whose work goes on only inside the window its renewals confirm is renewed `KeptRenewals` times a TTL: the engine's kept duties and the taker lease, one count for both. A RENEWAL OF A RECORD THE STORE LAST WROTE IS ONE ROUND TRIP (`kv/known.go`): the KV store keeps, per resource, the record it last wrote and the revision the broker gave it, and renews with one write at that revision, which lands only while the record is still exactly that write (a revision is never handed out twice), so the leader read that came before it told the store nothing; a write refused on that comparison reads and decides as before, and `TryAcquire` starts its first round from the same knowledge for a resource its own owner holds, so the presence's renewal is its write and the read-back. ONLY A RECORD THAT CANNOT LAPSE WHILE IT CAN BE READ is known, a seat or presence lease at the seat lease bucket's whole age, because a duty or a shorter seat lease is judged by its deadline and a write at its revision would renew it after it lapsed. Every "do I hold this?" is three-valued, held / definitively not / **unknown**, and treating unknown as loss tears a healthy company down over a two-second store blip. A LISTING IS THREE-VALUED TOO, every key live throughout it, or unknown, never a shorter list, and on the KV backend that is CERTIFIED rather than trusted, because a pass cannot tell it is complete: a renew rewrites a key behind the marker that ends the pass, and 848 of 3,634 membership reads missed a live lease. So each listing reads the stream's key index beside the pass and reads again, from the stream LEADER, every key the index names that the pass lost or delivered only as a delete marker, never through the bucket's own get, a direct get that a replica behind the quorum answers "not found", and a marker because a replica behind a re-creation serves one for a key live throughout, and an index answered while the stream has no leader is refused, so an election is unknown rather than a short fleet. A pass that goes five seconds without a delivery once the index has answered (`passIdle`, the client's own idle-heartbeat interval) is ENDED and certified, because the client's end marker never comes for a pass whose pending messages were removed before it reached them. The removal MARKERS that re-read pays for are swept by the maintenance duty from every ageless bucket after `MarkerRetention` (three hours, which is `MaxDutyTTL`), each purged through its own revision: a COST bound, never a correctness one, since no read here takes a marker as an answer and a create asks the leader what the key holds rather than conditioning on a marker a sweep may already have removed. Retention here is a BUCKET's age, never a per-write TTL, a delivery CLAIM included: it carries its own deadline only so that a lapsed claim is taken over by a compare-and-set, while the claims bucket's age (`MaxClaimTTL`, set by the longest claim anybody takes) is what removes it, and a claim asking for longer is refused rather than clamped. The DETACHED EXECUTORS' record (`SandboxRuns`) is the one opaque value here, what a job can make large kept BESIDE it as PARTS, each under a key of its own (`SandboxRunPart`, `MutateSandboxRunPart`, one of `coord.RunParts()`: `RunLog`, the bridged tool log, `RunHanded`, the sealed secrets a job's box was handed, and `RunLeads`, the turn's leads a later brief tells again; on the KV a second subject token, so a listing of records filtered on `*` never reads one), each written only beside a record (a create that finds the record gone after it removes itself), EVERY part removed with the record by its delete unless a record of the turn's next run stands beside it again by then (each part is read, then the record, and purged only through the revision read), and, where a node stopped between the removals, any part left without a record removed by the marker sweep, off the same pass of the runs' bucket its markers are found in (an update of a part so left is still taken until then, which nothing reads, since every reader comes through a record), and every change after its create is a DECISION its caller hands to `MutateSandboxRun`, which the store takes again on the record as it then stands whenever another writer landed first, through `casRetry`, within the bound every fleet key shares (`fleetCASAttempts`, twice `fleetWriters`, each attempt after a loss waiting a window of round trips), which outlasts the overlap of the calls one run's box can make at once, since one run's record takes every tool call its box makes over the bridge, from as many as twenty subagents at once, and a bound exhausted is `coord.ErrUnavailable`, never a refusal; the memory twin decides outside its lock too, so a decision asked twice is what every test sees as well. A run is WATCHED by its own key (`WatchSandboxRun`): its first state certified as a listing's answer is, then each later state, a closed channel meaning "stopped hearing", never a delete. And a PART beside it by the part's own key (`WatchSandboxRunPart`), under the same contract, its removal with its record delivered as a part that is gone: a node waiting on a run's tool log hears the log's writes alone, never every lease stamped on the run. `NodeStatus` also carries each node's own `api.auth.admin` POSTURE (`serve` or `refuse`, meta key `admin`), absent rather than empty on a node that serves no API, because the posture is applied at decision time and never written, so a fleet mid-rollout legally disagrees and nothing else would say so; `/query/fleet` reports it per node. It carries the ALARMS firing on the node too (`alarms`: one `AlarmMark` per kind, its kind and since when, the longest-standing first, off the node's own tracker), so every node's set is read at once and as it stands rather than only by whoever is looking at that node; behind a flag of its own (`AlarmsEvaluated`) rather than a nil list, because "nothing is firing" and "no table evaluated" are opposite claims and a nil slice is also what collecting nothing produces; and the kind and the start only, never the measurement, which moves on every evaluation. A LEASE'S META IS BOUNDED, by every backend and before anything is sent (`HoldLeaseMeta`: `MaxLeaseMetaBytes`, 8 KiB, refused as `ErrValueTooLarge`), and measured in the ONE encoding the KV backend writes a lease record in (`EncodeLeaseRecord`, JSON without HTML escapes, which `LeaseMetaBytes` measures by), because the presence is the one lease that carries a Meta and it is re-sent on every renewal; the figure is DERIVED, never chosen: the presence at its worst under Tier A's label limits and the status core's own bounds (the alarm table, `MaxBuildBytes` for the build, cut in `Meta`), rounded up to the next kibibyte, which `internal/engine`'s `TestThePresenceLeaseAtItsWorstFitsItsBound` holds both ways. And BESIDE the status, never in it, a Gitlet node's presence row carries what its git cache gives its BACKGROUND WORK (`GitBackgroundKey`, `git_background_bytes`: the cache's maintenance budget, re-read every maintenance pass), which every Gitlet node weighs every other by to place a repository's maintenance (`estate.Router.Maintainer`) rather than asking each in turn. It is kept outside the status because a beat whose status hook overran drops the status whole, and a placement input dropped with it would leave the node saying nothing of what it can hold for that beat; the seat host writes it on EVERY beat from memory, ZERO INCLUDED (a Gitlet node with nothing to give says so), so a row without it is a node NOT SAYING (`GitBackgroundFromMeta`'s false), never one with nothing to give; read through the one `intFromMeta`, since every target is 64-bit. A roster asked PER REQUEST is a LEASE VIEW (`view.go`): listed once a heartbeat and answered from memory, and WATCHED between listings (`WatchLive`, every claim, renewal and release of one class as the holder it left, narrowed at the broker like a listing); a write that disagrees with the last listing lists again at once, held to `MinViewRefresh`, and a renewal that agrees lists nothing, because a view that only listed learned of a join a heartbeat late, and a fleet booted at once, every first listing taken before any presence lease existed, routed for its first heartbeat as though each node were alone: a data node committed every push it received itself, and a node without `data` refused every push, fetch and read. The watch is begun before the first listing and is a HINT, never the answer, since a lapse is no write: the cadence still drops a node that stopped without releasing. A positions row read back carries the instant the STORE took it (`NodePositions.StoredAt`: on the KV backend the entry's own timestamp, the broker's clock, filled by every listing and never written) beside the writer's own stamp, so every row read back says how far its writer's clock stands from the broker's with no request of its own, and a register's rows, one broker clock storing them all, say how far each node's clock stands from the others': what the engine's `clock_skew` alarm compares against their median. A node's MODELS ride its status record beside its MCP servers and its runners (`NodeStatusRecord.Models`, `NodeStatusRecord.Runners`, below): one row per entry with only a 12-character prefix of its sign-in id, bounded text, never a fan-out. Three model classes live in the positions register, one record per entry: `llm_retry` (a person's retry, the later stamp kept, applied by its stamp), `llm_device_sign_in` (a device sign-in in progress, whose lifetime is a record field its readers keep, since a bucket age restarts at every update) and `llm_check` (`ModelCheck`, an entry's latest check, running on the node that asked it and read by whichever node a poll reaches: LOST once still running past its deadline, LAPSED a linger after it ended or after that deadline, its answer the API's rendering, opaque here and held to `MaxModelCheckAnswerBytes`, 64 KiB). Two MCP classes live there too (ADR-0074): `mcp_listing`, one node's listing of one server, written under the node's PRESENCE EPOCH and counted only while that presence holds at that epoch (`LiveMCPListings`, the one filter), and `mcp_definition`, a tool definition WHOLE (its schemas, and its title, hints title and description, which a listing cuts to a list's summary while the digest covers every byte) stored once, create-only, under its digest, so a change is shown by what the server actually changed; both bounded by the store as well as the writer, a listing's server and node stamped from its key, and the definitions one listing stores held to `MaxMCPListingDefinitionBytes` (1 MiB) in all by the node that builds it, a definition waiting for a review kept first. `mcp_restart` (`mcprestart.go`) is a person's request to restart an MCP server, one record per server and per seat named, a STAMP like a model retry (every node restarts what began before it, the later stamp wins, no age). `MCPInstance` (`mcpinstance.go`) is the wire shape of one instance's health between nodes, answered at query time and never stored, and the status record's MCP row carries only its bounded summary (`down_since`, `error_class`, `restarts`). `mcp_check` (`mcpcheck.go`) is each MCP server's LATEST TEST, the current value only (the later test wins, no age), bounded by its writer to 32 nodes and 512-byte sentences and refused by `Validate` past that; never its tools. `mcp_accepted` (`mcpaccepted.go`) is the DEFINITION EACH MCP TOOL'S LEVEL WAS DECIDED ON, one record per (server, the server's own name for the tool), written by COMPARE-AND-SET at the version read (zero creates only), its words bounded as a listing's; a removal accepted keeps its record, marked, so a server stays baselined (ADR-0078). `mcp_oauth` and `mcp_sign_in` (`mcpoauth.go`) are each MCP server's OAuth grant, its PUBLIC half only (who signed in, the issuer and client, the scopes, when it runs out, the vendor's words for a renewal refused for good, never a token), watched by every node, and a sign-in in progress, whose PKCE verifier and client are sealed in it and whose state is spent by moving it on at the version read; the sealed half of a grant is `fleetsecrets.Estate`'s (ADR-0075). A VALUE IS HELD TO WHAT ITS LEADER'S READ CARRIES BACK: every read of a key is the stream leader's, whose reply carries the value base64-encoded, and a leaf's server closes its whole link on a reply past the transport (crewlet/crewlet#1031), so one key holds at most `coord.MaxValueBytes` (6,290,542 bytes: `jsapi.MaxReadable` of the transport less the most header a write carries, which this package's test derives) and a settled bucket refuses a put or an update past it before anything is sent, on every node alike, since a member's value is a leaf's read, as `coord.ErrValueTooLarge`, which `unavailable`, `writeUnknown` and `createKey` pass through unmarked (a refusal made before sending is neither an outage nor a write that may have landed); the twin refuses the same values, and the activation pointer's form is the contract's (`coord.ActivationValue`), so both size it alike. The values near it are held to it where they are made: a sandbox run's row and log (`sandbox.RowBytes`) and a config write's document (`configapi`, before the revision is stored). A KV WRITE ACROSS A LINK WAITS AS LONG AS ITS BYTES TAKE: every write of a settled bucket whose caller set no deadline waits the request term and, where the store reaches its buckets across a link (`kv.Reach`: `AcrossLink` or `InProcess`, stated by whoever opens it, the engine from its broker's kind, and refused unset), the crossing of its key, value and headers at the object store's floor for every send the client may make of it inside one context (`jsapi.WriteSends`: the first and the client's own re-sends of a write nobody heard, which a KV write cannot turn off), every call of the client given that WHOLE bound at once (`jsapi.Sends.Whole`), fixed when the write starts, so an attempt after an in-flight answer waits within it and a write the leader keeps answering in flight ends `Undecided` by it (`jsinflight.Decide`'s budget); held to the client's five seconds alone, a write whose caller set no deadline and whose value took longer to cross failed as the store being unavailable while it crossed, and an MCP listing of up to 896 KiB failed the same way under the fifteen seconds of the beat that wrote it, on any link below about 60 KiB a second at its bound. A CALLER'S OWN DEADLINE IS KEPT, so A WRITER THAT BOUNDS A CHANGE OF A VALUE OF MEGABYTES FOR A REASON OF ITS OWN ADDS THE CROSSING ITSELF: the sandbox runs' contract says how long a change of a value at what one key holds takes to cross the node's connection, its read and every send of its write (`SandboxRuns.ValueCrossing`: `MaxValueCrossing`, 780.03 seconds, across a link at the floor — the read a change's decision is taken on, its reply of eight mebibytes four minutes, and the write's three sends, derived from the rules a leader's read of the runs and a settled write's budget are given, `kv.Reach.readCrossing` and `kv.sendCrossing`, at the longest key a run's write is sent under, and held by `kv`'s test to what that read and that budget give such a change; none in process, the twin included), which a run's writers add to their reasons (`sandbox.RowBudget`) — held to a reason alone, five seconds or thirty, a run's row of megabytes was cut while its bytes were still crossing (crewlet/crewlet#1076). A CASE'S STORE ACROSS A SLOW LEAF LINK is `kv/kvtest` (`SlowLeaf`: a member and a leaf joined to it across `jetstream/linktest`'s relay, the member's store to seed and read back through, crossing nothing, and the leaf's to write through; `AtTheBound` pads a value to what one key holds), shared by the sandbox's, the engine's and natsobj's cases across a link; `kv`'s own cases keep theirs, since a test of a package cannot import one that imports it. A NODE'S SUMMARY OF WHAT IT RUNS ON (`nodeinfra.go`, ADR-0142 as amended) rides its status record, hosts and names only and bounded. A NODE'S STATUS RECORD (`NodeStatusRecord`, `NodeStatusRecords`) is its MCP, model and runner rows and its infra summary, OFF its presence lease, because the presence is rewritten on every beat and carries what placement and liveness read, and those rows grow with a company's configuration: one record per node in its own bucket (`<prefix>_node_status`), aged at the apply status's `StatusFreshness`, so a dead node's record goes with no sweep, and joined to the presence by the epoch it was written under (`LiveNodeStatuses`, the rule `LiveMCPListings` follows), so a restarted node's earlier record is never counted as the live one's, and removed at a clean stop only through the epoch its own node wrote at (`DeleteNodeStatus`), so a later incarnation's record under the same node id stands; its wire form is written by hand (`Encode`, `DecodeNodeStatusRecord`) over the row encoders, a key this build does not know ignored, and the node is the key, never a field |
| `store` | The node's local databases: its own file (audit log, learning memory, config revisions) and the replicated estate beside it, which only a state log's applier writes; backup copies, locks and migrations. |
| `statelog` | The replicated state-log framework: one ordered stream per domain is the write-ahead log every data node derives identical SQL rows from, with its write authority, read levels, retention, recovery and alarm table. |
| `textindex` | The engine's own lexical search: the analyzer that turns a document into terms, BM25 ranking over the inverted list, and term search of a single text; pure functions, the SQL is `search`'s. |
| `keyset` | A row's place in an order as the per-depth ARMS that read the rows after or before it, each one index seek, so a later page costs what the first does; NULL is a position, last in both directions |
| `search` | Knowledge search: the BM25 lexical index, the 1-bit semantic first stage (an IVF index replicated on the vector log) with exact rerank and rank fusion, the chat and change indexes, and the cross-node fan-out. |
| `events` | The event envelope and the typed-payload registry that reconstructs events off the wire, with the event-type catalogue and its feed and rate rules. |
| `org` | The organization model: the hierarchy, the seats that hold it, and the derived seat identity every node shares. |
| `iam` | The engine's identity vocabulary, a leaf of values only: principals, the three login grammars, permissions and proof recency, and the one `OwnerOf` that resolves whose record a name is. |
| `authz` | The one authority table: whether a principal may do a verb to an object, decided by `Decide` over a row per verb, the route policy and refusal envelope, and the artifact, assistant and requester-ceiling rules. |
| `authz/orgchart` | `authz.Chart` over ONE `org.Organization`: who leads a person, a project, a unit and a page container, whether somebody leads anybody, whether a seat SITS in a unit or below it (its own unit chain compared by pointer; leading a unit from outside it is not sitting in it), which is what a share with a team admits, and whether a seat's ROLE carries a grant (`SeatGranted`: an AGENT seat's `health_tools`, a human seat and an unknown grant opening nothing) — with `GrantedSeats`, the same rule over the whole tree, so the seats a grant admits and the seats a wake about it reaches cannot differ — pure arithmetic over a tree, tested against a tree rather than through a running engine (measured, the walks inside the engine's own methods could have their loops deleted with that suite still green). Its own package because TWO callers hold a tree: the engine asks about the company it is RUNNING (`engine.ChartAuthority`, which delegates here per call), and a `/config` write asks about the two DOCUMENTS it compares — the revision it replaces and the one it proposes — which the running company is neither of. A nil tree answers `authz.ErrNoChart`, never `false` |
| `config` | The two configuration tiers, Tier A (`Bootstrap`, the operator's file) and Tier B (`Company`, the founder's stored document): their decoding, `${VAR}` resolution, validation, warnings and JSON Schema. |
| `advisor` | What a company's SETUP costs it, as findings an administrator acts on (ADR-0115): each a class, a severity, an owner's title and why, where in the document it sits, and its fix. DETECTION STAYS IN `config`: a finding about the document is a `config.Warning`, so `crewlet validate`, every `/config` write and every apply log raise the same check, and a warning with a `config.AdvisoryClass` gets a fix built here. A FIX IS DATA: one entity write per option (entity, id, the top-level fields to set, the revision summary), which the dashboard previews through the entity route's dry run and the administrator sends under the entity's own tag. The package imports no store, coordination, state log or API package (`TestTheAdvisorImportsNoWritePath`), so nothing here can apply a fix. Read off the REDACTED document by `GET /advisor`, with every integration's last reconcile findings beside it (`FromIntegrations`), whose owner or engine side is the finding kind's own verdict. BESIDE IT, WHAT THE COMPANY DID: `Detect` turns the tracker's `WorkPatterns` read of the last 7 days into the engine's own findings (`actor: engine`, `source: evidence`, nothing to send), each a fixed reading of records with its threshold beside it and no model call (`TestNoDetectorCallsAModel`), held to its own timeline in `internal/scenario` and to silence on the healthy one. ACCESS AN AGENT ASKS FOR (ADR-0118) is a closed catalogue here (`access.go`): `request_access` names a key, and `AccessFix` builds the entity change for the asking seat alone. THE CATALOGUE IS DERIVED, NEVER LISTED: the health tools, then one key per level of every permission a seat's GitHub App may hold (`github.SeatPermissions`), each merging what its level needs (`github.Needs`, so Workflows write brings Contents write), so an agent can ask for any GitHub gap the Agents tab could close (`TestTheAccessCatalogueIsWhatASeatCanHold`). A vendor's levels are not in it: there a level replaces a role, and an administering one is the Agents tab's to grant. THE ATLAS'S FINDINGS (ADR-0141, `atlas.go`): `DetectAtlas` reads five classes off the folded view and the sources' standings it is handed, one finding per class with its count and at most five entries, NAMED ONLY WHERE `AtlasEvidence.Names` says the reader holds the atlas app (`TestAReaderWithoutTheAtlasAppIsToldHowManyAndNeverWhich`). Their fix is `atlas_act`, an atlas tool call the reader sends; the `advisor` question alone adds them, so the insights sharer never reads one. |
| `advisor/analyst` | The setup advisor's analyst: one model run over the week's records as read tools, whose findings the engine grounds before any is listed (ADR-0157). |
| `atlas` | The company atlas's closed vocabulary and the one pure fold that turns typed, source-ranked claims into entries, relations and facts, plus the joins and reads over them (ADR-0126). |
| `atlas/derive` | Pure functions from what the engine already keeps to atlas claims, computed at every read and never stored. THE SETTINGS ARE READ ONLY REDACTED: `Settings` holds the redacting copy and `FromSettings` is the only way to make one, so a derivation that read the wrong field still reads no credential (`TestTheSettingsTheAtlasReadsHoldNoSecret`, the test that goes red when the copy is bypassed). Every integration kind has a row, held both ways. Records reach it as CLOSED PROJECTIONS declared here (`GitletRepo`, `GitletCheck`): a field nobody mapped, a description somebody wrote, a mirror's credential, never reaches it. A value its fact refuses is WITHHELD, named by where it sat and why, never by what it was; a `${VAR}` reference is never resolved. A tool server is known by its command's base name, never its arguments, which are no secret position. The engine's records (`Records`) arrive the same way, as projections with NO FIELD FOR AN EMAIL ADDRESS (a vendor account's name, who signed in, a plan's account), a vendor's free text or an error a node beat THE FLEET'S OWN ENTRIES (ADR-0142, `fleet.go`): each live node a server keyed `crewlet/<id>`, the broker a queue on its members or on an external cluster's servers, the object store a bucket, and the engine the service over them, all from `FleetNode`, the closed projection of a node's presence and the infra summary its status record carries. An address is claimed only through `atlas.FleetAddress`, always private. |
| `atlas/gather` | Reads the atlas: asks each place the engine keeps what it knows, through interfaces declared here (Gitlet's read, as `estate.CodeSeam` serves it), derives and folds. GITLET IS READ AS THE READER, so a repository only its unit may read reaches only that unit; only repositories the settings do not declare are read in detail for their other hosts. A SOURCE THAT FAILS IS REPORTED, never hidden and never fatal: every answer carries its coverage, which sources it read whole and why not, so a partial read is never drawn as a whole one. THE ATLAS'S OWN LOG IS ONE MORE SOURCE (`Log`, `SourceLog`): what people recorded folds beside what is computed, and the answer hands back the claims and the position it was current through for a writer that decides on them, never sent anywhere. Every record source is the engine's own read as it stands (integration standings, the seat access ledger, tool server listings and sign-ins, node beats, connection tests, the access log's `HostUse`, the usage history), held by compile-time assertions; a connection test counts only against the server's current settings. `BenchmarkRead` measures the whole read of a realistic company Standing `mcp_` and `llm_` alarms join their tool server or model entry as signals, by `statelog`'s own per-entry kinds, never by a kind's name and never with the alarm's sentence. A caller's own reason for not reading Gitlet (`Request.GitletUnread`) is said on its coverage only where the company keeps code there: a company that keeps none has Gitlet read in full, and no push use counted. The node leases it already lists for health also fill `derive.FleetNode`, from each row's profile and its `coord.NodeInfra`. |
| `atlas/collect` | The atlas-collect duty (ADR-0129): reads each connected integration's vendor on an interval and records on the atlas log only what moved. |
| `atlas/codescan` | THE ATLAS'S READ OF A GITLET REPOSITORY'S DEPLOY AND DEPENDENCY FILES (ADR-0133): what its default branch declares the company runs (services, the databases, queues and buckets beside them, the vendors its code calls) and how they relate, BY EXACT KEYS ONLY, each claim in the repository's own voice (`atlas.CodeSource`), LIKELY until a person confirms it. `Parse` is PURE and never fails: one listed file, read as its format into CLOSED TYPES, so an environment block, a command, a build argument, a Secret's data and a dependency's version have NO FIELD to be decoded into (`TestNoParserDeclaresAFieldForAnEnvironmentValue` walks the types and fails on such a field, a map of strings or a type that takes whatever a file holds; `TestTheFieldGateCatchesABrokenParser` proves the walk can fail), and what is decoded reaches a claim only through `collect.Builder`, which refuses a credential-shaped key or value (`redacttest.Shapes` is planted in every parsed field). A NAME MEANS SOMETHING ONLY ON A CLOSED TABLE (`tables.go`: images and charts that are a database or a queue, operators' resources, SDK packages per registry, Terraform providers and resource prefixes); nothing is guessed from a similar name. A parser's panic is an unparsed file, never the node's: the bytes are anybody's with a push. `Scanner` is a WORKER OF THE HOME LOOP (it satisfies gitlet/home's `Worker` without importing it; the engine registers it): `Due` lists every scanned repository whose tip (`gitlet.ReadDefaultTips`) is not the one its standing says was read whole, and every one with a standing that is no longer scanned; `Work` answers a nudge that moved no tip from the standing it last read, with one row read and no store read. A PASS lists the tip's files in ONE git read, reads only files whose blob it holds no reading of, at most `MaxReadsPerPass` (50), and PUBLISHES ONLY ONCE EVERY LISTED FILE IS READ, through `collect.Replace`, so the atlas never holds half a commit and a file that stops declaring something withdraws it at once. NEVER SCANNED: a `visibility: unit` repository, because what a scan records is on the log every holder of the atlas reads; a scratch, archived or importing one. A repository that stops being scanned, or is renamed, has its old voice withdrawn whole. STATE: what the files declared is the atlas log's; each repository's standing (`Status`: the tip read whole, `Rules`, what it listed, read, refused and recorded) is the coordination store's, in a bucket of its own (`CoordStore`, coord.AtlasCodeScans); each blob's reading is this node's MEMORY (`readings`, bounded by `MaxReadingBytes`, the repositories read longest ago forgotten first), whose loss costs one more read of a repository's listed files at its next move. `Rules` is raised by a build that reads more or differently, and every repository is read again though its tip has not moved. THE SAME READER READS A FORGE'S REPOSITORIES (ADR-0136, `Forges`, the collect duty's `collect.CodePass`): a GitHub or GitLab repository Gitlet does not mirror is listed and served by its forge (`collect.CodeHost`) and judged here, by `reader.settle`, the one place the closed list, the by-presence formats and the whole-commit rule are held for both hosts; `Forges.blob` holds the list AGAIN where a forge is asked for bytes, and holds the answer to the object listed by its git hash. A pass is bounded (`MaxReadsPerPass`, `MaxForgeBytesPerPass`, `MaxForgeTreesPerPass`), records its calls, trees, files and bytes, leaves a refused repository as it was, releases an unlisted one only where the listing is whole, and keeps its standings under `forge/` in the scan's bucket (`ForgeStore`), which `CoordStore.Load` SKIPS: read as Gitlet's, each would be a repository Gitlet no longer lists, and released. `Parse` takes a `Repo` (its voice, its short name, its atlas key), so a forge repository's service is built from the forge's key. |
| `atlaswake` | THE OWNING AGENT IS WOKEN WHEN ITS ATLAS ENTRY DRIFTS, AT MOST ONCE A UTC DAY (ADR-0143): what drift is (`Drift`: health turned to alerting, a contested value, a disputed one, a CLOSED set, each open one named by a digest so no record holds a value), who owns an entry (`Owned`: the seat the row shows where the chart holds it as an AGENT, never a person, a unit or an owner only suggested), the record that bounds the wakes and the one function that moves it (`Decide`), the duty's loop (`Worker`, the fleet singleton `atlas-wake`), and the inbound source `atlas_drift` with its parser and prompt. THE BOUND IS A COORDINATION RECORD a seat and entry (`coord.AtlasWakes`), written only at the revision read and CLAIMED BEFORE THE PUBLISH, so two holders of the duty cannot both wake a seat and a failed publish is made again as the same wake id rather than a second wake; not the shared day counter, which cannot be given back and cannot say what the seat was told. A SEAT THAT TAKES NO WORK IS SKIPPED WITH NOTHING WRITTEN (the engine's `seatTakesWork`), unlike every other wake, which the inbox holds: a day's one wake is not spent on mail read days later. THE DELIVERY IS IDS AND CLOSED WORDS ONLY, refused otherwise, and the prompt reads its metadata back through the closed sets: do not add the entry's name or a value to it, which are a vendor's or an agent's words. A PARTIAL READ FORGETS NOTHING (`Reading.Whole`). |
| `atlasdomain` | The state log's thirteenth domain: what people, agents and integrations' reads tell the engine about what the company runs on, as claims on one subject an entry (ADR-0126). |
| `advisordomain` | The state log's fourteenth domain: what the Advisor found and what became of it (each finding key's episodes and their history, runs, weekly reports, alarm episodes), one subject a finding key. |
| `configplane` | The control plane: how a fleet agrees which company configuration it runs, via an append-only activation pointer and per-node apply status, and the posture a node takes when it lacks the current one. |
| `seat` | Which seats this node runs: greedy fair-share claim and give-back by lease, a freshness-based admission gate, the heartbeat and presence row, and the watchdog. |
| `estate` | The replicated estate ROUTED, from every node — ADR-0025 and its amendment. ONE `Router` runs on EVERY node, with or without `data`, and every seam the tools, the operator's surfaces (the API's tracker, knowledge-base, artifacts and chat routes, the operator's MCP) and the duties consume is answered through it, so a tool cannot tell which kind of node it is on. Every data node holds the whole estate, so the router answers in-process where THIS node's copy serves — held to this node's floors exactly as a remote data node is — and otherwise asks the data nodes its `Placement` names (every live data node, off the WATCHED presence view): the one that answered last first, then the `rendezvous` order on its own id, a node gone silent last for thirty seconds, over that serving node's OWN subjects rather than a scatter or a queue group, because the asker must know which node an unanswered write went to — one for every operation but a push's proposal, which a node answers on a LANE of its own (`crewlet.estate.<node>.proposals`, `proposalAnswers` 3 136 wide: the SUM of what the committer and its two sibling writers each hold node-wide — `gitlet.CommitterNodeProposals` 1 088, `gitlet.ChangeWriterNodeWrites` and `gitlet.ChecksWriterNodeWrites` 1 024 each — every one refusing busy at once past its own share, so no component's burst holds a slot another's requests need), because a proposal holds its answer slot while it waits for its batch, and on the shared registration sixty-four queued pushes held every slot the node answered its reads, its other writes and a git fetch's resolve through; a lane answers only the operations declared on it (`op.answeredOn`) and refuses any other unserved, and a node registers every lane or none. A copy that is WRONG is OUT OF SERVICE — a peer asking is told `out_of_service`, and its node keeps its seats while their calls go to peers — and a copy that LAGS its logs is a worse node to ask, never no node: asked last, and told to answer anyway — and so is a node confirmed BEHIND THE COMPANY'S CONFIGURATION (shed or stuck, never lag alone: `Backend.Current`, the engine's `admits`, the reconciler's `Admits`), on every operation but admission's ungated ping, because what it supplies from its own epoch — the chart a people field and a lead resolve by, a unit, a search's scope, the code limits — is the revision the fleet replaced, a value every reader meets anyway when a revision changes one, while a refusal would cost a fleet whose only data node failed an apply its tracker, pages and usage; it answers a reason of its own, `behind_config`, which an asker REMEMBERS for `suspectFor` (per node, never per copy: the posture is the node's) and meanwhile skips the node for — asking it only as the last resort, after every copy that only lags (whose answer holds the current configuration, in that order whether or not the asker has heard the refusal yet, so every asker sends a repository's pushes to one node), told at once to answer anyway, and a push's runner-up instead — because unlike a lag it LASTS (until the apply succeeds, a stuck node's until a person acts), and forgotten between requests it cost every request one refusal per such node and every push to a repository it commits for its whole proposal; `engine.TestEveryEpochSeamAServingDecideReadsIsClassified` holds every seam a serving decide reads (`worklet.WriterDeps`, `Backend`, `ServerSeams`, the code committer's, change writer's, store's and reader's options, and the options each searcher, reader and store behind a `Backend` field is built from — `doclet.SearcherOptions`, `doclet.ReaderOptions`, `doclet.Options`, the search fan-out and the engine's item ranker) to a class — `grant`, `value`, `gate` or `none` — so a grant judged from the chart, which being asked last cannot make sound, is never added unseen; and an operation that JUDGES one DECLARES it (`op.judgesGrant`, a predicate over its decoded arguments: `code.read` and `code.resolve_ref` where the asker is a SEAT; `code.change` wherever the chart judges the write, a seat's or one that mentions anybody (`gitlet.ChangeProposal.JudgesChart`); `code.queue` for every enqueue, whoever asks, and a seat's dequeue (`gitlet.QueueProposal.JudgesChart`); `code.merge` for every merge, whoever asks (`gitlet.MergeProposal.JudgesChart`); and `code.conversation` wherever the chart judges the write — a seat's, an ask of reviewers by anybody, or a comment or review that mentions anybody (`gitlet.ConversationWrite.JudgesChart`) — and so wherever a read names the colleague it is made for, which rides beside a seat's asker alone — a person's read consulting no chart; and `code.checks` for a seat's rerun, which judges whether it may write the repository or wrote the change and whoever its turn works for (`gitlet.ChecksRequest.JudgesChart`), a person's rerun and report and the engine's own records consulting none), which such a node answers `behind_config` with the hard arm's own detail EVEN TOLD TO ANSWER ANYWAY — in its server and in this node's own in-process run alike — so the last resort meets the refusal again and the walk ends unserved rather than answer from a revision that may have revoked the grant (M3-O8's hard arm; `TestEveryOperationThatJudgesAGrantIsDeclared` holds every operation on a domain's copy whose arguments carry a `gitlet.Principal` named `Asker` to the declaration, and no git operation to it, whose Gitlet node refuses a seat itself) — and a READ a copy refuses about ITSELF (`statelog.ReadRefusal.Elsewhere`: `behind` a linearizable read's barrier, stalled, deferred, evicted, below the floor, cut off from its broker or a quorum) is asked of the next data node, the refusal the answer only where nobody does better, while `log_full`, `broker_refused` (the one stream every node appends its barrier to refused this one's, sealed or out of resources) and `maintenance`, true of every node alike, are answered at once: returned as the answer, a git fetch through a node whose applier fell a second behind was told 503 while two peers could serve it; and the walk PAYS FOR THE READ ONCE, since every node asked after a refusal is handed what the refusal says it paid for (`statelog.Spent`: the read index its barrier established, which the next node waits for rather than append its own; every position a node waited its whole bound for, a floor among them, which no later node waits for; a log a barrier ran out on, which gets no second barrier), where each node asked afresh appended a barrier to the log they were all behind on and waited a whole read budget, two seconds and a barrier per data node for one final `behind`. The failover rule is PER OPERATION CLASS — a read, a tracker write carrying its operation id, a page or chat write carrying its caller's key (`doclet.Actor.OpKey`, `chatlet.Actor.OpKey`; a seat's always does) and chat's erase and prune under the caller's own id move on, from an `unvouched` answer too, under the same id; a page or chat write with no key (whose op id the store mints per call) reported unanswered is `ErrOutcomeUnknown` and never repeated — and every request carries this node's ONE SESSION-FLOOR table, on the operation's OWN domain's log only (a wake's trigger position among them), so whichever data node answers has applied the node's own writes or says it is behind — and says so while the asker is still listening, because every wait a node makes before answering another (that floor, a read's barrier and level, a write's own record) ends `gitlet.ReplyMargin` short of the asker's deadline (`statelog.WithReplyMargin`): a `behind` sent after the asker stopped waiting is heard as a node that is gone. A read nobody answered, or a walk whose every data node REFUSED, is `ErrUnserved` (a git walk's saying it was the GITLET nodes that could not answer, `ErrUnserved.Git`), never an empty answer — except the knowledge search, which is best effort and answers that it did not run — and it says whether a node it asked WENT SILENT (`Silent`), because a node that refused ran nothing and a silent one may have: a push the walk could not place is `unavailable` only when every node refused it, and `unknown` otherwise; a repeatable write no data node ran, where some data node NEVER ANSWERED, ends in `ErrOutcomeUnknown` naming the silent ones, because such a node may have run it and "nothing ran" is the claim a caller acts on by writing again — the same operation id is the retry. What cannot cross a wire (the chart seams, the lead map, the searching seat's org) is supplied by the SERVING node's epoch, and three gates hold the wire honest: every field an operation's types carry arrives or is named with who supplies it, every sentinel and error type of statelog and of every domain an operation floors on — the executor log's among them, and the identity estate's own (`iam.ErrNoHolder`, `iam.ErrHolderUnseated`, `iamdomain.ErrNotFound`, `session.ErrNoCompany`) — keeps its errors.Is/As identity across it, and the envelope itself is pinned byte for byte. The IDENTITY reads a node without `data` needs are estate operations too, READ-CLASS and floored on the identity log: who holds a login (its binding row, for `iam.Holders`, `iam.OwnerOf` and a Tier A token's seat), who holds each human seat (`SeatHolders`, read whole, for the party registry's standing and a turn's withheld contacts) and who holds ONE, with the holder's grants (`identity.requester`, `Identity.Requester`: two seeks and one document's decode on the serving node, for the person a seat's turn acts for, composed at most once a call, and a new name rather than `identity.seat_holder` reshaped, so a node that does not know it leaves it unserved and no answer is read as a person holding nothing or everything) and the row a presented machine token is checked against (`identity.machine_token`) — the chart and the asking node's own Tier A token list applied on the ASKING side, since neither is the serving node's to know. Each crosses NARROWED: a binding without the row's verifiers and grants (`Binding`), a requester with the grants a turn's ceiling is decided on and nothing else of the person, and a token's row whole but for its VERIFIER, in whose place crosses a MAC over it and a nonce the asker drew for that question alone, under a key both nodes derive from the fleet keyring in a domain of its own (`TokenFacts`; the asker names its active entry and a node holding none runs nothing) — the asker MACs the verifier the presented token implies and takes it only where the two agree, so `credential.CheckToken` stays the one table, neither the secret nor the verifier reaches the broker, and an answer MAC'd for one question verifies under no other question's nonce; the keys ride in `Envelopes`, the one value both halves are handed. CHAT is a floored domain beside them, its search included (the rooms a seat may search are membership rows its own join may just have written): every read crosses with its VIEWER, the seat handle the asking node resolved, inside the signed body, and the SERVING reader decides that seat's rooms from its own membership rows — so a visible set never crosses, and the search resolves it on the node whose index it reads, in one operation that says when that index is still building and names the room each hit was said in, its name and kind from that same set (`ChatSearchAnswer.Rooms`) — while what a caller was admitted to do beyond membership crosses as the asking node's DECISION (`chatlet.MessageAuthority`, `chatlet.ChannelAuthority`), judged on the serving snapshot, and a room's retention is a write of its own rather than a field of a patch; the live channel's room check asks this node's own copy first even while it lags (`Chat.ChannelHere`), never one out of service. THE EXECUTOR LOG is the seventh floored domain (ADR-0073), and its writes are a class of their own: ENGINE-ORIGINATED (`opOrigin`, declared with `byEngine`), the engine's record of what a seat's box did — a turn's first read (`begin`), a capture, an end, the retention's deletes and a removed seat's — which no tool, person or operator surface makes, so `SurfaceWriteDomains` (what `agent/builtin`'s surface declarations are held to) leaves it out — where the code log stays in it, every one of its writes made by a person, a seat's tool or a push and none of its operations declared `byEngine`, the engine-built commit and Gitlet's own sweeps included, since each lands as the push or record its surface would have made — and two gates hold the class: every write on the log is the engine's and nothing else is, and no package serving a person or a model (`agent/builtin`, `api`, `assistant`, `cmd/crewlet`) names the writer facade `ExecutorWrites`, the domain's writer or an engine hand-out of either, by a walk of their source. Each is an idempotent write under the operation the domain derives per execution; its reads (`ExecutorReads`, which a surface may hold) are answered at the caller's level, a launch's conversation read at `linearizable` because a seat that moved holds no floor for the end another node published, and a seat's search of its own sessions (`executor.search`) on the node that holds the rows, which answers its hits alone, a data node of an older build answering it unknown and unserved so the router asks the next; and both facades are exactly the domain's method sets, so the engine cannot tell local from routed. A node in a maintenance mode is served every core half's reader and none of its writers. And every request and every reply is SIGNED under the fleet keyring in a domain of its own (`estate`) and judged by the verdicts a state-log record is (ADR-0031), through `signedask` — the one implementation every instruction channel shares, because the server dispatches a write AS the actor, and answers a chat read FOR the viewer, its envelope claims, and an unsigned channel beside signed records would let anything the broker admitted write as anybody — its admission vouches for a connection, never a request: a key the server does not hold answers that the request DID NOT RUN, so the router moves on, and a tampered envelope is refused and logged. A custody batch is an event envelope rather than an estate request, and stays outside the rule. A push, a compaction, an expunge record and a change's write are the KEYED operations (`op.keyedBy`: `code.propose`, `code.compact`, `code.expunge` — a person's purge request, or a maintainer's begin or commit, keyed by whichever it carries — and `code.change`, an open, an edit, a draft's state, a retarget, a close or a reopen, keyed by the repository its record names, and `code.queue`, an enqueue or a dequeue of a target's merge queue, and `code.merge`, a change's merge as one record its gate decides (ADR-0096), each keyed the same way — and beside them `code.conversation`, a write of a change's conversation, and `code.checks`, a write of a repository's checks — an attempt queued, claimed or ended — each keyed by the same repository and answered by the change writer or the checks writer beside that node's committer, raising this node's session floor but never the repository's own, since neither moves git content — all on the proposals lane, a compaction, an expunge record and a change's write waiting in the repository's one queue beside its pushes): each asked of its repository's committer — the first of `rendezvous.Order(repo id, every live data node)`, this node at its place in that order and never ahead of it, with no sticky node and the same out-of-service and silent demotions every order has — rather than of this node first, so a repository's pushes meet at one committer whichever node received them, and a committer that runs nothing (`gitlet.ErrNotServing`: stopping, a node that publishes nothing, a refusal about itself) hands them to that repository's runner-up with the same push id — or compaction id, which the domain's ledger collapses alike — and is asked LAST for pushes for the `suspectFor` window after (no sticky node moves a keyed order off a node that refused, so every later push re-sent it its whole proposal first); even the lagging last resort asks in the key's order. A repository's GIT WORK is asked of its HOME GITLET NODE instead (ADR-0089, amending ADR-0025): a second placement beside the data nodes, `RouterOptions.Git` (every live node with the gitlet role, off the same watched view; required, an empty one the way to say there is none), for an operation declared `keyedOnGit` on `GitCopy` — no domain's copy, so no copy verdict, no dispatch floor and no lag gate, its floor travelling in its arguments as the Gitlet node's cache's sync level (`CodeSeam.FloorOf` hands it this node's own writes to that repository) — asked in `rendezvous.Order(repo id, every live Gitlet node)` with the keyed order's demotions (a `no_backend` asked last for `suspectFor`, a silent node last of all) and none of the data walk's preferences, never `remembersBehind` or the last resort; this node at its place, in process, where its git half is built (`LocalGit`, resolved PER REQUEST, because Gitlet is built at boot or at the first apply that hosts code, after the router and the server exist), every other Gitlet node on LANES of their own, the reads' and the engine writes' (`laneGitReads` `queue.ConcurrentAnswers` wide, since the node's seat git-work share bounds the git running; `laneGitWrites` HOLDING at most `gitWritesHeld` (64) writes for its peers, mostly through waits that run no git, and `queue.ConcurrentAnswers` wider for the answers it gives AT ONCE — a write past the held ones is answered `behind`, at its share, before its arguments are decoded, never left waiting for a slot in the broker client's buffer, which drops past its 64 MiB, where its asker heard nothing for a whole write attempt and took the busy node for a gone one; a held write holds its request, its admitted copy and its decoded write, about 3 MiB at the payload's bound, measured; registered both or neither, only where `ServerOptions.Git` is set and from boot, answering `no_backend` until Gitlet is built, never silence), its server's git arm after the envelope's admission and the lane check and before the copy's verdict and the readiness gates, one attempt `Timing.GitReadAttempt` (17 s: a share wait, two read budgets, the read's own git's ten seconds of run time and the reply margin, whole; the read lane's queue and the short checks of what a read names are left out and come out of the attempt), each attempt SERVED UNTIL ITS ANSWER IS DUE, a reply's margin before it ends (`gitlet.AnswerBy`) — the git arm for a peer and this node's own half in process alike, the in-process run held to the same attempt — so every wait the read makes ends there and is answered unready while the asker still listens: served to the request's deadline and merely marked with the margin, which nothing on the git path reads, a read waiting on its share or a slot answered after its asker gave up, and the asker suspected a busy node and sent its repositories' reads to a runner-up that downloaded each. A Gitlet node that cannot answer for a reason about ITSELF (`gitlet.ErrGitUnready`: syncing, its share, no chart, behind its configuration, a failure of its own, or no copy held) answers `behind` — `behind_config` for a seat on a node behind its configuration, the node's posture, which the asker remembers for its data operations too — with the reason beside it (`reply.Unready`), never an error on the wire, and is NOT demoted — the syncing home is the right node a moment later — and ONE node a walk FILLS a copy at a time: the walk stamps its effective home's attempt (`gitlet.GitQuery.Home`, the first node once the demotions apply) and every other Gitlet node holding none answers `not_held` having started nothing, so a repository nobody holds costs the fleet one download, never one a Gitlet node; the fill moves to the next node of the walk only past a node bringing no copy up for the read (`gitlet.UnreadyReason.PassesFill`: behind its configuration, a failure of its own, not serving Gitlet), never past one that may be filling — kept on the first node, a home stuck behind a revision left every seat unable to read a repository it alone held — and never past a home at its share, which holds its copy and is only busy: the share is the node's, so a repository's seat reads are bounded by the shares of the nodes holding a copy of it. `Router.Home` names a repository's home — the node its reads and engine writes are asked of first, and the one its maintenance falls back to — the first node of the git walk's order with NO demotion, through the one function every keyed order is built by (`Router.keyed`), so on a quiet fleet the home and the reads' first node never disagree, and a home that went quiet is still the home, answered or the error of a placement that could not be read (`ErrNoGitletNode` where it lists no Gitlet node), never a guess. A home's own work is its repositories' CHANGE WORK (`gitlet/home`) — a repository's compaction and publication are its maintainer's (ADR-0091) — and `Router.GitOrder` answers a repository's WHOLE git order — every live Gitlet node in its rendezvous order, the home first, with no demotion — which a check's claim is ranked by on every Gitlet node alike and a running check's runner is judged present by (internal/gitlet/checkrun); `Router.HomeOf` answers which of MANY repositories this node is the home of off ONE reading of who serves git — what the home loop's backstop pass asks once over its workers' listings, which asked one repository at a time read the presence view, decoded every node's lease and sorted the Gitlet nodes for each — three-valued: the set, or for every one the error of a placement that could not be read (`ErrNoGitletNode` where it lists no Gitlet node), so a node that cannot tell does none of a home's work. `Router.GitOrders` is `GitOrder` of MANY repositories off ONE reading, for HomeOf's reason — a code search's scatter (`codesearch`) assigns every repository an asker may read, up to `config.CodeMaxRepos` of them, to the first node of its order and its runner-up to the second, and a reading a repository would read the presence view again for each and could place one scatter on a fleet that moved part way through it. `Router.Maintainer(repoID, charge)` is the Gitlet node a repository's MAINTENANCE — the compaction of its packs, the publication of its layers — is placed on: the first of the SAME order (`Router.keyed`, the rendezvous order Home reads, still with no demotion) whose ADVERTISED background budget holds the charge (each Gitlet node's `coord.GitBackgroundKey`, which its presence row carries on every beat BESIDE its status, so a beat whose status read overran still says it), else the home — the order and every figure read off ONE LISTING (`GitPlacement.Listing`, the git placement's `RouterOptions.Git`: the Gitlet nodes and what each says, read together), so a node is weighed by the listing that names it rather than ordered off one listing and weighed off the next a refresh put in its place, and a node that listing does not carry — this one included, wherever its own git half is built — is GONE, never not saying; so on alike nodes the maintainer IS the home, and on a fleet with one large Gitlet node every repository only it can hold is maintained there; NOTHING MOVES PAST THE HOME ON MISSING INFORMATION: a node whose row carries no figure is NOT SAYING, never one with nothing to give, and is passed over where it is a runner-up — kept in its place, a small runner-up not saying would take a large node's repositories, each beginning with a cold sync of its whole history only for its own admission to refuse the work — and keeps the repositories it is home to, as a quiet home keeps them, since the home holds their copies and a quiet home that cannot hold the work costs that repository its pass, refused at its admission over the copy it already holds; `Router.MaintainerOf` answers which of MANY repositories, each at its own charge, this node maintains off ONE listing for them all — what a maintainer's two passes ask once over their due listings, which asked one repository at a time read the presence view, decoded every node's lease and sorted the Gitlet nodes for each — three-valued: the set, or for every one a listing that could not be read, UNKNOWN, or one that carries no Gitlet node, `ErrNoGitletNode`; `Router.Maintainer` and `MaintainerOf` judge through one function (`maintainerOver`), so the two can never disagree; and `Router.Listed` is whether that listing carries THIS node at all — by the listing, never by its git half — which a maintainer's two loops wait on before their first pass, since a booting node is listed only once its host beats. Gitlet reaches it through `Router.Code()` (`CodeSeam`), which raises this node's floor only for its OWN writes — a proposal that landed, a push's, a compaction's or an expunge record's, and a push it proposed found landed — never for a read's position, and raises it TWICE: the session's on the whole code log, which every routed code operation carries, and the written repository's own (`FloorOf`, `repoFloors`), which a read by object id of that repository carries and a read of any other does not — floored at the session's, a push to one repository made every other's home sync for nothing on every read. The node keeps `config.CodeMaxRepos` repositories' floors, forgetting the lowest past it, which costs a read of that repository at most the Gitlet node's one sync at the log's head. A MERGE PREVIEW (`code.merge_preview`, `opCodeMergePreview`, `CodeSeam.MergePreview`) is a git READ like a read by object id — the same walk, the reads' lane and attempt, its attempt stamped home, a node that cannot answer saying `behind` with its reason — answered by the git half's `GitServer.MergePreview` (the merge a repository's home records a change's mergeability by, `gitlet/home`), its floor the query's own, raising none. An ENGINE WRITE (`code.git_write`, `opCodeGitWrite`, `CodeSeam.GitWrite`) walks the same git order as an `opIdempotentWrite` keyed by its push id — derived from its caller's operation, so the runner-up asked the same write after a node that ran nothing (`gitlet.ErrNotServing`, declined and asked last) or went silent builds the same push and is answered from its row where the silent node landed it — held to the one rule every party to a write holds it to (`gitlet.EngineWrite.Validate`) before any node is asked, and raising this node's floors only where it applied or is pending; its attempt on one Gitlet node, `Timing.GitWriteAttempt` (82 s: a share wait, three read budgets, the build's ten seconds, the upload's allowance, the proposal's own attempt and the margin, whole; every term's p99 measured beside its budget by BenchmarkEngineWrite, inside each but the share's wait, which sixty-four writes on a one-unit share reach whole and are sent on unready, and every answer's p99 3.2 s at the most), is the router's own whatever deadline its caller gives (`budgetFor`), since the Gitlet node fits every stage into it and only a wedged one costs it, and `NewRouter` refuses one no longer than the proposal's attempt and the margin. The router's attempt budgets — a read's and a write's on one data node, a git read's and an engine write's on one Gitlet node, and the admission question's whole — are ONE `Timing` (`RouterOptions.Timing`), `Production()` on every node, so a suite in another package can scale a WEDGED node's cost without reaching an unexported field (a node that is gone costs no attempt: nothing serves its subject). A shorter attempt never hears an honest node as silent — the serving node fits its waits into whatever attempt it is given, a reply's margin short of it — so `NewRouter` refuses only a field left zero by name and an attempt that cannot do its job: a read attempt no longer than `gitlet.ReplyMargin`, which leaves the serving node no time to wait for anything or run the read, and a write attempt no longer than the floor wait, a committer's batch and its answer (`gitlet.MinProposeAttempt`: `statelog.ReadBudget` + `gitlet.CommitBudget` + `gitlet.ReplyMargin`, 33 s — the domain's ONE sum of what a proposal needs, which Gitlet holds an engine write's upload to as well, so the two never disagree), under which a push whose committer first waited out its floor is answered busy, undecided. Production's ten-second read attempt is a linearizable read's waits at their whole budgets and the margin (2 + 5 + 2 + 1). A repository's readers are ONE PAGE A ROUTED CALL (`Usage.RepoReaders` over `usage.repo_reads`, with `usage.repo_reads_elided` on the first page; both floorless): the asker's page budget travels with the call, capped by the serving node's, every data node cuts and resumes by the one rule, a page that does not end after its cursor is refused, and a cursor or a range no read takes crosses the wire as its sentinel. A REPOSITORY LIFECYCLE REQUEST (`code.lifecycle`, `opCodeLifecycle`) is routed to any data node like a settings change and DECIDED THERE against that node's own chart and configuration (`judgesGrant`, its predicate true for every request, since every one names its actor and is judged by its grant), so a node confirmed behind refuses it `not_serving` and the asker tries another; the asker's floor is raised only by an answer that carries a record (`applied`, or `pending` at its position). `code.mark` is the one UNKEYED code write: a person's code inbox mark or their viewed files of a change, asked of any data node, whose writer refuses it for anybody but the person. An ENGINE WRITE'S ATTEMPT FOLLOWS ITS KIND (`op.attemptBy`, `Timing.GitWriteAttemptFor`): the git write attempt with its build term, `gitlet.WriteBuildDeadline`, replaced by the kind's own build budget, so a `merge_change` — landed as one merge record, its build a mergeability's thirty seconds — is given 102 s on one Gitlet node, a `rebase_change`, replaying up to a thousand commits under forty, 112 s, and every other kind its 82, read off the write each attempt of the walk carries, its home's included. A REQUEST A DATA NODE'S BUILD CANNOT READ WHOLE RUNS NOWHERE (ADR-0025's amendment, `wirerule.go`): an operation's INPUTS — its arguments and the actor it runs as, `request.Args` and `request.Actor` — cross under one rule, both halves the standard library's own: the router leaves every field at its zero off the wire (`OmitZeroStructFields`), so a field a later build adds reaches a data node only in a request that sets it and a field's zero means what a build without it does (the rule `define` states — and a pointer at a value its type's own IsZero calls zero is such a field, crossing as nil), and the serving node decodes them refusing a member its build does not declare (`RejectUnknownMembers`), at every depth, before it waits for a floor or runs anything, answering `ErrUnknownKeys` (its own typed code, every key named) — a read carrying a newer argument alike, never answered with it ignored — which the router reads as a node that RAN NOTHING, whatever the class, asking the next one (a keyed operation asks it last for requests carrying what it refused, and for those alone, `markUnread`; a repository's home that refused passes the fill, `passesFill`), and which ends a walk nobody served as the refusal over the walk's `ErrUnserved`, never over a write some node may have run. A VALUE NO WRITE MAY HOLD THAT THE WIRE WOULD NOT CARRY AS ITSELF is refused where it is asked (`op.refusedBeforeAsking`: a task's, an ask's or a patch's zero instant), so in process and routed answer alike. The ANSWER of a read a caller carries into another request (`op.answeredWhole`: `tracker.view_prior` to a save, `tracker.expanded_query` to the list read it expands, `code.repo_state`'s page cursor to the next page, `code.queue_due`'s queue work to the landing, ejection and settle its home asks of each entry as it read it and the close it makes for whom each was enqueued — the engine's alone, which the engine-class guard holds every person surface off, as it holds them off `TurnThreads` — and `artifacts.version_carried`, `tracker.views_carried`, `tracker.catalogue_carried`, `tracker.project_carried` and `artifactdata.schema_carried` — each a read of its own, so a screen that only shows the version, a container's views, the catalogue, the project or the schema reads it leniently; the write such a read feeds stays a whole replacement, since a merge could not tell a field cleared from one an older build never sent) crosses under the same rule the other way. wirerule_test.go's gates hold every field an operation takes to leaving the wire at its zero, every type whose own IsZero decides that zero to a reason it drops nothing (`zeroByMethod`), every pointer at such a type to a reason no reader tells it from nil (`zeroThroughPointer`), every type that decodes its own JSON to one (`decodesItself`), and every read answered whole to the request it is carried into (`answeredWholeBy`). AN ACTOR THAT MAY WRITE NOTHING IS ASKED OF NO NODE (ADR-0109): a write whose agent states nobody it writes for, or whose person carries somebody, is refused before it is asked anywhere, this node's own copy in process included, in the wire's own idiom for a value no request may carry (`actorWrites`: `op.refusedBeforeAsking` on every page and chat write whose arguments hold the actor, and on every code write whose request holds its asker beside whom the write is made for — a push, a change's lifecycle and its conversation, a write of a merge queue, a merge and an engine write (`codeWrites`, by `gitlet.Principal.WriterKind`) — each a declaration on the registered spec that a gate over the registry holds every such operation to, and `callAs` for every operation that runs as one, the tracker's and `tracker.view_prior` among them), so one answer comes in process and routed alike and no data node runs it. The serving node cannot refuse it itself: it reads an absent carrier as nobody, which is also what an older node sends, and `Actor.UnmarshalJSONFrom` states every actor that arrives whether or not a provenance came with it, since an older node sends an agent's provenance carrying nothing as no member at all. THE ACTORS CROSS UNDER THE SAME RULE as the rest of a request's inputs, and their codecs carry no policy: an actor has no encoder, so its plain encoding under the request's options is what crosses (an agent's stated for nobody sends an empty provenance, being stated is no zero), and it and `types.WrittenFor` decode through the request's own decoder (`encoding/json/v2`'s `UnmarshalJSONFrom`, returning the decoder's own error unwrapped, so the wire reads a member's path as the decoder recorded it; never an `UnmarshalJSON`, which starts again from `encoding/json`'s defaults), so a field at its zero inside an actor or its carrier stays off the wire and a member the serving build does not declare there is refused at its path; the six requests a code write forms decode themselves the same way, to state on arrival whom the write is made for whatever arrived and to refuse its carrier's kind, or an app a credential's carrier names, that the serving build does not know as a member it does not declare, named `args.written_for.kind` or `args.written_for.apps[]`, so the walk asks a node that knows it (gitlet's `writtenfor.go`, whose `unknownToThisBuild` is the one place a build decides whether it knows what a carrier names) — and so does this node's own copy handed such a request in process, whose Validate makes the same refusal of the same value first, before any other rule of the request and whatever else it holds, which the walk answers as the wire answers the same request and moves on from (`unreadHere`), so a carrier a run's row holds, which another build may have written, reaches a node of that build where one serves; the gates walk into the actors and those requests (`plainlyEncoded`, which `TestAPlainlyEncodedTypeWritesItsPlainEncoding` holds to their plain encoding and `TestATypeThatDecodesItselfAndWritesNoEncodingIsWalkedInto` holds every such type to) and name each as decoding itself (`decodesItself`). The engine's own TURN-START THREAD READ (`chat.turn_thread`, through `TurnThreads`, which the engine alone builds and the engine-class guard holds every person surface off) answers a thread with whom each message was written for, which no reader's view carries; where no data node served it and none went silent (an older build mid-roll) it reads the plain thread and says it carried nothing (`servedByNobody`), never on another refusal. THE ATLAS LOG is floored for its reads (`AtlasReads`: every live claim, an entry, the log as a page of lines and as daily counts, the purges) and a person's writes (`AtlasWrites`: a claim on the version it was decided from, an entry's purge, and a value's purge BY ITS DIGEST, so the value it erases never crosses), so the operator catalogue on a node without `data` reads and changes the atlas through a data node. |
| `rendezvous` | The one order a fleet's nodes are preferred in for a key: rendezvous (highest-random-weight) hashing, so every node computes the same placement from the same member set with no table to agree on |
| `objstore` | The fleet's OBJECT STORE (ADR-0026): the bytes of files too large for a replicated row, one object per upload under a minted `Key`, behind a `Backend` and a verifying `Store`; also the reference-table declarations the collector, audit and backup walk |
| `objstore/natsobj` | The default object backend: a JetStream object store bucket on the fleet's own broker, in NATS's own format, under a byte reservation so a full bucket refuses only uploads; it writes, reads and deletes through the stream, never the library's verbs |
| `objstore/s3obj` | The object store's S3 backend (AWS SDK) for any S3-compatible bucket: bounded, stall-watched, throttle-gated requests, multipart puts and ranged reads; `s3fake` is its in-process test server. |
