| `objstore/memobj`, `objstore/objstoretest` | The in-memory twin and the ONE suite every backend runs (`queuetest`'s idiom): an object reads back exactly, an empty one and large ones across the backend's own pieces and ranges included; a put whose reader fails — with `io.ErrUnexpectedEOF` or a wrapped `io.EOF` too, inside a piece or at its end — or whose context ends leaves nothing a stat or a listing can find, and fails with the reader's own error; a read with no deadline is refused and one whose context ends stops; a missing name is `ErrNotFound`, and a delete leaves nothing and is idempotent; a listing names every object once, verbatim, however slowly it is read, and stops at its visitor's error; an upload begun and never finished is `Pending` until `Abandon`ed; a put the backend stops taking (`Stalling`, where a backend can be made to stop) fails as `ErrStalled` between its stall and its clean-up and leaves nothing; and a name holding something the backend cannot read as an object (`Spoil`, where a backend can hold one) is `ErrCorrupt` to a stat and a get, listed holding nothing, and cleared by a delete — a twin that agreed only with itself would certify nothing |
| `objstore/collect` | The object store's one fleet duty (`object-collector`): an hourly lock-free collection of unreferenced objects and a daily audit that counts the objects the store has lost |
| `objstore/migrate` | One pass of `crewlet objects migrate --to s3`: every object a Required table names AND every one a Derived table names (`collect.Source.Moved`, `objstore.PassMove`, ADR-0081) is read verified through `Store.Open` and put into the target backend under the same name, then checked there at its size (and digest, where the target keeps one), `Copies` (4) at a time — a derived object because the moved fleet would otherwise rebuild it on every node at a cost that grows with everything it was built from, where the copy costs its bytes once; a Retired table's object stays behind, since no reader that has not caught up outlives the restart onto the target. Resumable by running it again, because a key never names other bytes and the target is the record of what was copied; a file the source cannot give back whole is reported by name, never stored, and a derived object it cannot give back is counted apart (`Report.Derived`) and holds nothing up, since the engine builds it again. It moves no record: the engine's final pass, in maintenance mode, seals the nats bucket and moves the fleet's recorded backend (`coord.ObjectStores.MoveObjectBackend`) |
| `objstore/references` | The one list of replicated tables whose rows name object-store objects, read by the collector, audit, backup and store move, and held against the schema both ways by a gate |
| `artifacts` | The artifacts vocabulary, values only: ids, kinds, capabilities, roles, shares, manifests, limits, authority stamps, upload intents, view tickets and datastore shapes; a leaf `authz` and `config` import |
| `artifacts/manifest` | Pure: the path grammar of a version (the engine's one file-path rule), reserved paths, whole and patch publishes, carry planning and the canonical manifest encoding. CONTENT ADDRESSING LIVES AT THE MANIFEST, never in an object key: keys stay random, one per upload, which is what lets the collector delete without a lock, and a carry never crosses an artifact (a company-wide hash lookup is an existence oracle) |
| `artifacts/mediatype` | Pure: the ONE extension table an artifact may publish (served type, class, compressible, scriptable). The served type is always the table's, never a client's |
| `artifacts/slideformat` | Pure: the slide format a `slides` deck holds (`deck.json` and one `<section>` per slide), checked at publish into one canonical tree the dashboard draws; a closed subset, refused rather than dropped. |
| `artifacts/svgsafe` | Pure: the ONE drawing allowlist both an inline slide `<svg>` (read by `slideformat` with the HTML tokenizer) and a standalone `.svg` (a brand logo) are held to: shapes, paths, text, gradients, clips, masks, markers and filter primitives, no script, event attribute, `style`, `<style>`, `foreignObject`, `<a>`, `<image>` or animation, every value free of `javascript:`, `data:`, `<`, `expression` and `@import`, every reference to an id of the same drawing. The standalone front end is encoding/xml in STRICT mode with no entity table, refusing any DOCTYPE or ENTITY, any processing instruction but the XML declaration, `xml:base`, a non-UTF-8 encoding and every namespace but SVG's and XLink's, matching names by their exact SVG spelling, bounding elements, depth and what `<use>` expands to (a use cycle refused), and requiring a viewBox between 1:8 and 8:1. WHAT IS STORED IS ITS RE-SERIALISATION, never the uploaded bytes: attributes sorted, everything escaped, no comment, no instruction and one namespace declaration, so a parser differential between Go's reader and a browser's has nothing to hide in. Refused with the line and the fix, never silently stripped |
| `artifacts/brandformat` | Pure: the format of a `brand` kit (`brand.json` plus its logo and font files): strict decoding, field rules, stored asset form, contrast, and the one generated brand stylesheet, canonical index and slide theme an adoption makes |
| `artifacts/canvasformat` | Pure: a `design`'s `canvas.json`: where each artboard of the version sits (boards keyed by their page's path, with position and size), the order they are drawn in, and the titles and stickies beside them. A CLOSED FORMAT, REFUSED RATHER THAN REPAIRED: decoded strictly (an unknown field is refused, not dropped), every number to its bound, and held to the version so every board is an HTML page of it and every HTML page a board, every problem answered at once at its place in the file. Also the outline the gallery and the agents read and the comment anchor (an artboard point, D65). No runtime of its own: an artboard is a plain page under the html contract (D59) |
| `artifacts/commentanchor` | Pure: where a comment on an artifact points and how its place is found again in each version, the authority of ADR-0154. One table keyed by EVERY artifact kind says which anchors its comments take (`html` a page's declared name or a point on it, `slides` a slide or an element on it, `design` a point on an artboard, every other kind the whole version), so a new kind states its anchors or fails a test. A THREAD STORES ITS ANCHOR AND THE VERSION IT WAS MADE ON, NEVER A PLACE PER VERSION: `Place` finds the anchor's content in a version's index (pages and the names they declare with `data-crewlet-anchor` or `comments.anchor`, a deck's elements by path, tag and text, a canvas's boards) at read, answering `here`, `moved`, `outdated` or `later`, so a publish re-anchors every thread with no write and a thread whose content is gone is kept, never dropped. The index holds no id and no clock, so it is cached by version |
| `artifacts/dashspec` | Pure: the format of a `dashboard` artifact's `dashboard.json` (datasets, widgets, engine source catalogue, closed deterministic calculations, outline, comment anchor) and its validation |
| `jsonform` | The strict JSON reader the artifact formats hold their indexes to, and the one reference of each index's shape an agent reads, both derived from the format's Go type (ADR-0059) |
| `artifactsdomain` | The artifacts state-log domain (`CREWLET_ARTIFACTS_LOG`): artifacts, versions, the objects they name, assets, shares, folders, the adopted brand, comment threads and their inbox notices |
| `assistantdomain` | The Dashboard Assistant's state-log domain: a person's conversations, turns, cards, proposals and files, sealed and replicated to every data node. |
| `accessdomain` | The state log's record of each seat's access (ADR-0103): what each turn was told of its own access, the SSH certificates signed, the SSH commands run or denied, and the verdicts on delegated requests (ADR-0125). |
| `accessreview` | An agent whose access changed REVIEWS ITS OPEN WORK ONCE (ADR-0111): the inbound source `access_review`, its parser and prompt, the seat's PENDING REVIEW record (kept in the coordination store's `access_review` class), the wake's derived id, and the open work a turn that tells a change lists. The node holding a seat measures it after its applies go quiet, on admission and on a seat access ledger write, against its told baseline by the closed list; a change records a pending review (first measured instant, the change in a person's words) and, where the seat has open work, publishes one request whose wake id is the seat, told record, digest and that instant, so a republish collapses and a redo after a revert is reviewed again. NO SENDER, so the turn is the company's own (ADR-0109); the change itself is told at the turn's dispatch from the access log. Any node may write the record, each write conditional on the revision it read. The record ends when the turn first told the change ends (or a check sees that turn ended), when the change is undone untold or its review is settled untold, or when the seat is removed. The section names work by key and run id, never by anybody's words (ADR-0040), and the node counts each work item once, past the listed pages too. |
| `executordomain` | The seats' executor sessions: `statelog`'s domain on `CREWLET_EXECUTOR_LOG`, one row per seat's turn naming its sealed session objects, its conversation's head and the seat's own search. |
| `executordomain/executordomaintest` | TEST-ONLY: one data node's copy of the executor log, the shipped applier and reader over a replicated estate of its own, for suites that may not name the state log (`internal/sandbox`) |
| `executorkeep` | The leaf vocabulary of kept executor work that the log's domain and the sandbox share: a turn's `Ending`, the session, harness and id bounds, `Excerpt`, the sealed shapes, `TurnRow` and `ConversationHead` (ADR-0073). |
| `executorread` | One read of a seat's kept executor session for every surface that reads one: the turn's row, the sealed object it names and a page of the transcript, with what the read came to said in one set of words. |
| `artifactdata` | The artifact datastore: shared, durable collections of documents as a strict state-log domain of its own, written by sequenced records and decided per write in the applier. |
| `artifactdata/canon` | Pure: the ONE canonical document encoding (sorted keys, numbers as decimal text) and the store state digest (a modular sum of document digests) |
| `artifactdata/query` | Pure: the datastore query grammar compiled to SQL over the field index; an unindexed filter is refused naming the fix, never answered by a scan. Every value is a bound parameter, the cursor is a keyset over the order and the document id, and a string longer than the index's 256-byte prefix is rechecked against the row so a prefix match is never answered as an equal one |
| `costlimit` | The one cost-weighted limiter: two token buckets per charge, a principal on a scope and the scope itself, paid together or not at all, PER NODE and never a fleet quota. The datastore weighs a write by the rows its apply writes; a dashboard's live sources weigh a read by the domain read it costs. One implementation so the refill, the forgetting bound and the rounded Retry-After cannot drift between the two — nor Gitlet's re-sync surface (`gitlet.NewResyncs`: ONE limiter a Gitlet node, which its coding boxes — a run on its seat — its checks' boxes — an attempt on its repository's checks — and its seats' code tools — a turn on its seat — all draw on, so a seat's boxes and tools pay one seat bucket), which charges it one a read through the code log's barrier |
| `node` | Composes a broker client, a coordination backend and the seat host into a runnable node: attaches a seat's mailbox on acquire, admits every turn (`AdmitTurn`), and drains |
| `engine` | The ENTANGLEMENT POINT — which concrete thing satisfies which seam. THE RUNTIME IS TWO HALVES, split by what depends on a company and never by domain (`core.go`, `native.go`): the CORE — the state log for EVERY registered domain, the node gate over every identity-claiming log, the identity estate's two sides (its node writer authoring as the ENGINE, `iamdomain.NodeKind`, so what the node decides for itself is never drawn as a machine's login), the directory trigger over it, and on a publishing node the trim, the identity duties and this node's own usage publisher — is published ONCE AT BOOT ON EVERY NODE THAT HOLDS THE ESTATE (`HoldsEstate`: a node with the `data` role, which `ingress` and `workers` require), company or none, because the node nobody has configured is the one a Tier A token's session signs in on to CREATE its company, and the one its first person is invited to and signs in on once that company declares the human seat they will hold (ADR-0032) — so every node that validates a session reads its own identity rows, with no cache and 503 rather than 401 when it cannot — the SEAT is the one thing that waits for a company, so `SeatView` answers a node running none with the seam's own `session.ErrNoCompany`, which the directory's writer turns into `iamdomain.ErrNoCompany` — `409 no_active_revision` at `/iam`, naming the remedy — rather than a 503 no wait clears, while the request path still reads it as unknown; and it is every domain rather than that one because a join replaces the whole replicated file, a snapshot names every registered domain and the trim counts nodes per log. The usage publisher is the core's although it is no singleton — every data node publishes its own days, from its own event log, whatever its company keeps, and a node without `data` keeps no event log, so its events reach a data node through CUSTODY and are counted in that node's day — because armed with the native half a company on Jira and Confluence never replicated a day of its spend, and a node handed its first company by an apply published none until it restarted. The NATIVE half (Worklet's, Doclet's and Chatlet's writers and readers, the lexical indexer, chat's own keyword index, the change feeds, the embedding duty, the trash reaper and chat's prune duty) waits for the first company that keeps them, and whoever reads it reads `NativeStarted` FIRST, so "not up yet" (503) is never confused with "not this company's" (404); on a node without `data` it starts REMOTE (`startRemote`): the same seams, every one answered by a data node. EVERY ESTATE READ AND WRITE goes through the ESTATE ROUTER (`router.go`, ADR-0025) — a seat's tools, the operator's surfaces and the API's work and page questions alike, on a data node too, whose own copy answers in-process under the same session floors — and the identity reads a node without `data` needs (who holds a login, who holds each human seat, and what a request's bearer is checked against: `MachineToken`'s row, whose verifier is set from the presented token only where the serving node's nonce-bound MAC confirms it, so `credential.CheckToken` decides there as on a data node, and `BoundSeat`'s binding, weighed by `boundRowOf` as a data node weighs its own) are estate operations like any other, floored on the identity log. Seats are admitted through ONE gate, `StateLogHydrated`: on a data node every core log whose health gates admission (`GatesSeatAdmission`: Worklet's, Doclet's and Chatlet's — never the identity log's, the artifacts' two, Ask Crewlet's, the code log's or a compacted one's) established strictly, AND the router serving every half the company runs natively; on a node without `data` the router's word from a data node — and `seatsAdmitted` is that gate behind a mode that publishes. ITS OPENING IS AN EVENT (`admission.go`): every log whose health gates admission tells the engine as it comes level (`statelog.RunnerDeps.OnLevel`), and so does the fleet's view's first listing (`coord.LeaseView.Listed`), and on either, while the gate's last answer to a pass was a refusal nothing has answered, the engine asks the gate again and asks the host for ONE pass the moment it admits — armed by the gate's own refusal, which is an event of its own so an opening that raced the pass is not missed, and never by the host's record of a withheld pass, which a node that cannot serve its seats writes on every pass without asking the gate — so a node that boots behind its logs, or is handed its first company, claims its seats as it comes level rather than at the sweep's next tick; a peer's copy coming to admit is the one opening no local event shows, and the pass every interval asks it. A copy that is WRONG rather than behind is taken OUT OF SERVICE (`judgeCopy`) and the node's seats STAY, reading the estate through another data node — each log's health, a stall on the checkpoint and a deferral's age all feed that judgement — while the identity log is no input to it: an identity fault feeds the vouch watch below and never takes the tracker's, the knowledge base's or chat's copy out of service. The NODE GATE judges an eviction or a readmission in twenty seconds and writes every identity-claiming log AT ONCE in thirty (`GateAnswerBudget`, fifty), and `GateClientWait()` — a minute — is the one wait the CLI and the dashboard are each held to exactly. The teardown ends the claim gate's watch first, since its gate reads the logs, the native halves and the fleet's view everything after it takes down, then the view triggers — the directory trigger and the vouch watch, since neither may outlive what it serves — then the fleet duties and the usage publisher (the native half's embedding writer and chat's prune duty before the core's usage publisher, trim and identity duties, every one before the duty leases are released; and every writer of a row a node's usage day counts — the learning passes, the scheduler, Gitlet, whose reads of a unit's repository are such rows, the auxiliary-spend ledger's last flush and the custody keeper — before the publisher's own last flush), then the native half's feeds, indexes and search answerer, and the logs last; a walk over the teardown's calls holds that order. Each domain's change feed (`domainFeed`) carries this node's replicated estate and the domain's runner beside its log, for a translator that decides a wake from its own applier's verdict; its reading of a frame is ONE function for a delivery and a dead letter's replay (`domainRecords.read`), which DEFERS a frame under a key this node does not hold and an envelope a newer build writes and acknowledges only a tampered one (#838), and every feed keeps its dead letters through the queue via ONE ledger per process (`deadLetterLedger`, feeddeadletters.go), which accounts for each entry a replay or an operator's discard drops under the claim the alarm reading's expiry count takes, so `feed_dead_lettered`'s reading (`deadLetterWatch`: one `DeadLetterMarks` read a beat, a headers-only listing only where the marks moved — every feed subject holding or having held entries when the last sequence moved, since a subscription's keep moves it and a keep beside an expiry leaves a count where it was, and only a subject whose count moved otherwise — the marks recorded only when every listing agrees with them, since a listing reads a replica, and the last answered listing kept, aged, when a read fails) counts an entry the retention let go once fleet-wide and never one somebody replayed or discarded. THE SAME READING WATCHES THE NOTIFICATION SERVICE'S INBOUND GROUP'S UNHANDLED SUBJECT (`notify.UnhandledSubject`, under its own subtree's marks), node-wide, for `inbound_dead_lettered`, and counts one the retention let go as `crewlet.notify.inbound.dead_letter_expired`; the inbound edge hands the service this ledger (`notify.Options.DeadLetters`), so the replay's drops take the same claim. The operator's surface over them (`FeedDeadLetters`, feeddeadletterops.go: `GET /feeds/dead-letters` and the discard) names its feeds from the domains' declarations through `feedFor` over a zero engine and a bare log, so a node answers for every feed whatever logs it runs; finds an entry by listing the feeds' own subjects, never another `dlq.` subject; and counts a discard only where its claim on the entry's going came first. Deliberately many small files rather than one large one -- this package is flat, and the hardest passage of all, the inbox handler, is a package of its own (`agent/inbox`); the config apply is `reconcile.go` and `epoch.go` here. THE RECONCILER IS THE ONLY ONLINE WRITER OF WHAT A NODE BOOTS ON (ADR-0057): it moves the boot row only to the revision the engine has just applied (`Reconciler.served`, never the pointer's revision at the applied epoch, which a recreated pointer can reuse for another), repairs it on every converged tick, and holds one lock per engine from its read of the pointer to that write (`Engine.following`), because two ticks interleaved let the one that read the older epoch apply last and walk the boot row back. It keeps the fleet's revision in the store's fleet row before validating it (`Reconciler.keep`), since a refused revision is still the company, and its apply record names what the engine serves (`Reconciler.Serving`) and the epoch it last APPLIED beside the one it attempted (`Reconciler.Applied`, `config_applied_epoch`, because a refusal says the node did not reach the epoch, never which one it did), so a node that refuses the fleet's revision keeps running the one before, and says so in the fleet view. It moves the boot row on EVERY tick — converged, out of retries and before a retry alike, because a node that refuses the fleet's next revision never converges again and the tick out of retries is the only one left to repair a write that failed — and the record carries the boot row as the reconciler last READ or WROTE it (`Reconciler.Boots`, `config_boot_revision_id`) rather than what the node serves, because the write is local and best effort and the fleet must say which revision the node would actually come back on. That name is never unknown: `NewReconciler` reads the row before it returns and fails when it cannot, as the boot fails on a store it cannot read, and a read that fails on a later tick leaves the last answer standing — published as none, an unread row was a node that boots on nothing to the reader keeping what it needs to start. It moves only on a read or a write that succeeded, so the record never names a boot revision ahead of the store whatever the order; the boot row moves BEFORE the record only so that the record says a move on the tick that made it. A node whose own database is scratch reports none, because the row it writes is deleted by the start that would read it — learned from the store handle (`store.DB.Scratch`), never from an option a caller could leave at its zero value. `mcp.go` is where `mcp_servers:` becomes running children: shared servers on the epoch, per-role children on a seat's LEASE, each claimed seat with its own bridge and its own registry, cut from the epoch's to the shared servers the seat is granted (`grantedSurface`, the one cut every surface a turn runs against comes through) — two children of one template publish the same tool names, so anything shared between seats hands one seat's identity to another. `seatservers.go` keeps those per-role children CONVERGED while the seat is held, through ONE RUNNER PER SEAT (the claim, an apply and a retry each ask it to go again rather than race it): every apply hands the seat's runner a reconcile against the new epoch and secret snapshot (a moved spec restarts, a missing child starts, a failed start retries from fifteen seconds doubling to five minutes, and a seat the published company no longer declares has every child retired by that pass rather than at its release, since placement may hold it a whole sweep interval longer) WITHOUT the apply waiting on it, and no pass starts a child unless `seat.Host.Provable` says the lease is still this node's — a child started once at the claim never saw a credential provisioning sealed a moment later, and its agent heard messages it could never answer. `toolcredentials.go` is the rule ANY tool server is STARTED by at all, in ONE function every start passes (`resolveServer`, the only source of a spec's values and of a box's): every `${VAR}` the server is started with — its own `env` or `headers`, its `url`, and a seat's own `mcp_env` block for it, merged over them key by key BEFORE anything resolves and by the key the server READS, so an http header is one header in any case — must resolve first, because a vendor's server handed an empty token refuses to start or acts as nobody — one a provisioning pass mints for the seat, read off that pass's seat plan (`seatMinter`), which is the plan its own run mints by and the only one this package reads (a walk holds it), WAITS unstarted and not failed until the seal reaches the node; one that pass's last report says it cannot mint until a person acts FAILS with that finding, judged again on every pass of the node's status record since a seat's pass never sees an integration change; one nothing mints FAILS naming the variable and its remedies — the integration's provision command only for a seat's own credential, and the `optional_keys` declaration only for a server's own setting, never for a seat's identity or a variable the url needs; none is retried on a timer, because every change to the snapshot runs the seat's pass and an apply's reconcile of the shared servers — a shared server held back is never handed to its bridge, whose supervision would retry it — and a running server whose credential stops resolving is stopped; and the same rule withholds the server from a harness's box, and holds an executor's OWN environment and setup files (`runEnvironment`, `sandbox.go`, each declaring its own `optional_keys`): an executor's run is the turn itself, which a refusal would fail, so it starts without what is missing and says so (`sandbox_env_unresolved`). Every turn's tools are served from ONE tool-session host per process (`toolsession.NewHost`, made at boot and closed at shutdown), refused at boot naming `TMPDIR` when its socket paths would not fit. `bridge.go` and `agentrun.go` are where a run reaches the seat's tools: the launch opens the run's bridge session under its JOB and the ROUND the job works in (`LaunchRequest.Opened`, `sandbox.PendingRun.NamedJob`, as a recovery's reopen names it too), so a call still running when a relaunch carries the round on is listed in the round's log marked late rather than dropped, and each append carries the bridge's word on whether anybody still waited for the call (`sandbox.BridgeCall.Abandoned`), fenced on the seat for every call, and records the NODE that launched it on the run's row; the session's ledger (`bridgeLedger`) WRITES EVERY CALL'S ENTRY ON THE RUN'S LOG BEFORE THE CALL RUNS (`Begin`, `sandbox.AppendBegin`): its identity, and who runs it (`runner`, the incarnation this process holds its taker lease under, `Engine.callRunner`) and until when, beside the id the call's harness gave it (`sandbox.BridgeCall.CallID`), which the update carries too and nothing finds the call by, read under the key the job names (`sandbox.OpenedJob.CallIDMeta`) that `Engine.openBridge` hands the session, the launch's from the runner it starts the job on and a run taken over from this node's runner of its harness (`reopenAgentRun`), within `beginBudget` (five seconds, its retries included), the call run only once the log took it — an entry not taken is a call not run, named at warning (`bridge_call_not_recorded`) and refused to its box, so no call runs off the record and a resumed segment counts every call the run made; then the call's ONE UPDATE as it returns (`Record`), tried four times, the last once a stream leader's re-election has passed (`appendRetryWaits`) — an attempt that put the call in the log settles it whatever became of its spend, the attempts after it trying only the record's write of that, a job replaced by a relaunch of either kind answered the one way — the spend's error of an attempt that landed, and none for a late call's first update, which the store answers `sandbox.ErrJobReplaced` (`appendRetrying`) — and an update no attempt landed leaves the entry, the call's identity on the record, and is answered owed (`mcpbridge.ErrUnrecorded`, said at debug as `bridge_call_update_refused`), which the call's own goroutine on the bridge asks for again until it lands or the call's bound passes: no other write carries anything of another call's; a call whose entry was written and that was then not run is withdrawn (`Withdraw`); a call nobody waited for whose update found the run's log gone is named at warning (`bridge_abandoned_call_after_its_job`), the session that made it holding no outcome of it either; each line names its call by its tool, its place and the opening of its digest (`namedCall`); a run a recovery finds still running has its session re-opened from the run's own row (`reopen.go`), its surface rebuilt from the parked turn and the calls already logged, so its box's token resolves on the node that holds the seat after a restart or a move; every node's bridge joins the fleet's relay (`bridgefleet.go`) — an ingress node with `api.port` 0 excepted, which serves nothing a box could dial — and is told when a seat is taken (after its runs are recovered) and when it is handed on. A direct box dials its own node's listener on loopback wherever that node runs seats and binds a listener loopback reaches (`Options.BoxHostURL`, which the bridge and the telemetry receiver are both built from, the receiver only where the node forwards to an upstream) and every other box `CREWLET_MCP_BRIDGE_URL`, the fleet's address, and every cell a launch can reach that its bridge cannot is said once at apply (`executor_bridge_unreachable`, `advisories.go`). `conversation.go` READS A TURN'S CONVERSATION ONCE, AT ITS DISPATCH (`readConversation`: `ExecutorReads.Head` at `linearizable`, fail-open as `executor_conversation_unread`, and none for a turn with no conversation key or of a company that keeps no session): how its earlier turns ended, rendered under the turn's token by `prompts.BuildConversationEndings` and handed the runner as `RunnerInput.Endings`, which every park freezes, and the base its first launch continues and freezes (`agentLauncher.conversation`, which the probe answers with no read: `conversationRead.basis`), told the run as a session its turn went on past, and when it was kept, where the head's end said so, and apart as a lost turn's where it was lost (`baseBasis`); a dispatched turn of a conversation that ends in its own frame without parking or an error, and one whose first launch was abandoned once its record was written, records its end from `runTurn`. A SCHEDULE'S FIRE IS A TURN OF ITS SCHEDULE'S CONVERSATION — the scheduler stamps it, so its dispatch reads the head the last fire left and the endings are told in the schedule's words (`notify.Scheduled`) — and is NEVER OFFERED TO A PARKED RUN AS A REPLY: `Dispatcher.answered` refuses any delivery whose conversation `notify.Derived` says nobody can reply into before it asks the coordinator or spends the delivery's headroom, since every fire carries the conversation the last fire's run parked under. A first launch refused below its own floor (its brief beside the static floor, or a continued session's context beside its brief) parks the seat's mail ON THAT FLOOR (`Dispatcher.Budget` is handed the refusal and the events of the turn that met it, `floorOf`), and a colleague's ask waiting behind ANY PARK is owed while the park holds, ASKED OF THE NODE HOLDING THE SEAT (`parkheld.go`: `colleagueOwed.heldByPark`, `heldParks` over `topics.HeldPark`), since a park lives in its holder's process and the idle sweep runs wherever the maintenance duty is; the sweep's own recount of the shared counters (`budgetParked`) answers only for the static floor. `sandbox.go` parks a turn's state HELD TO ITS SHARE OF THE RUN'S ROW (`persistSuspension` through `execstate.EncodeWithin` at `sandbox.ExecuteStateBytes`: the closed rounds condensed, `sandbox_suspension_condensed`, and a state whose own context will not fit refused as `sandbox_suspension_too_large` rather than by the store) and resumes a parked turn (`parkedTurnRunner`, which the reopen builds its runner with too) — with every relaunch CONTINUING THE SESSION OF THE JOB IT FOLLOWS in whatever box it gets where a runner of the seat's chain restores it (`executorrestore.go`: `agentLauncher.ProbeSession`, asking `Company.chainRestores`, every member's runner, since the launch picks its member once, as it launches, and a plan can be spent or come back between, and answering the kept capture's save point (`SessionCapture.SavePoint`), which the runner reads each call's place in the order the log took it against (`BridgedCall.Appended`, carried by `bridgedCalls` with whether the log took the call late, `ledger.Call.Late`, and whether nobody waited for it, `BridgedCall.Abandoned`, neither of which a save point holds, and with the held call an approved one was made for, `BridgedCall.HeldID`, which tells the call an answer's lead tells from one an earlier answer approved, and with the id the call's harness gave it, `BridgedCall.CallID`, the box's word and no part of the call's identity, carried by `unfinishedCalls` for a call still in flight too and read only to ask a reading of which calls a cut-off kept session holds, and by `runnerResume` with how many late calls the log dropped, `Resume.LateElided`, and how many calls the resume's read saw, `Resume.BridgedRead`, which every relaunch hands back to its launch, its opening marking late what the log took after it, and with the reads the job's brief counted without listing them, `Resume.Owed` off the record's `OmittedCalls`, the probe reading a capture's as `SessionBasis.Owed`, and each brief's own set carried to the launch, `sandboxContinuation` and `sandboxSession` copying `Told` and `Fresh`, and `sandboxSession` beside a kept capture's two briefs the two told by a reading of what it holds, `runner.SessionContinuation.Exact`, with the release they were read for, which the sandbox layer hands only to a box of that release, never beside a base; what a CUT-OFF KEPT SESSION HOLDS read for its relaunch by `agentLauncher.SessionHoldings`, the runner's `HoldingsAsker`, over `sandbox.Coordinator.HoldingsOf` through the runner of the member driving the harness that wrote the session and no other, `chainRestorers.reader`, for the release that wrote it, `SessionBasis.Release` off the row's `HarnessVersion`, never an error to the runner and logged whatever it answers: `executor_session_holdings_read` with the asked calls it held and told, the distinct unreported subagents a told call is under, the release and what follows (no brief told by a reading that holds and moves nothing, `readOutcome`), beside one `executor_session_subagent_unreported` per such subagent, never per call, or `executor_session_holdings_unread` with why, a reading not read whole among it); and `executorRestore` over the router's `ExecutorReads` and the session store), the member the launch picks runs the harness that wrote it, and the seat's budget can carry it, which the launch decides once the briefs that continue it exist (`agentLauncher.continuedFloor`, its floor reckoned for the member picked and no other — the job before's own session in its box counted only where that member continues in that box, `continuesInItsBox`, by the launch's own tests of it — EACH SESSION THE LAUNCH MAY CONTINUE HELD TO ITS OWN FLOOR and dropped alone where that one cannot be carried, the launch held to the largest floor of those it still offers and of its fresh brief, since its box takes up either or starts its run on that brief, and no session refused for its size alone, a kept capture's briefs told by a reading counted in its floor, `keptFloor`, and dropped alone, on a copy of the session with `executor_session_holdings_unused`, where they are what the launch cannot carry and its own briefs are not); what a reader of a seat's sessions is told a launch would be launched on — a relaunch of a live turn, or a conversation's next turn — is THE NODE HOLDING THE SEAT's pick (`relaunchtarget.go`: `Engine.RelaunchTargets`, one per session asked about, by its turn, or by none for a conversation's next turn, this node's own `Company.relaunchTarget` — the first runner member not sitting out on its own bench, its cell, `memberCell`, the node, and whether the company keeps sessions — off the chain of the revision that launch runs on, `relaunchCompany`: a resumed segment running here relaunches on the revision it was admitted under, `resumedSegments`, whatever an apply put live since, and a next turn, asked by no turn, is admitted under the live one — where it holds the seat, and otherwise asked of the incarnation the seat's lease names over `topics.HeldRelaunch`, with that member's runner's answer for each turn's session; an unreadable lease, an unheld seat and a silent holder are each a target that cannot be said, never a no), telling the runner how the launch decided before the run starts (`sessionDecision`, whether the brief handed is the one told by a reading among it), and a person's answer otherwise CARRIED ON in the run's own session where its box was kept (`runner.Asked`), and a resume the budget refuses HELD on its window (`budgetHold`, `sandbox.HeldError`) rather than abandoned, on the floor THE LAUNCH WAS REFUSED BELOW: its fresh brief BESIDE the static floor, which counts only the request's fixed part (held where that is past the most room a capped window can leave the launch, each ceiling less what the launch's own turn charged there, read off the meter the turn charges through, `meter.chargedIn` and `budgetBasis.room`, since the turn spends before its launch reads the window and config holds every ceiling at or above the static floor alone: at the static floor where that fits in the room, its first request then free to take the window past its ceiling by up to the room less the static floor more than a launch held at the room, `sandbox_spend_over_budget` saying what it took, since held at the room it would need a window holding nothing but its own turn's spend, which a busy company's other seats may never leave it; and at the room where the turn's own spend left less; `launchFloor.fresh`, logged as `executor_floor_held`; and held so after a turnover its launch waited, whatever its brief: a launch refused below a floor above the static floor waits ONE turnover at most, since a busy company's other seats spend in the window first after every turnover and a floor within the room can be past what every window leaves the launch, its next try in a later window holding that fresh floor and dropping a session the window still leaves no room for (`launchFloor.after`, `launchFloor.stillShort`), the window riding the seat's park on this node (`floorWaits`, kept under the ids of the events of the delivery whose launch it refused, read by that delivery's tries and no other's, so a person's message the park's release hands the seat first from its priority lane never takes it, and dropped once the delivery will not come back, `Dispatcher.Gone`) or the hold's refusal onto the job's record (`sandbox.Refusal.Window`, `sandbox.LaunchRecord.RelaunchRefusedIn`)), raised by a session the launch may continue, the turn's kept capture or the job's own copy in its box, to its context with the brief that continues it, or to that brief beside the static floor where no capture measured that context (`launchfloor.go`: `launchFloor`, read by `executorHeadroom`, `meter.starved`, `budgetHold` and `starvedReason`, carried from the refusal to the hold by `floorRefusal`; a session past that room is not continued, and a continued relaunch is held, never parked); a completion's turn gate asks NO budget and NO limit its models know (`seatCannotRun`), so a run that finishes on a full window, or on a plan another seat's run just spent, is collected and charged in its window, its box paused, before its review is held. A resume that meets its models' limit PARKS its seat as a dispatch does (`parkResumeOnLimit`: `seat_provider_parked`, the chain's probe), since a plan is usually spent inside a run whose seat no delivery reaches, and so does a collected completion whose review's chain already knows the limit (`collectedRefusal`, before its turn runs), which is also what parks a seat whose mail its run holds again after an apply carries its entry's sit-out; and a seat parked on its provider here admits NO resume until the park is released (`providerParkRefusal`, at the turn gate, naming the probe's next call only while it is still to come), whichever chain the resume's first call takes: past its own hold's instant, a completion whose review had sent it back to spent runners was otherwise reviewed and relaunched into the vendor's window every hour. A PARK OUTLIVES NO SEAT: a seat's release waits for no turn that can park it, so a park taken while the seat was held asks for the seat once it is taken and undoes itself where the release took it meanwhile (`budgetPark`, `providerPark` through `unparkReleased`, which leaves the probe and says the seat released even where the forget got to its record first), and one asked after the release's detach is refused by the queue (`seatParks.forget`): left, its probe published a seat parked that a peer ran, and its hold kept the inbox shut on this node with no park to lift it. A park's NOTICES go through `parkNotices` (`parknotice.go`): once per change, the newest per seat, published by the caller while the broker answers, and held and retried on a one-to-thirty-second backoff while it refuses, said once when the outage starts and once when they land, where each renewal of each seat was once published on the spot and logged as it failed; and a probe refused with a next try already past waits `harness.ShortestSitOut` rather than asking again at once. A RUN GIVEN REPOSITORIES (`sandboxrepos.go`, ADR-0093) has them resolved after the launch's own refusals and BEFORE its holds are sealed — a resolution takes a scratch repository's hold, which waits on the seal — through the code tools' one resolution, refused as the call's result where this node does not serve Gitlet or has no `CREWLET_BOX_GIT_URL` (`BoxURLs.Git`, read at boot with the box's other two addresses; `sandbox_git_url_unset`), and each launch's git environment (`boxGitEnv`, boxgit.go) is made once the store names the job: that launch's run token and the seat's commit identity by `codeSigner`'s one rule, over the run environment, so the engine's names win. What such a run PUSHED is read back when it is collected (`runpushes.go`, the coordinator's `sandbox.RunPushes`): its launch's push rows on the repositories it was given to push to, the first page LINEARIZABLE — the collection is where "it pushed nothing" must never be a stale answer — and every later page at session at the first page's position, collapsed per ref by the run's first push of it and held to `sandbox.MaxPushedRefs` refs, the rest counted, so what a box pushes in a loop costs the collecting node no more than a record can carry; the router's code seam is asked at each collection, since a node serves Gitlet with its revision and the coordinator outlives every revision. The same collection names each pushed branch by the live change it heads and opens the changes a run asked for (`runchanges.go`: one `branch_changes` read of every repository's branches at `session` at the push record's position, `sandbox.PushRecord.At`, so a collection pays the record's one barrier and no second; the open a STEP of the launch's own derived operation, `openOpID`, so a retried collection on any node opens nothing twice; every ask at once, one goroutine a repository, so a collection waits for its slowest open; as the run's seat, on the run's work item, titled by the delivered commit's subject, else its branch's name, else its id, through `gitlet.DeliveredTitle`, the push's own rule), and a box's admission stamps its pushes with the run's work item's id and project (`boxRuns`, `RunProvenance.WorkID`, `.WorkProject`) so a `git push -o change` of the box carries the item too, in the shape the collection's open gives it; the collection's open is the seat's write made for whom the launching turn works for, as its checkout composed it and the row keeps it (the row's `CodeWorkingFor`, `runCheckouts` granting it through `resolveRunRepos`, a round carried on keeping it with its grants, `carriedGrants`), held to the colleague whose ask woke that turn or, for a credential acting as no seat, to the ceiling of nobody, the seat's own work alone (`openCeiling`, the row's `CodeCeiling`), as the box's pushes are, each of which `boxRuns` reads with the run (`BoxRun.WorkingFor`); an open that ran nowhere — the estate's walk found no committer to run it and none gone silent, its own claim that nothing ran — is reported so definitely, naming why (`ranNowhere`, read as a code tool reads the same answers, builtin's `failedWrite`): no node could read it, one at least refusing it unread (mid-roll, a committer of an older build, which does not declare whom it is made for; the refusal of keys that wraps the walk nobody served, never an answer this build could not read, which ran where it was given; `unopenedUnreadable`), or no Gitlet node could take it, every committer out of service, unable to run it or behind (`unopenedUnserved`), each not opened, nothing of it run, the commits kept, the seat's to open again with `code_open_change`; so is a committer's refusal of it as no proposal it can decide, in the refusal's own words — whom it is made for, on a chart that moved since the checkout — nothing of it run and the commits kept (`unopenedRefused`); every failure that may have run keeps "whether it opened is unknown", a walk with a node gone silent and an open sent to a committer never heard from among them, and the collection asks no open again, since it holds the run's seat until the turn resumes. A host setting passed through by name that may be unset is DECLARED, by KEY, in the server's `optional_keys`, and a key so declared whose value does not resolve is LEFT OUT whole — never passed empty, never half a value — except a variable a pass mints for the seat, which is waited for whatever the declaration says; not spelled `${VAR:-}`, which the reference grammar deliberately does not parse. `mcpsupervise.go` is what the engine decides about the bridges' own supervision: a per-role child that DIES is restarted by its bridge only while `seat.Host.Provable` holds — the predicate the passes ask — and a pass leaves it to that bridge (`mcp.Bridge.Runs` answers yes for it) rather than starting a second copy, so the runner and the supervisor never run two loops over one child; a shared server whose start failed is retried by its bridge, and what it brings back is refiled into the CURRENT epoch's registry — the one part of a published epoch that changes — and every held seat's surface. It is also where every fleet-singleton DUTY is armed, and the reason is the same: the trim, the maintenance sweep, the EMBEDDING duty and chat's PRUNE (`chatretention.go`, a record carrying a cutoff the broker's own instants are compared with, so every data node deletes one set) each need the company's provider, the log's publisher and the fleet's leases in one place. The org chart's projections are here too and are NOT duties — the tracker's projects, the knowledge containers and the unit ROOMS (`chatunits.go`) follow every published company on every node, because each create arbitrates on its own subject and the loser is told it is held. The alarm table is the exception that proves it — evaluated on EVERY node on its own HEARTBEAT (`statelog.AlarmInterval`, which IS `statelog.PositionHeartbeat`, the ten seconds every data node republishes its position on — one declaration, because a copy of the number per package agreed only until one was retuned) and never behind the trim's duty claim, because a reading describes one NODE and a table evaluated only where the lease sits would report the lease holder's health as the fleet's, and because the trim's quarter-hour silently raised every sixty-second threshold to fifteen minutes. What that evaluation finds firing rides the node's PRESENCE HEARTBEAT (`nodeStatus`, off the tracker the health envelope reads, from memory), and a node with no tracker or none evaluated yet publishes no alarms rather than an empty set, so a peer never reads it as quiet. The trim's tick is where the SCANS the table needs are taken — the vector coverage and each log's daily intake, the second withheld through a log's first two days because its first day is the one a company imports into — and so is the store pool's wait DELTA, which whoever reads it clears for everyone after, so it is taken on that regular quarter-hour alone, the cadence the `pool_wait` p95 is a percentile of; the heartbeat and every report read them back rather than repeating them; a gauge whose measurement became unknown is WITHDRAWN rather than left holding the last figure. Every field of the reading it evaluates has a PRODUCER that the reading's assembly reaches (`TestEveryAlarmReadingFieldHasAProducer`, a `sourcetree/decltype` walk from `retention.reading` and statelog's `ReportInputs.alarms`), because a field nothing fills reads as zero, which is exactly what a healthy node looks like: `code_maintenance_refused` and `prefetch_slow` were each silent on every running node that way. The code half (`codeReading`) reads the COMMITTER beside the git cache and the compactor, never inside their branch: a committer runs on every data node of a company hosting code, the gitlet role or not, so `code_commit_busy` is filled wherever one decides pushes — its five fields assigned in `codeReading` itself from the concrete committer's `BusyReading`, since the producers gate follows no call whose result is the committer's own shape, and the repository named by this node's own rows (`gitlet.Store.NameOf`), else by its id; and the CODE READER on every node that holds the code log's rows, whatever else it runs, for `code_checks_backlog` (`gitlet.Reader.ChecksBacklog`): the queued attempts past their OWN timeouts are measured from the rows by every data node, never by a runner, so a fleet whose every Gitlet node is gone still raises it. And the HOME'S QUEUE WORKER on a Gitlet node for `code_merge_rate_over_capacity` (`gitlethome.Loop.AlarmReading`, read after the compactor): only the home builds a queue's trains, so only it times their rounds, and a case reaches it through `WithHomeSeams`. And the TRIM'S TICK for `code_refs_near_limit` (`refsnear.go`): `retention.evaluate` takes `gitlet.Reader.RefsNearLimit` through the `retention.refs` field on every data node of a company hosting code, CACHED under `retention.mu` until the next tick and copied by `retention.reading` with the bound and the window it was judged at (`gitlet.MaxRefsPerRepo`, `gitlet.RefExpiry`) — the vector coverage's idiom, because the reading walks the repositories and counts each near one's refs while a report is assembled on every beat, request and poll — never in `Engine.codeReading`, the cache's half, which returns on a node with no cache; a failed read clears the figure and logs `code_refs_near_limit_unread` once a tick. The reading of this node's own disk (`retentioncapacity.go`) names the copy holding the largest write-ahead log and counts, per volume, the log each file's last copy measured a copy holding back, so `wal_large` can say a copy's end folds the log back in and `volume_low` keeps room for it beside the second copy. Whether the trim floor can be read is observed on the heartbeat itself (`floorwatch.go`), through the read path's own two steps — one listing, then `floorFor` per domain — so `floor_unknown` lists exactly the floors every read refuses on, one log each in its `domains`. How far this node's clock, and the clock of the broker member leading the position register, stand from the fleet's is measured on the position heartbeat too (`clockskew.go`), off the register it has just read — every data node's row, stamped by its node and stored at the member's instant, read only where the member stored it within four heartbeats of this node's own, and the fleet's clock their median — so `clock_skew` names the clock that moved and costs no read or write of its own, and a measure stands until two beats in a row could not renew it or the heartbeat stops — counted in beats by the heartbeat, never as an age the reading judges, since an age counted from a beat's start lapsed between two beats whenever a beat and the next listing outlasted it, clearing a standing alarm for one evaluation and raising it again at the next. A binding dangles by ONE rule, `bindings.go`, which is the request path's own seat table applied to a directory row, and `/iam/check` is the one place it is reported, with the repair its holder's KIND takes — a person is moved to another human seat or removed, since the directory refuses to unbind one, and a service account unbound or moved — never an alarm, because it is a fact about the company's content that every node would raise at once and none could repair (ADR-0015). The IDENTITY estate's duty is armed here too (`identityduties.go`) — the per-bucket sweep publisher — on its own lease of three of its hourly intervals, inside `coord.MaxDutyTTL`. It is armed only where the `workers` role runs, although every data node runs the identity domain — elsewhere every claim would be refused, so arming it would be a loop that never runs reported as a duty that does — and `/health` serves what a node armed as `identity_duty_seconds`, because a duty never armed looks exactly like one finding nothing. And the COMPANY KEY a blind is derived under (`companykeys.go`) is MINTED here, on first use, with a CREATE the store refuses over a key that landed first, and every minter reads back whichever did: the store is last-write-wins for a put, and two nodes that each found a key absent each read their OWN write back and derived under different keys. The fleet hold and the in-process gate stay only as the courtesy that keeps two nodes from each running the guard — a lease cannot fence a holder paused past it, so a put under the hold split the company exactly as it did with none. A mint takes a REQUIRED guard, and the identity key's refuses both an estate holding a blind (minting over a DELETED key orphans every address) and rows that have not APPLIED the whole identity log — behind it, or holding a record they retained, which moves the checkpoint without writing the rows — whose "no blind here" may be rows that were never applied; the log's END is read first (`IdentityLogEnd`) and the rows prove themselves against it in their own snapshot. A NODE THAT STOPS VOUCHING FOR ITS IDENTITY ROWS SAYS SO (`identityvouch.go`): every identity read compares the identity log's lag against `statelog.StallGrace`, and an applier halted on a record it cannot read, or frozen behind a broker, commits no batch for any listener to hear — so a watch over that same lag hands the credential listener a move naming everyone the instant it crosses the grace, once per crossing, which is the instant the request path starts answering 503 — and that move only closes the node's sockets, whose reconnects the handshake decides. The party registry has TWO rebuild triggers — a published company, and the identity applier's `Moved.Seats` (`directory.go`), with a thirty-second re-read behind it, which is the only trigger beside a published company on a node without `data`, where who holds each seat is read through the estate router (`SeatHolders`) — serialised on one lock, each building a WHOLE registry for its company and swapping it, never a diff; a directory it cannot read carries the last reading forward, and a node that has NEVER read one withholds every human seat until it does, because the reading it would otherwise carry is the chart-only one. WHOSE CREDENTIALS a committed identity batch moved (`iamdomain.Moved`, `identitymoved.go`) goes to whatever `SetOnIdentityMoved` registered — the API, which decides again the dashboard sockets a move names and closes every socket on a move naming nobody, since every data node applies the identity log and so closes its own, and every node serving the API is one — and an adoption or a reopened estate, which put a donor's rows in place with no committed batch to name a revocation the donor applied, say `Everyone` (`stateLog.estateReplaced`). The appliers' post-commit hooks are one `applyHooks` value built by `Engine.applyHooks`, so the register's own cases build appliers from what a running node hands them — a nil hook compiles and passes every case in the domain's own package. WHOSE INBOX a committed batch moved (`inbox.Movement`, `inbox.go`: the domain it came from, `Source`, and the instant it was stored, `At`) goes from the appliers of the domains the register marks `Inbox` — Worklet's and the code log's — the mark is the wiring, since `stateLog.inboxFor` hands the listener to those alone — to whatever `SetOnInboxMoved` registered, the API's `inbox_changed` push; while this node's copy is out of service, the movements come from a sound peer's appliers through the ONE LIVE RELAY below, and every switch is a re-read naming the source (`SetOnInboxGap`). The FOUR hooks that announce to sockets — the inbox movements of the domains marked `Inbox`, chat's committed batches (`SetChatLive`, the hub the process attaches after `New`), and what the artifacts' and the datastore's commits moved (`OnArtifactsMoved`, `OnArtifactDataMoved`, which the artifact origin's verdict cache hears too) — go through ONE LIVE RELAY (`liverelay.go`): a node whose copy is out of service hears a sound peer's appliers instead of its own, every domain switched at once because the verdict is the whole copy's, one ring bounded by its encoded bytes rather than its batches, and every switch a re-read in every domain (to the artifact listeners, their domains' own reset), and every ask and answer SIGNED on a `signedask` channel of its own (`live.relay`, built once in `New`, the relay refused rather than served unsigned): it only reads, but a follower pushes what it is answered to every socket it holds as a peer's appliers' work — chat excerpts and mentions, datastore documents whole — so an unsigned answer was anybody on the broker showing words in a room under anybody's name, and an answer the follower cannot believe is passed over while it waits for its server's own, since the transport names no sender and a forged reply that ended the ask would move every follower off every server. The artifact copies' own follower of that verdict, which withheld this node's artifact pushes and relayed nothing, is gone: a second reader of one fact on its own clock left an out-of-service node's artifact rooms hearing nobody. Every fleet decision that destroys, by the company document, what no undo restores is made only on a node that has applied the revision the fleet's activation pointer names (`maintenance.go`, `fleetCompany`): the mailbox sweep's roster judges a seat ABSENT there and nowhere else, so a node behind on an apply never stamps a seat the fleet has just added, and the clearing of a removed seat's pause, the forgetting of a removed human seat's chat read state, chat's PRUNE horizon and the assistant's retention term are read the same way, so none of them deletes by a revision the fleet has replaced. THE ORG IS THE APPLIED EPOCH'S: `roles:` and `units:` are part of every revision, built into the running `org.Organization` once per apply, and ONE list (`converge.go`, `followCompany`) brings everything derived from a company up to the published one — the party registry, the seats' vendor identities and tool surfaces, the tracker's projects, the knowledge containers and the unit rooms (stamped with the revision's activation instant), the mailboxes, the scheduler and the socket push — run by boot and by every apply, so a step added to one path is a step both get. Every HOLD on a seat's mailbox — a person's pause, a budget park waiting out a spent window — is keyed on the seat's ID (`seatpause.go`, `budgetpark.go`), because the inbox a hold sits on is named by the id. The A2A ANSWER LEG is the engine's (`a2a.go`): a colleague's ask is answered by whatever ends the turn that took it up, off the trigger or off the run's record, a lost run's included (`answerLostTurn`, the coordinator's `TurnLost`); and so is the idle sweep's question whether a quiet channel is still OWED (`a2aowed.go`, `colleagueOwed`): its ask on a live run's record, or waiting in its seat's inbox held by a run, a person's pause or a budget park, the same four readings the seat's own holds are made from. A pause with `stop_running` also ENDS the seat's running executor, on the node that holds the seat — when the pause lands, on every complete read of the pauses and when a stopped seat is acquired (`runstop.go`), whose recovery re-opens no session for the run it then ends (`reopen.go`), nor for one whose box went with a node the fleet found gone — and closes the turn parked on it as stopped under identities derived from the run's ending (`closeStoppedTurn`), while a launch the stop beats to its park closes its own turn as stopped, and a resumed turn whose call comes back refused for a limit or the budget as the stop lands is settled as stopped rather than reverted to wait and parked (`resumeWaits`, `sandbox.go`); a turn's park record is published BEFORE its park is written, so anything that ends the parked turn is newer than the park. A person's STOP is read ONE way (`stoppedBy`) for both of a detached executor's write edges — Gitlet's box admission (`codeBoxRuns`) and the session a launch opens on the tool bridge (the one `agentLauncher.launch` and `Engine.reopenAgentRun` open, fenced by `Engine.seatFence`) — so its box's pushes and its bridged writes are refused, and let through again, together. And it is where every FLEET-WIDE consumer group — one any node may attach, so a message goes to whichever member takes it first — declares whether its handler needs data (`groups.go`): `joins` is the one door every attachment passes, a node without `data` joins none that do, and a gate holds the table against the tree's subscriptions in both directions, because a stateless member of a group that reads the estate answers its share of the messages from nothing and nothing fails. And it is where the INSTRUCTION CHANNELS the engine serves beside the estate are signed (`fleetbroker.go`, `steer.go`, `sandboxhost.go`, `assistantfleet.go`, `codesearch.go`, `mcpcheck.go`) — a broker member removing a voter through its own system account, the node running a turn taking a person's note as that person, a box's host reading a run's box back, and pausing it, for the node collecting the run, the node running an Ask Crewlet turn replaying it or stopping it for the person a request names, a Gitlet node searching its repositories as the asker a request names, and the nodes an MCP server runs on testing it as the company — each a `signedask` channel built ONCE from the fleet keyring on every node (in `New`, the assistant's in `AssistantFleet`) and refused rather than served unsigned, because each carries out what it is asked on the asking node's decision, and the broker's admission vouches for a connection, never a request: a member runs only what it admits, a key it does not hold is "did not run" so the next member carries the removal, and the carrying member's log names the author, kind and credential read off the signed request; `TestEveryInstructionChannelSignsInDomainsOfItsOwn` holds every channel's key domains apart from each other's and every state log's, and `TestEverySignedChannelTheTreeBuildsIsClaimed` holds every call in the module's non-test source that names a key domain to a domain that test claims, reading the name with its types and out through the constructors it is handed to, so a channel built without a claim fails rather than signing in domains nothing compares. And `gitlethost.go` is where a node with the `gitlet` role builds Gitlet for a company on Gitlet, with the runtime: the cache's directory prepared before git's HOME exists, its budget derived from the ROOM the cache can use — the volume's free space PLUS what the cache already holds (free space excludes the cache's own files, so a budget from it alone shrank at every warm restart), LESS what an embedded broker on the same volume may still store under its limit (`jetstream.Queue.VolumeClaim`: the broker's limit is a share of the same free space, so a budget beside it claimed the same bytes twice) and the host's spool allowance — half of a shared volume's room, seven eighths of one of its own, never more and with no floor, and a room of nothing REFUSES Gitlet by name, since any budget there is bytes the volume does not have — and IN A TEST BINARY Gitlet built with `gitlet.cache_max_bytes` or `gitlet.max_transfers` unset is refused before any of that (`declared.go`, `ErrUndeclaredCodeCache`), since both are then the runner's figures rather than the case's: only a case that stands in for the cache's volume (`cacheDisk`) or boots under `SizedByTheMachine`, whose subject is the derivation, is let through, and the test binary is told apart by the testing package's flags (`internal/testbinary`) because linking that package into the binary is refused; "the same volume" is one pool of space (device id, filesystem id, or the space left read at one instant to within the coarser block, so a btrfs subvolume or a ZFS dataset of the store's pool is shared whatever its recordsize) — the git pool, the cache, the GIT READS (`internal/gitlet/query`, over the same cache and pool under one seat git-work share of the pool's read lane, the chart and the posture read per read) and the host over the engine's own router, chart and audit — handed THAT SAME SHARE, never a second, so the builds of its ENGINE WRITES (ADR-0092) and the git reads hold at most half the lane between them, judged by the same chart and gated by the same posture (`Roster: e.codeRoster`, `Current: e.admits`, read per write), and signed by `codeSigner` (`codesign.go`): from the CURRENT epoch, read ONCE a signature so a seat's role and the identity domain come off one epoch, the author the acting principal's seat — an agent's own, or the human seat a person's credential is bound to — by its role's name and its role's email AS WRITTEN, never `org.Role.ResolvedEmail`, which reads THIS node's environment and would let a failover on another Gitlet node build another commit of one write and write an address a company kept out of its document into history, a name or an address git would not write as given, an address that is no `<local>@<domain>` of at most 254 bytes and a `${VAR}` reference falling back to the handle and `<handle>@<code.identity_domain>`; the committer `Crewlet <engine@<code.identity_domain>>`, spelled once (`engineCommitter`) for what `Sign` writes and for `Committer`, the address a coding box's pack may hold no commit of that the repository does not already hold (`forged_committer`, read off the same epoch); a node with no epoch or no such seat signs nothing (`gitlet.ErrNotServing`, so the walk asks another Gitlet node) — and the COMPACTOR (`gitlet/compact`), built there unstarted and STARTED with the native half's duties (`startNativeDuties`) — past the runtime's publication, which its first pass reads this node's git half and copy through (started at the build, that pass found the node home to nothing), and behind the mode's publisher gate, since a compaction stores objects and appends its record; read per request through `GitletHost`, never captured, and stopped ONCE within one bound in the teardown BEFORE the committer, since its syncs append barriers and its pushes propose — the home's change work first, then the compactor, then the git reads, cut and waited for, then the host and its cache. The HOME'S CHANGE WORK (`gitlet/home`) is built beside the compactor and started and stopped with it, its nudges a watch on this node's own code log, each sequence a position in the generation this node's applier last committed in (`codeNudges`). The COMMITTER judges a seat's push from the same current epoch a seat's read is judged by (`codeSeatRules`: the epoch's roster and the company's clock, the quota's day, off ONE epoch, so a revision moving a seat and the clock together is judged by both or by neither; false before the first epoch is installed, on which the committer hands a seat's push to the repository's next committer) and behind the same posture gate (`Current: e.admits`, `Reconciler.Admits` through `SetAdmits`), so a node confirmed behind the company's configuration decides no seat's push. Its sibling the CHANGE WRITER (`gitlet.ChangeWriter`, a change's conversation on the change's own subject) is built beside it on the same terms — the same `codeSeatRules`, posture gate and retention — handed out with it through the write set, so a maintenance mode serves neither, and stopped right after it in the same bound before the estate subject stops answering. The host and the git reads RECORD every read of a `visibility: unit` repository they serve through `recordCodeRead`, which FILES it in the node's reads ledger (`codeReads`, `gitlet/readaudit`) and returns — it used to publish there, so a fetch's response, a read's git-lane slot and every read on the page it cut waited for the broker's replicated, fsynced acknowledgement, up to the client's five seconds while the event stream had no leader — and the ledger publishes each reader's reads of a flush as one `code_fetched` event of this node's own log, which the usage publisher derives into the day's `repo_read` record; the two ledgers (`auxSpend` and `codeReads`) take their last flush at once on one five-second budget (`stopLedgers`), after Gitlet stops and before the usage publisher's own — NEVER through the operator audit (`auditGit`), whose envelope source is the runtime audit's and whose types the dashboard's audit log reads as people's calls, when a read is neither a call nor necessarily a person's; and `CodeReaderDeps` hands `code_repo_readers` the usage domain's readers through the router, on `CodeToolDeps`' terms. `CodeToolDeps` wires Gitlet's writes and the turn's hold seam over the very router seam its reads take (`Writer`, `Holder`), so a node that serves a seat's code reads serves its writes and keeps its holds on the same terms — and, where the company runs Worklet, Worklet's read of one task (`Tasks`, the work tools' own reader), which resolves a Worklet item a change write names by its key. The router's GIT PLACEMENT is every live node whose presence profile has the gitlet role, off the same watched view as the data placement (`wholeGit`, `gitletNodesOf`), and this node's git half is `engineGit`, which loads the published runtime on EVERY call and answers its `gitHalf` — the query server's reads by object id, Gitlet's engine writes and the home's merge previews (`gitlethome.Previews`), built together over one cache, one pool and one share and stopped together — handed to the router and, where the profile has the gitlet role, to `serveEstate`, so the git lanes registered at boot answer `no_backend` until an apply builds Gitlet and serve it after with no restart: captured at either's construction, a Gitlet node that applied its company after boot answered "not serving Gitlet" to every read and write until it restarted. The router's GIT BUDGETS are what each Gitlet node's presence row says its cache gives its background work, read with the Gitlet nodes themselves off ONE listing of the same view (`wholeGit.Listing` over `presenceRoster.GitListing`, every lease decoded once and by `gitletNodeOf`, the rule `LiveGitletNodes` lists by; `coord.GitBackgroundKey` beside the status: a row without it a node NOT SAYING, never one with nothing to give), and a Gitlet node advertises its own on every beat — handed to the seat host as a hook beside the status (`node.Config.GitBackground`, `Engine.gitBackground`, nil on a node without the gitlet role) rather than as a status field, because a beat whose status read overran drops the status whole — from the PUBLISHED runtime's cache (`gitBackgroundBudget`, zero where it holds none), so a repository's maintainer is weighed by the figure its admissions hold the work to. (the live relay's, the presence seal's, the box bridge's relay's, the assistant's and the seals an executor's record carries among them). A note to a turn whose executor is out is NOT a scatter (`runsteer.go`, ADR-0067): it waits SEALED on the run's record in the coordination store (`seat.steer.note`, beside a sealed seat anchor, `seat.steer.seat`), filed by any node with one compare-and-swap, served to the run's harness by the inbox its bridge session carries — built from the record alone, so a node that reopens the session builds the same one — read by the launch's brief and the review, carried in the order the record NUMBERED the writes that read them (`RunSteer.Reads`) rather than any node's clock, every take NAMED (`takeName`) so one whose call answered an error is SETTLED by its name — off a hook's path, its serve removed since the hook was answered nothing (`settleHook`, `settleUnanswered`: asked until the store answers, not by a clock, one loop a run, ended only by the node's teardown, after a last attempt at every take in a window of its own from the teardown's start (`steerSettleStopBudget`), beside the give-backs and NEVER ON THE STOP'S ALLOWANCE (its doc says why), which a teardown waits out (`runSteers.stopWith`, `settleOnce`, `waitSettles`), and said unsettled past `steerSettlePatience`), and on a review's path in answer mode, handing over what its write recorded, a take whose settle the store answered no more being kept by the segment and settled again at its next round or brief — a brief's launch sealing such a note with its take (`rowSource.lostTakes`, through the launcher's `agentLauncher.notes`), so its write records the note read by the job it opens in the take's place and at the take's own place among the reads, the brief telling it there too from the places the source keeps of every read it hands over (`rowSource.orders`, `steer.Briefing.Orders`, the runner's `inReadOrder`), so a later round's note correcting it is never told ahead of it — standing meanwhile for a note waiting (`rowSource.Waiting`), and marked only at the segment's end, in its path first so the run's ending reads the mark, and off it where the store answers that no more (`rowSource.Close`); a relaunch's brief reads the record's marks as they stand (`steer.Briefing.Unanswered`), a mark landing after the segment resumed among them, and the launch's write records each marked note its brief tells again read by the job it opens, told again, at the marked take's own place among the reads (`retoldNotes`, `runner.AgentRunRequest.Retold`, `sandbox.NoteRead.Retold`) — a poll answering a note only once `ServeNotes` marked it served to the job (`runInbox.Wait`), a confirmation whose take's answer was lost settled on its own path in answer mode and what it recorded reported there, a confirmation made again reporting a note an earlier one of its job read as the same record (`runInbox.confirm`, `runInbox.confirmedBefore`), a resume carrying the served notes' ids (`servedNotes`, `runner.Resume.Served`), and a LATE CALL the store files for the working job routed by kind before any seal opens, drawn in the engine's words under its session's fence token (`lateItemMessage`, `runInbox.Bind`), after a batch's notes, never at a finish — and every note no step reported reading has its end reported by the run's ending alone, as its record knows it (`CoordinatorOptions.SteersEnded`, `runSteers.ended`): delivered at the brief of a started job that told it again (`steer.Retold`); unconfirmed, at the job whose harness was handed it and never confirmed; missed, at the step whose take recorded it read and lost its answer, where no later launch whose job started told it, a telling keeping the take's read beside it for a launch that fails after its write; expired, every other note no job ran with, the brief of a launch that failed after its write included. So nothing else reports one; the desk HANDS a first segment's box over IN THE WRITE THAT CREATES THE RECORD (`steerDesk.handOver`, `LaunchRequest.HandedNotes`), holding an offer arriving meanwhile until it lands, so a note to a running turn is never answered `closed` nor filed ahead of one the box took first. ONE READING OF TIER A'S STREAM BLOCK (`streamConfig`) and ONE CONFIGURATION OF EVERY COORDINATION STORE (`coordinationStoresOf`, which `kv.OpenFleet` opens) serve the boot and `OpenFleetStore` alike — the fleet's coordination store reached by a process that is not the engine (a `crewlet` command while the engine is stopped): a solo member starts its own broker on its store directory, under that directory's lock and with no leaf listener; a node on an external broker dials it; a member of a cluster (no quorum alone, and rejoining as this node for one value is a restart's cost) and a node that holds no data (a leaf of the members') are `ErrNoFleetStoreHere`. Only the fleet store: not the queue, which would provision the engine's streams, nor the lease bucket or the object store, whose settings the engine decides. A node serving Gitlet ANSWERS THE FLEET'S CODE STATUS on `crewlet.code.status` (`codestatus.go`) with `host.Server.Status`, registered once the host starts and withdrawn first inside the host's one bounded stop, so no peer is told of a cache that is going; UNSIGNED, since a node answers it about itself and carries nothing out for the asker (internal/signedask's rule), and versioned, a question at a version this build does not speak answered by nobody; a question NAMES the Gitlet nodes that answer it (`nodes`, none named asking every one) and says whether they send their hot lists (`without_hot`), both additive at protocol 1, so a node it does not name answers nothing before it reads its status and a page of a few Gitlet nodes is answered by those few. ANY node ASKS it, for `code_status`, in two steps: `GitletNodes`, the Gitlet nodes the presence leases list live (a read of the coordination store, no node asked), and `CodeStatus`, a scatter of the nodes the tool's page names — or of every Gitlet node, naming none, for the fleet's first page and its hot list — inside the fleet read budget (`eventfan.FleetReadBudget`), lasting until every node it ASKED has answered — never a count of replies, because a node it did not ask can answer too (an older build answers whatever the question names, a draining one a question naming nobody), and such a reply is dropped — reported with the one coverage shape every fanned answer carries over the nodes asked — a Gitlet node that did not answer, or answered at a version this build does not read, is named rather than counted healthy, and a node with no broker asks its own host.
