The health tools take `HealthToolDeps` on both surfaces — the presence leases with the requester ceiling's halves beside them (`turnRequesters`, `ChartAuthorityOf`, read per call), so `fleet_health` in a turn somebody woke reads only for somebody of the company who may act — and a seat's runner is handed the builtins its seat is refused outright (`RunnerFor` takes the context and asks `WithheldBuiltins` (`withheldbuiltins.go`): `builtin.Withheld` over a snapshot of the runner's own registry, as the seat's principal, against the epoch it pins), so what a seat is offered and what it is admitted are one decision — and the code writes too where the seat's code tier writes no repository (`builtin.CodeWritesWithheld` over that epoch's role, `gitlet.Tier(role.CodeAccess)` as `codeRoster` reads it): every call of one would be refused by every repository's grant, which the authority table, admitting everybody to a code write so each repository's grant decides it, cannot say ahead of the calls. `WithheldBuiltins` takes the chart, the seat and a registry snapshot rather than reading the epoch because it has a second reader, the count of the built-ins each agent is offered on Settings › MCP & tools (`mcp_servers_status`' `builtin_tools`, which the API's runtime decides over the chart that answer lists the agent in, against the snapshot its rows came from), and one answer for both is what keeps that count from parting from a turn's surfaces. A data node's ALARM TRANSITIONS leave through an OUTBOX (`alarmwakes.go`, armed with the retention loops and stopped with them): the tracker hands each one on as it is made, and the outbox publishes it as the inbound delivery `alarmwake` routes (source `alarms`), retried until the broker takes it — two seconds doubling to the alarm interval — and in order, bounded at 1024 with the oldest not in flight dropped and counted (`crewlet.alarm.transitions.dropped`, the taken ones on `crewlet.alarm.transitions{change}`); a transition still queued when the node stops is not recovered, since the next start's first evaluation says what is still firing. The alarms' parser rides `nativeParsers` on every node, whatever halves it runs, built per epoch from the chart's granted seats. Gitlet's (`gitlet.NewParser`, `gitlet.Prompt`) rides it where the company hosts its repositories (`halves.code`), its wakes relayed by the code log's feed the register's code entry declares (`crewlet-code-feed`), and `gitlet.Prompt` is one of `workItemNamers`, so a code wake charges its turn to the work item the change implements (ADR-0097). WHO A SEAT'S TURN IS ACTING FOR IS COMPOSED ONCE (`turnRequesters`, requesters.go), handed to the seat's registry (`builtin.Deps.Requesters`, equip.go) and to the families that decide a relation beside it (`ArtifactToolDeps`, `CodeToolDeps`, each read per call) — an agent seat as its own principal, every app and never admin, and a person's seat as the person the routed identity directory says holds it, at their stage, holding the apps their grants name and never admin (ADR-0109): that one seat's requester (`Identity.Requester`), never the whole directory, because it is composed at most once a call; a removed holder, a stage that may not act and a seat no person holds use no app — so one requester is one principal whichever tool decides for it. WHO WOKE IT is read off the trigger by one resolution (`subjectOf`, prefetch.go) for the requester, the senders and every counterparty profile alike: a vendor's sender as an id in its own namespace, and a FIRST-PARTY notice's writer by the kind its record holds (`types.NotificationWriter`, `writerSubject`) — a seat's change, or a person's as their seat, that seat by its handle; the engine's own, nobody, though the turn works for whom its record about an agent's earlier enqueue carries (the merge queue's ejection and a close it could not make: `writtenForIn` reads an agent's writer and the engine's alike, for the woken turn, a parked run's reply and a chat edit's worked-through key); a credential acting as no seat, its platform identity and its LOGIN, the requester the turn is then worked for by the apps every one of its writes carried (`credentialRequesterOf`, onto `turnctx.Turn.RequesterLogin`/`RequesterApps` and the parked run's row), narrowed again per call by what THIS node resolves the login to (`turnRequesters.Credential`: a `token:` login by this node's Tier A alone, any other by the directory alone, ADR-0109); a removed seat or an unknown kind, the platform identity a stranger keeps — because the tracker's and the knowledge base's actor is spelled as a handle, a login or a node's name alike, and read as a vendor id every colleague's comment woke a stranger's turn. The requester is the first voice besides the seat's OWN (`requesterOf`): the outcome of its own act asks it nothing, and a colleague who speaks after it is who the turn is for. THE LIVE TURNS' CODE HOLDS (codeholds.go, M3-decisions M3-O61, M3-O64) — the scratch repositories and the seats' branches a turn named, by keys this package never decodes: a turn's `turnctx.Holds` lives in this process while it runs — every running turn in one registry (`runningTurns`), entered by the dispatch and a resume for the turn's whole run — and on its RUN ROW once it launches (`sandbox.PendingRun.CodeHolds`): both launch paths write the set on the row they open and attach the row's writer through `Opened` (the seal), and a resumed turn starts from the row's set (`rowHolds`), so a parked run holds what its turn named until its row ends; a coding box's push adds the seats' branches it names to the same row (`boxRuns.Hold`), because a box pushes as its run and never as a turn, and a row that is gone or ending holds nothing new and is no error, since a run that has ended depends on nothing. `Engine.codeHolds` is the code-retention pass's `Retention.Holds`: every run row, whatever its status (`ListRuns`) — listed BEFORE and AGAIN AFTER the ask, because a turn's first launch moves its holds from its memory onto a new row while the ask is on its way, and a row written after the first listing by a turn whose frame returned before the ask reached its node was in neither answer — and every seat-lease holder's running turns, asked on `crewlet.code.holds` (an unsigned ephemeral read scatter inside `eventfan.FleetReadBudget`, lasting until every holder the leases NAME has answered — never a count of replies, because every node answers it, one holding no seat among them, and its answer would fill the count while a holder's went unread) — the seat holders, never the presence listing, because a draining node gives its presence back before its turns finish — and a store, a lease read or a holder that cannot be read is an error, so a page expires nothing on an unknown. The box credentials are built at `New` on EVERY node over the fleet keyring, whatever its roles: a coding box's run tokens (`BoxTokens`) and a check's box's tokens (`CheckTokens`), in one key domain — the node that launches a run or claims a check mints, whichever Gitlet node the box dials verifies, and the API verifies either on a node where Gitlet is not built (`api.CodeSurface.VerifyBox`) — and Gitlet admits both by them (`host.Boxes`). A `direct` or `container` box is its launching node's (`sandboxhost.go`): every node runs a waiter of its own for the boxes on its host beside the fleet's, answers a peer's signed read of one of them (`sandbox.box_read`, only what the run's row licenses, for its share of the wait the request carries, counted from its arrival on this node's clock; at most sixty-four at once and sixty-four waiting, busy past them, on a registration wider than both) and listens for the nudges (`crewlet.sandbox.nudge`) its waiters act on — a run's `submit_work` recorded by the bridge ledger, a box left to its host — and the fleet's waiter reads the presence leases to find a node gone for good. The sandbox coordinator holds this process's TAKER LEASE (`coordinatorOptions`): the node's own lease store, through `holdLeases`, whose give-back is a step of the stop's one allowance once the teardown has begun — the coordinator gives it back on the lifetime it claimed it on, which no stop's context reaches — the engine's incarnation and the seat lease TTL in force, none of the three without the store, and `stopSandbox`, called by the teardown alone, after the drain, which keeps the lease while presence is gone, stops it only once its last keep has ended (`TestTheSandboxIsStoppedOnlyByTheTeardownAfterTheDrain` holds the order from the source); the runtime is built and published under one lock (`publishSandbox`), never built to lose a compare-and-swap, since a second coordinator would claim the same lease at the same epoch and give it back under the published one's keeps. A node's MODEL STATE is gathered for its status record (`modelstate.go`: each entry's sign-in plan, the parks naming it, the login watcher of `hostlogin.go`) and its executor cells probed (`runnerprobe.go`); a person's retry is a coordination record every node applies by its stamp (`modelretry.go`), a device sign-in one record per model whose process runs on one node (`devicesignin.go`), a model's check one record per model, run on the node that took it under the node's lifetime and its own budget and read by any node (`modelcheckrun.go`: one check of a model at a time, the same ask handed the running one, a stopping node's checks ended saying so, a removed model's record cleared at the apply), a run refused on a sign-in replaced while it ran is carried on once (`signInReplaced`), and so is one whose provider refused the request it made in a session it continued, in a session of its own (`requestRefused`). The model alarms' and the MCP alarms' inputs are fleet-wide, read off ONE listing of the presence leases and the status records joined to them per evaluation, which carries the last good one across a blip in either (`modelalarms.go`, `mcpalarms.go`); an MCP server's status-record row names the instance its `down_since` and `waiting_since` date, so its failure and its outage are one instance's facts. WHAT ITS MCP SERVERS LIST is derived off its bridges at every beat and written only when it moved, under its presence epoch, schemas first, on a PASS OF ITS OWN beside the beat (`startListingPass`: one at a time, a beat that finds one in flight leaving its listings to it), each write waiting as long as the coordination store's rule gives its bytes rather than sharing the beat's fifteen seconds, which only the beat's own steps keep (`mcpBeatBound`), because a listing at its bound across a link at the floor takes about 26 s a send; every node keeps a watched copy of the fleet's listings, which the tool catalogue adds a per-agent server's tools from and which a server's detail reads, and the maintenance sweep removes what no reader counts (`mcplistings.go`, ADR-0074). HOW EACH INSTANCE IS stays in the node's memory and is read off ONE reading of each instance (`mcpReadings`) by both the status record's row and a server's page, which asks the nodes holding one over `crewlet.observe.mcp_instances` within the history read's two-second budget (`mcpinstances.go`, the ADR-0021 pattern). A PERSON'S RESTART is a coordination record every node watches and applies once per stamp to the instances it runs whose state began before it, a seat's by its holder alone, and the apply that removes a server or seat deletes it (`mcprestart.go`). A PERSON'S TEST of a server is asked of the nodes that run it over a SIGNED channel (`mcp.check`, since the node asked starts a process or dials as the company), every live node for a shared server and only the node that proves it holds the seat for a per-agent one, each testing with the values a start of it would get (`resolveServer`, `serverSpec`) and answering a bounded, redacted report; a saved server's test is recorded as its latest (`coord.MCPCheck`) and swept with the server, a draft's never (`mcpcheck.go`). EXECUTOR SESSIONS (`executorsession.go`, ADR-0073): the sandbox runtime keeps each launch's session and records every turn's end through the estate router's `ExecutorWrites` (`Engine.executorLog`, set in `New` — never a data node's own writer, since a seat's box is wherever its seat runs), with `executorKeep` (the switch and the per-seat budget off the FLEET's revision, the keyring the secrets a capture scrubs by value are sealed under — references resolved, the entry's sign-in, its login's strings, and every token an integration issues the seat, at the launch and as the completion poll renews it in a running box, which `refreshTokenFiles` records before it writes (`recordToken` through the waiters' `Record`, `sandbox.Coordinator.RecordHanded`), a token the store does not take not written on that poll — the turn's mint by `rebaseFor`, the sealed put). Every way a turn that launched its executor ends records its END, OWED on the run's record (`oweExecutorEnd`) for the run's ending to record: the resume's (`recordResume`, a stop its own fence met, an abandon — a relaunch that failed in its launch included, whose record waits for the frame — and a panic once begun, `resumeGuarded`), and the coordinator's for EVERY stop of a run, parked, launching or before its park, and every loss (`closeStoppedTurn`, `answerLostTurn`, which read delivery with `turn.Answered` off the run's bridged log the coordinator read for the ending and hands them, a log it could not read being `DeliveryUnknown`, never "did not reply") — a frame whose launch a stop decided on the run ended (`stoppedByRun`) answers and owes nothing. Only a frame that finds no record left — a dispatch's launch its own fence refused once its record was written — records the end at once (`endTurnNow`). `answerColleague` reports what it sent and whether the reply went. A launch's secrets for its session's scrub are the engine's to name, by where each value came from (`launchSecrets`, `referencedValues`). AN MCP SERVER SIGNED IN TO WITH OAUTH (`mcpoauth.go`, ADR-0075) has one grant for the company: a sign-in started on any node (`StartMCPSignIn`) is a state signed under the fleet keyring and spent once by whichever node the vendor returns the browser to (`CompleteMCPSignIn`), the refresh token sealed in `fleetsecrets.Estate` and the public half a `coord.MCPGrant`; each node's access token is held in its memory by a per-grant source (`bearerFor`) the shared-server pass hands the spec, only while the server's address is the resource the grant was issued for, renewed under the fleet's `mcp_oauth_renewal` lease and written back at the version read, a refusal for good recorded so no node asks again; a sign-out or a removal revokes at the vendor and deletes (`RevokeMCPGrant`), and a sign-in or sign-out re-activates the revision. A SERVICE IDENTITY (`oauth.grant: client_credentials`, `mcpserviceidentity.go`) is the same source with another way to be issued a token: nobody signs in, nothing is kept and no lease is taken (nothing shared is spent), each node signs in as the company's own client when a request needs a token, reading the token endpoint from the server's metadata, and holds a failure for the restart schedule's wait (`serviceIdentity.issue`), a new id, secret or scope list being a new source asked at once. `resolveServer` holds a server back whose own client's secret does not resolve. A test that sends what it tests (a draft, or a value with its entry as the fleet's active revision states it, so no node needs to have applied the write) sends the entry and every value sealed apart under the fleet's keyring, bound to its request (`mcpEntryAAD`, `mcpValueAAD`, wire version 3), never recorded. A TEST'S NODE ON AN EXTERNAL BROKER IS SIZED BY THE BROKER'S ACCOUNT (`declared.go`, `ErrUndeclaredBrokerLimit`): such a node reads the account's limit and never a server's cap, and where the account states none it stands three quarters of its own volume's free space in for one and sizes its object bucket and state logs from that, so in a test binary `openNATS` refuses it before anything is reserved on the broker, naming `externaltest.Start`; a member (its own server's cap) and a leaf (its members' account, which the engine's own broker never limits) are not asked, nor is a budget the broker did not answer, and only a case under `SizedByTheMachine` is let through. THE STORES ON A BROKER CONNECTION ARE TOLD WHETHER IT CROSSES A LINK BY ONE READING (`acrossLink`, every broker kind but a member's): the object store's puts' window (`natsobj.Config.AcrossLink`), the coordination store's write budgets (`coordinationReach`, `kv.Reach`) and the memory sync's hydrations, their pulls' window and their requests' crossing (`memsync.Config.AcrossLink`, beside the host's `HeartbeatInterval` and `clusteredStream`; `memsync.New` refuses a link with no heartbeat, which fails the engine's build). `runnerResume` READS THE COPY'S MEASURE off the job's record (`sandbox.LaunchRecord.Context`, into `runner.Resume.ContextTokens`), the job's own request's or one carried along the copy's lineage, which the runner holds a relaunch continuing the copy in place to beside the job's newest capture's. `runnerResume` OPENS THE RUN'S LEADS as the coordinator read them beside its row (`sandbox.ResumeRequest.Leads`, `resumeInput.Leads`; `resumeLeads`, under the coordinator's own `leads` seal, `sandbox.NewLeadSeal`): those the job's record marks as its brief's told, by their numbers, as told, a held lead's result opened through the call seal, and one that does not open handed on as unread, never dropped (`sandbox_lead_unread` at warning for a key or a kind this node cannot read, `sandbox_lead_unopened` at error); where the coordinator could not read them at all, those the job's record marks, by their numbers, for the relaunch to hand on (`unreadLeads`, `runner.Resume.LeadsUnread`); and the turn's frozen base (`frozenBase`), which a task lead told again says the turn began after. THE PROBE'S WINDOW (`agentLauncher.continuationWindow`): the least standard window over the chain's Claude Code members, each on its entry's route (`claudeRoute`), set on every basis it answers, with a kept capture's count (`runner.SessionBasis.Count`); the resume hands the runner the job's record's count and restart basis (`runner.Resume.Count`, `BasisRestart`); and the launch floor judges a member under the window Claude Code holds its model to on its exact platform, read off its entry (`floorWindow`, `claudePlatform`), so a session kept under a larger window is not continued on a member one request of whose model cannot carry it. A TURN'S RECORD (`publishTurnCompleted`) carries the turn's calls as `runner.Spend.Calls` keeps them, every phase's kept list held to a record's one share with what neither list keeps counted by tool (`calls_elided`, `elided_calls`), and `actedOutside` reads a call such a tally counts as succeeded as surely as a listed one. `Engine.Start` runs the node on a context detached from its caller's, yet A CONTEXT THAT ENDS WHILE THE NODE STARTS BEGINS THE HOST'S DRAIN at once (`context.AfterFunc` → `Node.BeginDrain`), because the seat host's first pass runs inside the start and a caller's shutdown begins only once Start returns: without it a signal during that pass claimed the node's whole share onto a node already stopping THE NODE'S STATUS RECORD is written on a loop of its own (`nodestatusrecord.go`), never on the beat, so a slow data path delays the record and never the presence: every renew period it builds the rows from memory (`statusRecordRows`) and writes them when they moved, comparing everything but each model's last answer, which moves on every call, when the presence epoch moved, and at least every `coord.ReconcileInterval` whatever moved, four writes inside the bucket's age, and at once when the seat host claims the presence at a new epoch (`seat.Config.PresenceClaimed`, which wakes the loop), so a boot is never a renew period unreported; it writes nothing while the node holds no presence, and a clean stop removes the record through the latest epoch it asked to write at, as ONE STEP OF THE STOP'S ALLOWANCE after the seats are given back and beside the MCP listings' removal (`removeStatusRecord`, `removeMCPListings`), never under a bound of its own, since the fallbacks are the bucket's age and the sweep and no reader counts either once the presence has gone. Every reader of a node's rows — the model retry's `fleetSitsOut`, the model and MCP alarms, the Models page, the servers' status and the atlas — reads the records joined to the live presence (`liveStatusRecords`) |

## The agent runtime

| Package | What it is for |
|---|---|
| `agent/turn` | The turn loop — an executor and a reviewer — the guards around them, and the one question the whole engine turns on: did this turn actually reach anybody? WHO IS WAITING is derived at dispatch from the trigger's own type and WHAT RAN is the tool loop's own record, so neither half is something a model can get wrong. It replaced a reconciliation of the planner's PREDICTED tools against reality, which is where both recorded delivery incidents came from. WHAT RAN includes what the run's record could not keep: a round's elided middle (`Work.Elided`) is read by every delivery check as calls by name and outcome (`Work.Called`, `Record`, a closed round's too) and closes with the round (`ledger.Iteration.Elided`), so a reply posted from the middle of a long run is never refuted and sent back to be posted again. WHAT A TURN DID OUTSIDE THE ENGINE is one rule beside it (`Acted`): a delivery, or a call whose own annotations say open-world and do not say read-only — a read of the world (`ssh_read`, a search annotated as one) changes nothing a retry would repeat. It decides whether a `no_action` may end the turn as skipped (`Check`, which read deliveries alone, so a turn nobody asked for that asked a colleague and said nobody was asking ended skipped) as well as whether a broken turn is run again, and every correction that turns on an act names the tools the turn acted with (`ActedWith`, five by name, then a count), since a model told it called nothing argues with the correction, and the `no_action` correction names apart, as one the engine cannot tell from a write, a call to an MCP tool that no hint marks as a read or as a write, from its server or from an operator's override (`ActedAccount`, `Surface.PresumedWrites`), since a model told it acted after only a search argues the same way; and none says a turn filed its work elsewhere or reached somebody on such a call alone (`KnownDelivered`). A BROKEN TURN IS GIVEN UP rather than run again from its trigger by one rule for both paths a turn arrives by (`Abandon`): after a panic, after a refusal a retry would meet again (`ErrPermanent`, which a round lost with its host past its limit carries), or after a call that rule counts (`Result.Acted`) |
| `agent/runner` | Drives the phases against real models and real tools — the wiring between the prompt builder, the tool registry, the provider chain and the turn loop's contract, none of which knows the others. Plumbing, plus the one rule plumbing cannot express: what happens when the model never gives a structured answer at all. What a valid answer IS lives here too, in the decoders; the SHAPE is `agent/structured`. Every phase is a harness's own loop over the seat's tools, and every phase but the executor runs on this host, served from the process's one tool-session host (`Config.Sessions`, required — a runner with none is refused before any turn). The executor is ONE runtime (`agentrun.go`): its harness's own agentic run in a box — the launch, which parks the turn, and the collection, which replays the run's bridged calls, in the order the bridge admitted them, through the run's surface rebuilt as the run left it to recover the submission it made, with the same prompt, the same surface, the same submission and the same rescue as every phase, and only the rounds happening in the harness. The run's log keeps each call to its share of the log, so the live `submit_work` refuses a submission past what the log keeps whole (`keptWhole`, `sandbox.MaxSubmissionBytes`), and the replay never replays a newest submission the log kept in part (`BridgedCall.ArgsBytes`): the round is rescued rather than reported with a marker as its summary. The live `submit_work` also refuses a `no_action` once the turn's record shows it acted outside the engine, saying what it did (`makingWork`, `turn.ActedAccount`), a call to an MCP tool that no hint marks as a read or as a write, from its server or from an operator's override, named as one the engine cannot tell from a write — live only: the replay judges a made submission against every call the round made, those after it included, and a `no_action` the run made before it acted is the run's own account, which `turn.Check` corrects, rather than a round rescued as one that submitted nothing. Every segment that re-enters a run SEEDS the turn's call log BY IDENTITY (`seedCalls`): each kept call by its digest, each call the log dropped from a long run's middle by its tallied identity (`Resume.Elided`), each call a bridged call's own work made — a `delegate`'s workers, which count on the call's own log (`subagent.Tool.CallForTurn`) — by its tallied identity too (`Resume.Absorbed`), a call whose arguments the record withholds (a held call and the one a person approved) as an unknown call of its tool, EVERY CALL THE LOG HELD IN FLIGHT by the identity its entry carries (`Resume.Unfinished`, and a reopen's `unfinished`: a call still running on the node the seat was taken from among them), and the closed rounds' calls and both their tallies likewise, so a cut argument, a dropped call, a worker's call or a call still running never moves an operation id a later call derives. A CALL IN FLIGHT AS THE ROUND IS READ is listed in the round's record after its calls, failed, as one whose OUTCOME IS UNKNOWN (`ledger.Call.Unfinished`, `UnfinishedNote`: its effects may stand, to be checked before it is made again, never a plain failure), and told to every run carrying the round on beside its late calls, the copy in its job's own box whatever its harness's end among them, under words that say it may have taken effect (`calledAfter`), and read by the round's reviewer and every later round under rules that except it alike from the failed calls that did not take effect (`prompts.ReviewHeader`, `prompts.PriorWorkHeader`, `prompts.ContinuedRoundRules`); never a submission, a delivery or a load. A person's answer to a run's question CARRIES THE RUN ON (`asked.go`, `Resume.Asked`) rather than being reported to the turn: the run continues the asking job's kept session (`continued.go`) or, where it was not kept, its own in its kept box, or starts afresh told the question, the answer and its branch. A FRESH RUN CARRYING A ROUND ON IS TOLD EVERY CALL OF IT (a loss, an answer or a replaced sign-in whose job's session its launch continues neither way, and a provider's refusal of the session its job continued, which no launch continues): its brief is the round's own, then THE TURN'S EARLIER ANSWERS, every answer lead the run keeps beside its record, oldest first, each naming the round it came in (`turnsAnswers`, `earlierAnswers`, `prompts.KeyAnswersBefore`), which a send-back's fresh brief tells too since the ledger carries none of a person's replies, the call one of them approved in no list and one of a round the ledger lists pointed to there only where that list says how it ended, and one in an entry that may count it rather than list it (a span of rounds a park dropped, a round whose tally may count its tool's calls, as every round beside a span does, or a read past the read cap) told with what became of it as one of that entry's calls (`answerAt`, `doneFor`, `heldIn`), then what became of the run before it (`prompts.KeyCarriedOn`), each form of an answer's saying as a loss's and a replaced sign-in's do that what that run did through the seat's tools did happen (`happened`), then every call of the round from its start (`calledAfter` at zero, `prompts.CalledBefore`, `prompts.KeyRoundSoFar`) within the prior-work ledger's budgets, built for every relaunch carrying a round on before its budget's last ask since its box may fall back to it, and taken whole by a base's continuation; and A SESSION HOLDS WHAT ITS BRIEFS TOLD, NEVER WHAT THEIR BUDGETS LEFT OUT: each brief that tells a round's calls answers the reads its read cap counted without listing them, off the render's own walk (`ledger.ShownCallGroups`, `leftOut`), which its launch records on the job it opens (`Continuation.Told`, `Fresh`, `SessionContinuation.Told`, `sandbox.BriefTold`), and the next brief to that job's copy (`Resume.Owed`, the record's `OmittedCalls`) or to a capture of it placed by its read (`SessionBasis.Owed`) tells them first, held at no point (`heldAt`), under words of their own (`prompts.AfterSave.Owed`), the one read cap spent on them last. A round carried on in its job's own box (an answer or a replaced sign-in, `Continuation.Brief`) is told, where the job's harness is not known to have ended its own run (`Resume.HarnessEnd`, the job's record's own word) and the box is still there (`carried.boxKept`), what such a copy may lack (`inPlaceAfter`): that it was cut off where the record says so (`prompts.InterruptedCopy`), and that it may have been where it says nothing (`prompts.MayBeInterruptedCopy`), in words that speak of no save, whether or not a call follows; EVERY CALL OF THE ROUND under the header of calls it may not show, every dropped one counted (`calledInPlace`), but the call a person approved that an answer's lead tells; and EVERY NOTE THE TURN HAS READ, counted under a header of notes it may already show (`prompts.NotesReadAgain`) — never the job's own calls or notes alone, since the copy is not known to hold the job's brief (no probe measured whether Claude Code 2.1.310 writes a continued session's next message before it answers a call), and a copy that lacks it lacks the calls and the notes it told, which no record names; a copy whose harness ended its own run is told every note from the first read no session of its job holds on, under the same header (`firstUnheld`), and a note the run's bridge served it the same way; the notes a copy is told come from one place (`inPlaceNotes`). Where the harness did end its own run, the copy holds every call its job made and every one its job's brief told — every call of the round but the ones the log took late, a fresh run's brief telling every one of them — but the reads that brief counted without listing them (`Resume.Owed`), which it is told first, and is not known to hold a late one, nor the outcome of one NOBODY WAITED FOR (`BridgedCall.Abandoned`: a call its harness cancelled, or that outlived its job's session on the bridge, which it shows as timed out or with no outcome) (`heldAt` at a nil point), nor that of a call still in flight as the round was read (`Resume.Unfinished`), which no record holds: it is told those alone, under their own words, with how many of them the log dropped from its middle counted there (`prompts.AfterSave.LateDropped`), and before them that a successful `submit_work` among the calls it holds does not stand (`prompts.AfterSave.HeldSubmission`), so every continuation of the round built after a late call landed is told it, in a kept session and in place alike. And the leads its job's brief told, which a copy that lacks the brief lacks too, as a kept session cut off that lacks its own does (`keptSession`): told again after its cut-off section and before its calls, as below (`leadsAgain`). All of it is built before the budget's last ask as the kept brief is, and its guard holds the launch's skills without the job's own loads (`sessionDecided`). A reply in the conversation it asked in reaches it inside a fence of the relaunching turn's token, labelled as the reply's trigger labels the message (ADR-0040); an answer given by turn reaches it defused and unfenced, as the instruction of the person who gave it. A REPLY TOO LARGE FOR THE RUN reaches it as no answer at all (`Asked.Refused`, `RefusedReply`, ADR-0114): told in the engine's words, quoting nothing, that the person replied, how many messages it was and both figures, that it was given none of it, and to ask again with `ask_and_wait` — saying in the question why, for a shorter form or another way to get it — or carry on and say in its final reply that the reply never reached it; and the round's reviewer is told of the refusal beside the round's calls, in none of which the reply is (`RunRecord.RefusedReplies` to `turn.Work.RefusedReplies`, rendered by `prompts.RefusedReplies` with the duplicate rule's exception beside it), so a question asked again after one reads as the follow-up it is. So does a job LOST with its host (`lost.go`, `Resume.Lost`): the same round relaunched in the lost job's kept session where its launcher says it can — the capture its loss's read took where its box was left — told it was cut off (`Lost.Continuation`), and in a fresh session otherwise and after a second loss in the round, told it was cut off, why and where to look for its work — unless the round's calls already hold its submission, which collects it, never a late call's (`sinceAnswer`, which the collection's replay reads through too: a late call was made by a job that is over, the first job carrying the round on was never told it, and every later continuation is told it as no submission of the round) — and refused past its limit with `ErrRoundLost`, closing the round's phase failed and ending its turn: the refusal carries `turn.ErrPermanent`, so the resume settles the run rather than retry a refusal every retry meets. A node that recovers a run still RUNNING — after a restart or a move — rebuilds its surface from the turn's parked state and the calls already in the run's log (`ReopenAgentRun`), so the next call's operation id carries on from them. Every segment that re-enters a run — its collection, an answer or a loss carrying it on, a reopen — makes ONE rebuild (`rebuiltSurface`): the tools and skills the launch recorded — the skills only where the job's box said it took up the session its launch chose (`Resume.Continued`, off the job's record; `Resume.parked`), none where it started its own or said nothing — then every skill its own job loaded over the bridge (past `execstate.State.BridgeFrom`), and the turn's call log seeded with the round's calls; a relaunch then re-arms its guard for the session it takes up, as the launch decides (`sessionDecided`), and empties it again where the run's box says, before the run's first call, that it started a session of its own instead (`AgentRunRequest.Started`, `sessionStarted`; a run taken over is told the same through `ReopenedRun.Started`). AN EXECUTOR HOLDS EVERY TOOL ITS SEAT DOES from its launch (`executorActive`) — every MCP server's but those that run in its box — and no discovery pair (`discovers`): its harness keeps a large server's tools out of its requests itself, so a run activates nothing and its record lists nothing left to discover. The collection calls nothing through its surface, and is rebuilt the same way all the same. And the surface's own METER (`BridgedSpend`), because the harness calls the seat's tools after the segment that launched it has charged what it spent, so what those calls cost (the auxiliary rewrites, the delegated workers) is counted apart, written to the run's row with every bridged call and paid by the segment that resumes. A seat's WITHHELD builtins (`Config.Withheld`: the tools the authority table refuses that seat outright, computed by the engine once per runner from the epoch it pins) leave the registry's snapshot through ONE method every phase reads (`seatSnapshot`) — the executor in either runtime and on a resume, the reviewer, the onboarding turn and the workers a phase hands its tools to — so a seat is never shown a tool whose every call it would be refused. The DISCOVERY PAIR (`list_mcp_server_tools`, `activate_tool`) never passes through the registry, so `DiscoveryTools` hands it out as ENTRIES carrying their own hints — read-only, idempotent, closed-world — which every phase surface and a worker's (`subagent.Config.Discovery`) add as built: activating changes only which of the seat's tools the phase is offered, and a pair a caller wrapped bare reached the bridge unannotated, where a person's stop refused a stopped run's discovery as a write it could not classify. It hands the extension judge the turn's fence token (`extension.Request.Boundary`), so the judge can rekey the task's fences onto a token of its own call, and writes the judge's verdict back into the conversation only through `extension.Nudge`, which defuses the judge's reason under that same turn token — a reason is a model's words about other people's words, and spliced in raw it could close a fence the seat reads. A person's NOTES reach a phase through ONE SOURCE (`SteerSource`, `Config.Steer`): the first segment's box, or the run's record a resumed segment reads — taken at a round of a host phase, PEEKED for a run's brief (`Brief`), whose launch records them read and publishes them delivered once it has launched (`Launched.LaunchID`), and given back when the launch fails BEFORE ITS WRITE (`Return`) — a launch that fails after it names the job it opened, and the record's ending reports them; a resumed segment opens with every note the record says the turn read (`Resume.Steered`), and the execute record lists its round's reads at round 1 with the kind of step — every job's of the round, the asking job's included where an answer carried the run on, as it lists that job's calls (`runReads`), and none the record marks unanswered, which its harness was never handed. Four kinds of round are CARRIED ON rather than collected: one a person's answer drives (`Asked`), one lost with its host (`Lost`), one refused on a sign-in replaced while it ran (`Rotated`, once), and one whose continued session its provider refused (`Refused`, once, in a session of its own). A seat's EXECUTOR LISTS NO TOOL SKILL: each launch hands its harness the skills its phase matches as the harness's own (`handSkills`, `AgentRunRequest.Skills`), named `crewlet:<name>` from the key (`nativeNames`: lowercase, every run of anything but a letter or digit one hyphen, collisions told apart in key order), described by the summary and ending with a note for a required one, with the body `load_tool_skill` answers; its harness reports each load, which the bridge records as the run's `load_tool_skill` call, so the guard, the page's reads and every rebuild read one kind of load. The names are on the parked state (`execstate.State.NativeSkills`), which every rebuild hands the guard and every reopened bridge session reads a reported load by. EVERY LAUNCH ASKS WHICH SESSION IT CONTINUES (`continued.go`; `SessionProber`, asked before the briefs are written, on a turn's first launch too). A RELAUNCH CONTINUES THE SESSION OF THE JOB IT FOLLOWS where its launcher says it can: its brief is the session's next message (`keptSession`: what carries it on — a send-back's `prompts.BuildContinuedRound`, the answer, the loss, the sign-in — that it may end mid-call, every call the session may not have been answered — every call the round's log took after the capture's save point (`SessionBasis.Through`, read against `BridgedCall.Appended`, never a call's place, so a call in flight at the read is told whatever the log dropped below it; one with no number is after every save point, and so is a LATE one, `ledger.Call.Late`, taken after its job was replaced, which no brief of the round had told when the log took it and the session of the job that made it may not hold, and one NOBODY WAITED FOR, `BridgedCall.Abandoned`, whose job's session shows it with no outcome or a failed one — `heldAt`, the one test by the save point — told apart, under words of their own whatever the session's end, again by every later continuation of the round but one of a session the row says TOOK it, by a telling at or below its stamped note cursor (`heldByTelling`), a successful `submit_work` among them told as none of the round's; and never the call a person approved for the held call an answer's lead tells, `carried.tellsHeld` against `BridgedCall.HeldID`, which a later attempt at that answer reads in the log past the save point of every capture taken before it was made, while one an earlier answer approved is told as any call is; and a successful `submit_work` among the rest told as none of the round's too, every call a brief tells having been made before the relaunch it opens, `prompts.AfterSave.EarlierSubmission`; and, first and whether or not a call is listed, one the session holds, `prompts.AfterSave.HeldSubmission`, set only by the round's own calls), rendered as the prior-work ledger renders a round and held to its budgets (`ledger.Format`, fitted as `priorWork` fits it), under ONE read cap with the late calls' list (`ledger.FormatCallGroups`, spent on the late calls first, a late call left out recorded apart from the owed reads, `omittedReads`) — then how many more it took after the save and dropped from its middle, exact where every call is numbered (`droppedAfterSave`: the appends past the point less the listed calls numbered past it, a listed call with no number never taken off, so a log an older build appended to may over-count, held to the round's dropped calls, and never under-counts), with the round's tally (`prompts.CalledAfter`, `ledger.FormatElided`) and, under the late calls' own words whatever the point, how many LATE calls it dropped (`Resume.LateElided`, `prompts.AfterSave.LateDropped`: they keep no mark and no number, and are never told as calls the session's own run made after its save; one past the point is counted among those as well, by its number, an over-count), told of a session cut off (`SessionBasis.Interrupted`, `prompts.AfterSave.Interrupted`) as calls it may already show, since its save point is the newest its launch held rather than where it ends (`calledAfter` sets `Interrupted` itself, from `mayShow`), and A CALL THE SESSION MAY SHOW THAT A TOLD CALL COULD BE TAKEN FOR LISTED AGAIN after the calls since the save and before the late calls (`readAlike`, `listedAgain`, `prompts.AfterSave.Shown`), first the turn's closed rounds' calls the brief tells nowhere else, each line ending with `prompts.EarlierRoundNote` (`closedShown`: none on a run of its own, `toldAs.fresh`; none of the closed entry standing for the round the calls are of, `roundCalls.of`, which a send-back's closed rounds hold last; none of the rounds the brief renders as work already done, `toldAs.rendered`, a task told again's `toldAgain.rendered`; no `submit_work`; and no withheld call of the round and tool of a held call a lead tells, `toldHeld`, since a closed list keeps no held id), then the round's own calls held at the point: the late calls' words, and those of calls the session may already show, say a call shown twice is ONE call, so a held or closed call and a told call of one tool whose lines do not tell their arguments apart, compared value by value as either line shows it, symmetric (`ledger.ShowsWhole` asked of both; a value both lines show whole as the JSON value it is, never its spelling, a null key as absent; a value either line fits, a value a record left out at any depth (`kept.IsLeftOut`), and arguments not comparable (`argsComparable`: sealed, in flight, not read back, undigested, or left out whole, `kept.IsNotKept`), alike whatever they were, never by how much a record kept (`ArgsBytes`), since a record says in the arguments what it left out; results never compared; `readalike.go`), would let the model take the told call for it and lose it; never a list under words that say the session lacks its calls, nor the owed reads; spent last under the one cap and never recorded omitted; and A TOLD CALL ANOTHER CALL OF THE BRIEF COULD BE TAKEN FOR MARKED, its line ending with `prompts.ToldApartNote` (`notes`, `partners.couldBeTakenFor`: a shown call of the other told list read alike, both marked; a held call of its tool a lead tells, `toldAs.leads`, never a held call of the lead's own round and tool, whose fate the lead tells; the shown lines and the tallies naming its tool or none of the rounds the brief renders as work already done, `partnersOf`), on every path, a run of its own included, never within one list, on or against an owed read, against a line the cap left out, or on a call listed again; and WHAT THE SESSION MAY SHOW THAT NO LIST NAMES said in a section before the lists (`otherCalls`, `prompts.AfterSave.Other`), by the tools the told calls name and never by a count, only beside words that say a told call may be shown twice and never on a run of its own: the conversation's earlier turns where `Resume.Base` is set, the closed rounds where the session may hold them only in a summary's words (`toldAs.summed`), less the calls listed again, the calls the log no longer lists one by one where some may be in the session (`droppedMayShow`) and the closed rounds' tallies, and the workers' calls under a delegation (`roundCalls.absorbed`, `ledger.Iteration.Absorbed`), each tool name defused — every note the turn read after its `NoteCursor` (`steer.Reading.Order`, a read this segment made not yet numbered) — of a session cut off, every note the turn has read whatever its cursor, counted under a header of notes it may already show (`prompts.NotesReadAgain`), as the copy in a job's own box is told them; of one whose notes stop before a read the record marks UNANSWERED (`steer.Reading.Unanswered`, a take whose answer never reached its harness), every note from that read on under the same header, its cursor stopped there as the carried reads say even where the capture's stamp counted it, the mark having landed after (`notesHeldBy`), the carried reads marked first as the record marks them when the relaunch's brief reads it (`markUnanswered`), every brief telling each marked read again and the launch naming them for its write to record the telling (`AgentRunRequest.Retold`, `retold`) — and then the fresh ones, a note the run's bridge SERVED a harness told under the same header wherever it falls, the header taking every note up to the last served one so the order read is kept (`toldNotes`, `Resume.Served`), and every read told in the order the record numbered it — the segment's own reads, which reach the runner unnumbered, and a lost take's note the brief hands over at that take's place, from the places the brief's source gives (`inReadOrder`, `steer.Briefing.Orders`), so a later note correcting it is never told ahead of it), in the box it was last worked in and in any other (`prompts.BoxReplaced`), the fresh brief beside both — built, but for its lead (`keptBrief.led`), before the relaunch asks the seat's budget for the last time, since telling the calls after the save can call the seat's auxiliary model, while the lead of a round a person's answer carries on says what the call they approved returned, which is made only after that check; and the launch's context with it (`Continuation.ContextTokens`, the larger of the job's newest capture's measure and its record's, `Resume.ContextTokens`), for the floor of a run continued in its own box, and the read every brief was built from (off `Resume.BridgedRead`, a send-back's too), past which the launch's opening marks the round's calls late: a call of the job before that lands while the relaunch is readied is told by none of its briefs. A TURN'S FIRST LAUNCH, and a relaunch of a turn that has kept nothing, CONTINUES THE SESSION ITS CONVERSATION'S LAST TURN LEFT where its launcher answers one (`SessionBasis.Base`, `baseSession`): in a box of its own, its brief `prompts.BuildNextTurn`'s lead — where this turn begins, what a new box changes, and, where the base's turn's end said it went on past its session, where the session ends instead, or, where it was lost, that the session may not hold its last (`BaseBasis.WentOn`, `Lost`, `CapturedAt`) — then the launch's own fresh brief or reseed whole, which tells how the conversation's earlier turns ended (`Config.Endings`, frozen on the park as `execstate.Frozen.Endings`). A FRESH BRIEF NAMES THE KEPT SESSION ITS LAUNCH DOES NOT CONTINUE (`namingKeptSession`, `prompts.KeptSession`, appended as a section of its own, `prompts.KeyKeptSession`, by `prompts.Prompt.Then` after a base continuation has taken the brief whole): the session the probe read (`SessionBasis.KeptTurn`, the turn's own where a capture of it is kept, else the conversation's head the turn read, continued or not — on a relaunch only where the turn's row was read holding it, `sandbox.ContinuedSession.KeptTurn`), and to read it with `read_session` — only where the run's surface carries that tool, since a run that starts a session of its own cannot otherwise know one it could read is there — on every relaunch, a round lost a second time included, whose probe is asked for that alone; NEVER WHY it is not continued, which the probe words for the log and which quotes what said no, a store's error or what a box printed. THE GUARD FOLLOWS THE LAUNCH (`sessionDecided`, `AgentRunRequest.Decided`): the surface is built with an empty guard, and once the launch has its box and before the run starts it is armed for the session the run takes up (`skills.Guard.Replace`) — the launch state of the job it follows and that job's own loads (`ownCalls`: past `execstate.State.BridgeFrom`, a place in the order the bridge admitted calls, and none the log took after the capture's save point, by the test the calls after it are told by, nor a late one or one nobody waited for in any rebuild of the guard), or nothing for a session of its own or for the one the conversation's last turn left — and the brief the launch handed is published as the phase's opening frame (`emitter.prompted`). The job's parked state records the skills the launch armed AS IT ARMED THEM (`launchDecided.skills`), never the guard once the launch returns: the run starts inside the launch, and a load its harness reported by then is one of the job's own calls. Every rebuild replays only its job's own loads (`rebuiltSurface`). A SESSION THAT MAY LACK ITS JOB'S BRIEF (the copy in its box, `copyMayLackBrief`; a kept capture cut off, `SessionBasis.MayLackBrief`: the one predicate per path its calls, notes and leads read) IS TOLD AGAIN THE LEADS THAT BRIEF TOLD (`Resume.Leads`, `leadsAgain`, leads.go), after its cut-off section and before its calls, in words true of any session of the work: an answer as one a run of this work asked (`answerAgain`), a held call with what it returned and its approved call in no list (`withHeld`), a review from the notes of the round it closed, never as "the same session" (`prompts.ContinuedRound.Again`), said in its place as one not kept where a park has folded that round into a span, which keeps no review, never among the earlier leads not kept, since it can follow a lead told before it (`reviewOf`, `prompts.ReviewNotKept`), the turn's task with its boundary, saying what it says of the session the conversation's last turn left only of one that began as that one (`prompts.NextTurnAgain`), and the closed rounds its brief rendered, kept as the last one's number since a park's span makes fewer entries than rounds, a span that runs on past it told whole since it holds their calls (`taskAgain`, `turnAsk`, `roundsThrough`, `lastRound`) and then the turn's answers that brief told, or was to tell where its read of them failed, kept with the task in its order and never told apart where the task is told, each once under the oldest task told that carries it (`answersBetween`, `tasksTold`); and every brief hands its launch its own leads (`Asked.lead`, `reviewLead`, `taskLead`, `ownLeads`) and the ones it told again, or, where the resume could not read them, was to by their numbers (`sandbox.BriefTold.Own`, `Told`, `ToldUnread`, `Resume.LeadsUnread`), built once the call a person approved is made. EVERY PATH'S FIRST REQUEST IS JUDGED ONCE (`firstrequest.go`): Claude Code compacts a session before a request where its count with the brief reaches its threshold, so each path decides one value after its tellings and its lead exist (`sessionHolds`: the record's cut-off word, a restart of the job's thread after its brief, `restartedInJob`, a restart of its thread anywhere or one the count cannot rule out, `restarted`, which tells the closed rounds as ones it may hold only in a summary's words (`closedSummarized`, `restartedAnywhere`), and its harness's verdict, `firstRequest`, `settled`, `compacts`, judged on the held telling's brief, the kept path on the worse of its capture's count and its job's copy's, `keptCount`), and every telling and the guard read it: a session that may lack what it is counted as holding is told as a cut-off one, and one that may be a summary (`summarized`) is told every call of the round from its start, every lead its record keeps and every note the turn has read under `prompts.MayCompactSession`, its guard arming no skill; the kept path's calls are told by ONE expression (`keptCalls`), and a reading of what a cut-off session holds is handed to the telling beside it; A CUT-OFF KEPT SESSION IS TOLD ONLY THE CALLS IT LACKS where its first request is this build's to judge by its brief (`firstRequest.judges`, Claude Code's alone, the one predicate `settled`, `compacts` and the attach read): `keptTellings` asks the launcher once per path (`HoldingsAsker`, `heldReading`, never per telling, and not at all for a capture whose harness ended its own run, a verdict settled before any brief, or an ask of no call) for the release that wrote the session (`SessionBasis.Release`), the ask built by `holdingsAsk` from the calls the one expression tells (every listed call but the approved ones; asked only where the telling would list it, its key valid and carried by no other call listed or in flight, by ONE harness-keyed function, `callKey`, Claude Code's the call's id, OpenCode's its place in the log, every other harness's none), and builds a third telling (`keptTellings.exact`) that tells none of the calls the reading holds (they join the held side of the calls listed again and, where its job is past `BridgeFrom`, the guard's loads) and each call it finds in an unreported subagent's thread under that subagent's name, spent in the one cap after the late calls (`subagentCalls`, `subagentName`: the box's words, whitespace collapsed, cut to `ledger.ValueLimit` runes and defused); `keptTellings.led` decides it FIRST, attaching it (`SessionContinuation.Exact`, `ExactTelling`) only where its own brief sends whole, judged on its own brief, and else logging `executor_session_holdings_unused`, so ONE VALUE PER TELLING THE LAUNCH MAY HAND: the default's, and the exact telling's (`exactHolds`), which `sessionDecided` reads where the launch says it handed that brief (`SessionDecision.Exact`); both tellings are built before the budget's last ask (`keptTellings`, `inPlaceTellings`), and `executor_session_may_compact` says where the verdict switches one. A DURABLE AGENT RECORD HOLDS EACH PAYLOAD TO A SHARE OF ONE MESSAGE (`recordbytes.go`): a phase's record and a turn's are each one event, refused whole past `queue.MaxPayloadBytes`, so the tool list (`keptExecutions`: every row to the bridged call's shares, then the middle past `sandbox.MaxBridgeCalls` dropped into a tally by tool held to a census, `RecordCalls`), the round window (`keptRounds`, `maxRecordRounds`: a dropped round's calls tallied with it), the prompts (`keptPrompt`, part by part with the outline rewritten so it still tiles), the prose, the response, the notes and the tool names each keep their share and say what they left out, measured as the event encodes them, and `TestAPhaseRecordAtEveryBoundFitsOneMessage` holds the sum under the ceiling with every input at its worst. A collected run's copy of its report on its round says the response holds it whole (`answerCopy`). A review's words are held WHERE THEY ARE MADE (`reviewWithinBounds`): `submit_review` refuses a `final_artifact` past what a run's report may weigh and notes past what a submission may, naming the field, since the artifact is every record's and reply's answer and the notes every later round's brief. A RUNNING PHASE'S LIVE FRAME is held to live shares (`liveList`, `liveNarrationBytes`, `keptRunning`, `liveTail`), since it is republished whole five times a second and a slow client's queue holds hundreds: past them it keeps the NEWEST of what grows, its calls with no head (`listRule`, which the record's rule is the other value of), and counts the rest, and `TestARunningPhasesFrameAtEveryBoundStaysUnderAMiB` holds it under a MiB |
| `agent/runner/steersourcetest` | The one contract suite every `runner.SteerSource` runs: the first segment's box and the executor's record of a resumed segment, held to the same take, `Waiting` and brief promises. |
| `agent/structured` | How a phase gives a typed answer: it calls a tool, the ordinary tool loop carries the arguments out, and `Remarshal` maps them to a payload, refusing unknown fields. |
| `agent/phase` | The per-phase vocabulary: which phase is running, which provider chain it runs on, and what a turn concluded. |
| `agent/toolloop` | One phase's model-and-tools conversation: starts harness runs, serves each the phase's tools through a dispatcher that gates every call, and folds the runs back into the phase's record (ADR-0049) |
| `agent/steer` | A person's note to a running turn: the box one turn holds, the signed wire a note crosses, and the record of where it was read. |
| `agent/execstate` | The versioned wire format of a turn parked on its executor's run: the turn's bookkeeping and start-of-turn context, serialized into the pending-run row and held to its share of it. |
| `agent/extension` | The round-cap extension judge: when a phase runs out of tool rounds, a cheap model says whether it is progressing, and the engine grants more rounds or falls through to the rescue path. |
| `agent/subagent` | `delegate`: the short-lived leaf workers an executor hands work to, as a task graph of tool loops with a persona, a slice of the parent's tools, a declared answer and hard caps. |
| `agent/inbox` | What wakes a seat, and what it must not be woken by twice. `ledgered` is the completion side |
| `agent/ledger` | The prior-work ledger: `iteration` (within one turn, across its `self_iterate` rounds), `budgets`. Structured from what the engine recorded, never a summariser's. A PAYLOAD past its budget — an argument value, a failed call's error, a round's produced text — is REWRITTEN by the seat's auxiliary model, never cut: the ledger only NAMES the pieces (`IterationPieces`, `CallPieces`) and `ledgerfit` fits them, omitting an argument by size and digest where no rewrite can be had and carrying a draft whole. Whether a line shows an argument value as itself, not a piece fitted in its place, is `ShowsWhole`, the render's own test (`valuePiece`), which a caller asks rather than a rule of its own. A line's two halves are `CallLine`'s, ONE LINE EACH (a tool's line breaks drawn as spaces), shared with a renderer of one call. The same budgets (`Format`: the value limit and the read cap) hold the calls a run that continues a session, kept or in its job's own box, is told its round made (`agent/runner`'s `calledAfter`): those after the session's save, or every call of the round for a run that starts a session of its own carrying it on; the late ones (those that finished after their run stopped waiting for them, those nobody waited for, and those still in flight); and the reads an earlier brief counted without listing them, lists under ONE read cap (`FormatCallGroups`: the cap counts the reads of every group of a render together, in order, and each group's omitted line its own; `FormatCalls` is its one-group case, and `CallPieces` over the groups' concatenation names exactly what they show). `ShownCallGroups` is the walk that render draws its lines from, the calls each group shows, so a caller naming the reads a render counted without listing them (`agent/runner`'s `leftOut`) reads them off it. A caller may end a call's line with words of its own (`FormatOptions.Notes`), given by the call's PLACE in its group, never its position among the lines printed, never fitted, and printed nowhere for a call the render leaves out; `ShownIndices` is the same walk by place, which `ShownCallGroups` is built on, for a caller that must know which places a render shows. ACROSS TURNS IS NOT ITS: a conversation's next turn continues the session its last turn left and is told how the earlier turns ended from the executor log (`executordomain`, rendered by `agent/prompts`), so a conversation has one history — its transcript — rather than a second account kept beside it. A call carries its IDENTITY (`Call.Digest`, the digest its run's call log admitted it under) beside arguments a record may keep only in part — or says its arguments, and with them its identity, are sealed (`Call.Withheld`: a held call, and the one a person approved), or that its OUTCOME IS NOT KNOWN (`Call.Unfinished`: a bridged call its run's log held in flight as the round was read, failed, counted by its identity), or that its arguments were in text that COULD NOT BE READ (`Call.ArgsUnread`: its record's text does not read back, or the bridge could not parse what the model wrote, `sandbox.BridgeCall.ArgsUnparsed`; set by `engine`'s decode of a bridged call, counted as a call of its tool whose arguments are not known where it has no digest); a line says each in place of the arguments (`withheldArgs`, `unkeptArgs` for an unknown outcome that carries none, `unreadArgs`), never `tool()`, which reads as the tool called with none — and a round the calls its run's record DROPPED from a long run's middle, by identity (`Iteration.Elided`, `Elided`, an outcome nobody knows counted apart from the failures it is among, `Elided.Unfinished`, which `FormatElided` says may have taken effect, so a park that folds a round's calls into its tally never tells the next round such a call failed), and the ones its calls' own work made (`Iteration.Absorbed`, a delegated worker's): what a later segment seeds its call log by; the dropped ones read by the delivery checks as calls by name and outcome (`Called`, never for a render, which would draw a call made with no arguments) and said to the reviewer, the next round and a session a relaunch continues after its save (`FormatElided`), because a log that silently skips is a log that lies. A SPAN of rounds a park dropped is one entry standing for them (`Iteration.Through`, written by `agent/execstate`): their calls in its two tallies by tool, never a tool's name folded away, since the delivery checks read names, and its LAST round's artifact hash, which `agent/turn`'s stall guard is seeded by, so each round is still compared with the one before it; drawn as those rounds, saying what they set out to do, produced and were told is not kept (`renderSpan`), never as a round that did nothing |
| `agent/prefetch` | The turn-start context assembly: relevant knowledge, memory, the CHAT THREAD the turn was woken in, the seat's own OPEN ASKS to the people who triggered the turn (ADR-0124), the SYSTEMS the ask names exactly in the company's atlas (at most `MaxSystems` 8 entries and `MaxSystemsBytes` 1,200 bytes, only in a turn whose requester may read the atlas, private addresses only within the seat's reach, fenced at the least trust their writers earn), the blocks a prompt gets. FROZEN before the runner exists, which is what stops a self_iterate loop moving the system prompt underneath a turn. Nothing re-fetches mid-turn any more: the executor asks, with `search_knowledge`, over this same seam. The thread block is what a prompt telling an agent to go and read one cost: three MCP rounds before a word, and an agent that skipped them answering a pointer. It is bounded by ONE BYTE CEILING on its own measure (about thirty ordinary messages) and a third of the bound on how a conversation's earlier turns ended, which the executor is handed beside it and which both ride every request of its session: the root and the newest WHOLE messages are kept verbatim and the ones between them are CONDENSED by the seat's auxiliary model, dropped (and counted) only where no rewrite can be had; and its two failure sentences differ, because "nothing was said earlier" and "this node could not read it" send a seat to opposite places. The second covers a maintenance-mode node, an instance down at boot and a refused seat token, none of which is a chat API error. Its three AUXILIARY JUDGEMENTS (the memory filter, the knowledge-query writer, the episode summary) read the turn's ASK (`Request.Ask`, a part per trigger event, each a lead, its words and the `provenance.Label` they were read under, DATA rather than a rendering, so the engine carries the ask's merged label, `Ask.Label`, onto the turn's record of what it was asked) with somebody else's words FENCED as the trigger labels them, beside the standing sentence, under a token minted per call and never the turn's (what each answers decides what the seat is shown, and a model echoes the token it is handed), and the summary's briefing is defused under the turn's token before it enters the prompt; the vector and the recorded ask are the same text unfenced, since a random token in it would make every vector of one question differ (ADR-0040). Its block of SIMILAR PRIOR WORK renders each past turn through `learning.RenderPastTurn`, the one rendering `query_episodes` shares, so a past turn's ask is fenced under the label the episode kept: the turn's token for the block, the call's for the summary's input. EVERY BLOCK DEFUSES WHAT A STORE HANDS IT (memories, traits, skills, a past turn's account), because each is what a model wrote after reading other people's words, and a gate reads every string of `Blocks` by reflection rather than a list of blocks `Blocks.ThreadContextAnswered` names only the waking message's WRITER's waiting messages written for the same whom (`waitingMessages`, by the backend's sender id in the same read, and by the carrier as the partition keys it, `notify.WorkingForKey`, against the turn's own `Request.WorkingFor`): another writer's message, or one written for another requester, is owed a turn of its own, worked for them (ADR-0109), and a read that could not say whom its messages were written for marks none. Each is reported at the version the block showed it (`Answered.Version`, from `notify.Message.Version`), so the engine's mark of an edited message drops the wake of that edit and of no later one. |
| `agent/prompts` | The per-phase prompt text and one builder per phase, each returning its outline beside its text, plus the continuation briefs for a kept session, a send-back and a relaunch. |
| `agent/skills` | Prompt fragments admitted from the knowledge base and injected per phase, plus the load-before-use guard. The registry is keyed by the PAGE a skill was read from, not by its key: every change names a page, and a page whose key was edited no longer says what it used to hold, so a key-addressed registry could never remove the old key. Pages as the identity are also what make a one-page update end exactly where a walk of the same pages would A phase can also HAND its harness the skills it matches as the harness's own (`Offer.Hand`, recorded as an offering as a rendered catalogue is), each with the body a load of it answers; the guard then names a refused skill as that harness lists it (`Guard.HandedAs`, `Blocked.Listed`), since the session's model was never told a key. |
| `agent/skillsync` | The one loop per node that keeps the tool-skill registry current: walks, single-page reads, fleet broadcast, periodic walk and bounded retry, all on one goroutine |
| `agent/builtin` | The tools the engine itself ships to a seat and the operator, and the one authorizer that gates every one of them at registration. |
| `agent/colleague` | Resolving a free-text query to one seat or an honest list of candidates, never a guess: the lookup before an A2A ask, a chat mention or a page mention |
| `agent/turnctx` | What a turn IS, carried as an explicit argument rather than ambient state: work key, run id, holds, lookups, call log and answers seen, shared safely across its goroutines. |
| `tools` | The tool registry and the per-phase surfaces built from it: four kinds of tool under one contract, with where each came from recorded at registration. |
| `tools/argval` | How a first-party tool READS ONE ARGUMENT a model sent: ONE READER PER DECLARED TYPE, which both decides whether a value is one and returns the value read (ADR-0059) — a string takes a number or a boolean as the model wrote it, a list of strings one string or a comma-separated one, an integer and a number a value or a decimal text that spells one exactly (`Whole`, `Number`), a boolean only true or false, and a closed set its member trimmed and without regard to case (`Choice`). The surface's check of a call against its schema calls it to decide and the builtin tools' helpers call the same reader to read, so a value the check admits is read as exactly what it spells: with a reader each, a set written as `"GP-12"` passed the check as a list and the tool read no list, and `"1e3"` passed as an integer and the tool read 1. A property test holds the check and every helper to the exact value over a corpus of awkward spellings. A LEAF over the standard library |
| `tools/toolargs` | A tool call's arguments: the one decode every transport shares, and the canonical form (closed-set members spelt as the set spells them, nulls left out) every step after the entry reads. |
| `caveat` | What a landed write or a refusal tells its caller beside the outcome, worded for a model and for a person at once, with the action asked of a person (ADR-0060) |
| `modelnote` | The sentence a tool call answered a MODEL with (a refusal's, or the note on a successful call's arguments, ADR-0059), carried IN PROCESS ONLY, from the route that answered it to an in-process caller whose own model made the request: the Dashboard Assistant executing a change its person approved (ADR-0060). A route answering a person answers a refusal in the person's words, or in its code's own sentence, and never the tool's, which names arguments and tools: right for the person, and wrong for the assistant's model, the one reader that can act on it, which handed only the floor could not tell its person what to change. So the route TELLS the tool's sentence here (`Tell`: the operator surface tells a refused call's beside its answer, and a successful call's note on its arguments), and only a caller that asked for it in its own request's context (`Expect`, the assistant's in-process dispatcher) hears it. NOTHING HERE IS EVER WRITTEN TO A RESPONSE: a request from a browser or a script carries no note, so a route telling one tells nobody, and the dispatcher reads the note off the context rather than off the answer. A LEAF over the standard library |
| `mcp` | MCP client: supervises each server (a stdio child is a process tree on an allowlisted environment), files and levels its tools, and reports why a server failed. |
| `inbox` | A person's inbox as every native domain keeps one: the marks over a domain's notices, the one gesture that moves them, the classifier a page is marked by, and the movement an applier announces. |
| `mcp/oauth` | The OAuth client a remote MCP server is signed in with: PKCE S256 only, resource-bound (RFC 8707), refresh required, renewal, revocation, dynamic registration. Holds nothing; the grant is `engine`'s (ADR-0075) |
| `mcp/catalogue` | The MCP servers this build knows: the curated table, the one recognition of a configured server by address or command, and the search of a community registry |
| `a2a` | Agent-to-agent asking: one ask, one answer, then closed; the channel is an authorization record over the durable seat inbox, with a `Service` that addresses and a `Sweeper` that retires. |
| `decision` | The shape of a structured ask, shared by everywhere an agent asks a person to choose (ADR-0023, ADR-0068): `Decision` (one-sentence question, two to four options with slug ids, the one recommended and why, evidence, role, and an `inform` only a tracker ask may carry), its caps and `Decision.Validate`, every fault at once. `ValidateRecommendedAt` is what an AGENT's decision is held to (ADR-0100): the recommendation and its rationale required, because every question an agent puts to a person carries one; a person's decision and the engine's held-call approval are held to the shape alone. VALUES ONLY: a work item's decision is checked against the world by `worklet.ValidateDecision`, and an executor's ask (`sandbox.Ask`) adds its context and branch; both refuse in this package's words. HERE AND NOT IN `worklet`, because `worklet` already depends on `sandbox`, so a decision declared in the tracker could never ride a run's row; a second copy of the shape in `sandbox` would be two contracts a model meets and a person reads, free to drift. |
| `delegated` | Delegated decisions (ADR-0125): what a request that waits on a person is FOR, its `Kind` (an act the engine sets from the structured data that made the request, never from prose; `other` is never delegated) and the `Act` an engine-composed ask carries; and the values every delegate's verdict is decided by: `Combine` (a panel allows only when every delegate allowed, low confidence and disagreement escalate), the engine's closed words for an overruled allow and for a merge's or a deploy's unmet conditions (`Unmet`), the always-escalate categories and sensitive labels, the repository paths no delegate lets a merge or a deploy through on (`EscalatingPaths`, beside a rule's own globs, matched by `gitref.PathGlob`), what a merge or deploy rule adds to those conditions (`Release`: its paths and the checks it requires on a repository's branch, `RequiredChecks`, matched by `DeclaredFor`, which the GitHub reads judge as check runs that succeeded on the commit decided), `Limits` (the deadline and the two hourly caps every delegate is held to, which the company document moves within ranges, and `DefaultLimits` where it sets none) and `Sampled`; and `Subject`, the identity of what a request decides apart from its wording, by which a seat's repeat of a request still open joins it at the hold (`agent/builtin`'s `CallHold`) and makes no second one. VALUES ONLY, a leaf over `gitref` and `words`: config, the run record, the tracker, the access log and the verdict tool all read one vocabulary, so the delegate's tool (`agent/builtin`), the engine's sweep and the config validation cannot each say a different thing about what a panel or a low confidence means. |
| `worklet` | Worklet, the engine's own work tracker and `statelog`'s first domain: its records, subjects, writer, applier, reads and the routing of who hears about a change. |
| `changefeed` | Durable, fleet-wide pull consumer over a state log that derives a wake from each committed record, with replay and dead-lettering of records a node cannot read |
| `routing` | Who a committed record concerns and which of them it wakes: the one rule every native domain routes by (one reason per handle, ordered fallback chain, roster-filtered wake, delivery states). |
| `pickup` | The WORK PICKUP (ADR-0112): the one wake derived from STATE rather than from a change, because an assignment wakes its assignee once and a turn that did not act on it was the last word on the item. `Picker` is the sequence every node holding seats runs once a minute (`Tick`), over consumer-declared seams (`Holder`, `ReadyReader`, `Ledger`, `Claims`, `Publisher`) the engine fills: `Idle` over the holder's `Conditions` with a SETTLE of one pass, then Worklet's ready work PAGE BY PAGE, asking the fleet's completion ledger KEY BY KEY and stopping at the first `Key` (seat, item, the last change that WOULD HAVE WOKEN THE SEAT, status, for started work the seat's own last field change, the start it waited until, and the recheck it came back for) it does not hold, remembering a ready set found all offered by its fingerprint; the seat's cooldown claim only when the pickup passed over an item the seat left unchanged (a DECLINE), then the pickup's own claim, then a claim on the item without the seat's own progress that spaces hand-backs of started work by the same cooldown, then a `work_pickup`, a wake type of its own, whose id is derived from the key, stamped with the instant of the change it names and carrying whom the item is for (`types.WorkingFor`, ADR-0109; a requester the organisation no longer holds as a stranger) (`Event`). A failed publish gives its claims back. A RECHECK A DAY (ADR-0122): an item still ready for its holder (`worklet.ReadyItem.Quiet`, the seat's last write whatever it was, since an answer that took the item out of its ready work holds it there by itself) gains `Key.Recheck` once a whole `RecheckAfter` has passed since it went quiet, so it is new work for the ledger, and the all-offered memory expires at the first recheck due; a recheck is held until a day after the end of the item's last pickup at its version, read off the ledger's instants (`Ledger.WorkedAt`). An agent's wait whose reason names a person nobody asked is ready before its date and keyed apart (`Key.OnPerson`), so its date still hands it. A DEAD WAIT (ADR-0128): an item whose every open blocker is a pull request the engine's poll recorded nothing moving (`worklet.DeadWait`) is ready for its holder and keyed on those heads alone (`Key.Dead`), so it is handed once per head and never rechecked, under its own origin reason (`types.DeadWaitReason`) so the turn ledger counts it apart. Its turn is delivered only by a typed answer on its item (`turn.Reply.Item`, read off the seat's own rows since the turn began by `worklet.Reader.PickupAnswer`), never by a comment alone, or ends as it is where the item left the seat meanwhile. Pure but for its seams; the engine wires it in `internal/engine/pickup.go` |
| `alarmwake` | Turns a node's alarm transitions into wakes (ADR-0095) for every agent seat whose role carries `health_tools`: the notify spine's parser and prompt for the inbound source `alarms`. |
| `doclet` | Doclet, the engine's own knowledge base (containers, pages, revisions, change record) and `statelog`'s third domain; a drop-in for Confluence through the `knowledge` seam. |
| `iamdomain` | The company's identity estate: people, their logins and addresses, credentials, invitations and sessions, as `statelog`'s fourth domain over the `iam` values leaf. |
| `iam/credential` | What somebody proves themselves with: argon2id passwords, TOTP second factors, recovery codes, machine tokens, one-time password links, and the sign-in throttle. Stores verifiers, never secrets. |
| `iam/session` | The signed session cookie a browser holds, and the tables that resolve it to a person and a seat, answering 503 and never 401 on a node that is behind. |
| `iam/authevents` | The one trail authentication facts reach: verified facts publish as events, failed attempts only count per source and publish one `iam_login_failures` per closed minute. |
| `usage` | What each node's seats, schedules and people did each company day, and who read unit-private repositories: one record per node and day, replicated by `statelog` (ADR-0020), summed by the reader. |
| `gitlet` | The code domain of Gitlet, the engine's own git host: repositories, their changes, reviews and merge queue as records on the `CREWLET_CODE_LOG` state log, with the packs' bytes in the object store. |
