| `chatlet` | Chatlet, the engine's own chat and `statelog`'s ninth domain: channels, rooms, DMs, threads, messages, reactions, retention, erase and prune, with the routing snapshot a wake derives from. |
| `knowledge` | The backend-neutral seam through which turn-start knowledge prefetch and `search_knowledge` search the one team knowledge base, best effort, plus the one bound on ranked-search query text. |
| `providers/llm` | The model contract a harness implements: a conversation in, an answer and usage out, and for a seat at work the phase's tools as a `ToolHost` the harness calls natively |
| `providers/llm/chain` | The seat's model fallback: an `llm.Provider` over its entries tried in order, per call, with a per-launch `Pick` for executors and `Unready`/`KnownLimit` to answer without a call |
| `providers/llm/llmtest` | A scripted-model provider for tests that drives the tool host through its real MCP session, as a harness does |
| `providers/llm/harness` | The only model backend: drives a vendor's own harness as a process, with a seat home per call, the seat's tools served as one MCP server, and a spent sign-in benched on this node. |
| `providers/llm/harness/harnessprofile` | The harness profile table (`profiles.yaml`): what each harness accepts, the merge of an entry's `overrides` and the rules a merged profile is held to; a leaf so `config` can judge entries against it. |
| `providers/embeddings` | The vector backend: the knowledge corpus duty, each knowledge search's query vector, and diary and episode recall. It owns the FACT of what a model accepts — `Limits` in bytes of `Prepare`d text (whitespace collapsed and each stray byte a U+FFFD, as the request's JSON encoding makes it, so every bound, digest and cut is of the text the server decodes), resolved from `config.EmbeddingModels`, because every tokenizer these models use emits at most one token per byte plus a wrapper, so no tokenizer is needed — and enforces it: an input past the bound is refused before anything is sent (`ErrTooLong`), and `EmbedBatch` packs a batch into the requests the model's limits admit (`Limits.Requests`). The POLICY for a longer text is the caller's: the corpus embeds its `Opening`, a turn's ask is `EmbedWhole` — pieces cut between words, one call, a length-weighted mean of their directions. Nothing retries, because what a failure costs differs by caller; what every caller gets instead is the CLASS — `ErrRefused` (refused again unchanged), `ErrTransient`, `ErrConfiguration` — so the corpus can set aside one poison input rather than resend its batch for ever. A batch request has its own ceiling (`BatchTimeout`, a minute, a fifth of the corpus tick's progress budget) apart from a single call's fifteen seconds. The fake enforces the same limits by the same code and fails on request in the same classes. It is also the engine's one model-vendor SDK client (`openai.go`, `vendor.go`), since no harness computes embeddings: built with NO SDK RETRIES and with every `OPENAI_*` setting the SDK reads from the process environment undone (`withoutAmbientEnvironment`), so an admin key, an organization header or a custom header exported for something else never reaches the endpoint an embedder names, and with what the endpoint SAID about a refusal (`detail`) on the error, since the SDK's own text names the status alone |
| `sandbox` | The executor runtime: every agent seat's executor is its harness's own run in a box. A run is DETACHED: the launch starts it, the turn parks, and the engine resumes the same turn with the run's report, minutes later, possibly after a restart, possibly on another node. Nothing parks a goroutine on a running job. EACH COLLECTED LAUNCH IS A RECORD OF ITS OWN (`types.ExecutorCollected`, `Coordinator.publishCollected`) (its tokens, its harness's turns and price, its report and transcript) published ONCE per launch with its charge (`LaunchRecord.Published`), and NO PHASE: the work of the `execute` round that launched it, which every spend reader files it under. A run ASKS a person through `ask_and_wait` on its bridge (ADR-0068), in the shape of a `decision.Decision` (ask.go): the tool validates the whole ask, `PendingStore.RecordAsk` writes it onto the job's row only while that job is running, and the collection parks on what the row holds, never on a file in the box. A run that asks a person something frees the seat, and the answer CARRIES THE RUN ON (`LaunchRequest.Continue`): the same round relaunched, continuing the turn's kept session in whatever box it gets (below), else the asking job's own session in the box it kept where the relaunch reuses that box (`PriorBox.ContinuesIn` and `Coordinator.hostsPrior`: the same harness, the same cell, a box this node drives), or afresh from its pushed branch otherwise. HOW THE JOB'S HARNESS ENDED IS ON THE JOB'S OWN RECORD (harnessend.go, `LaunchRecord.HarnessEnd`, `PendingStore.MarkHarnessEnd`), since the copy a relaunch carrying the round on in the job's box continues is described by no capture: the read that collects a job writes `own_run` or `cut_off` by `readCapture`'s line, whether or not it takes or keeps a capture — after its take, in the room the read leaves the take's own records, which counts its crossing as the read's budget does (`Coordinator.recordsBy`, `Coordinator.takeRecordsRoom`, `Coordinator.recordContext`) — cut off once cut off for the job (`HarnessEnd.after`), and carries it back with the result for the claim it collects for (`Result.HarnessEnd`, merged in `collectClaimed`); an empty record is never read as an own run, and a read made while the run's ending is decided records nothing. AND WHAT THE COPY'S NEWEST REQUEST CARRIED, in that read's one write (`LaunchRecord.Context`, `session_context`; `MarkHarnessEnd` handed an `EndRead`, landing where the end or the measure changes) and with no box read of its own (`Result.LastRequest`, set by `Collect` after its result block): Claude Code's off the run's event stream, its newest main-thread assistant line (`parent_tool_use_id` present and null) and that line's usage read by the layer's one rule (`readUsage`), a usage that does not read, or a line past the bound or cut short, clearing the figure; OpenCode's off the session's own message list, which the run's script asks of its server once the session is exported, never on an end request, one assistant message a page by the cursor read as text anchored at the answer's end (`openCodeContext`, at most `openCodeContextPages`, 6, and `openCodeContextBytes`, 1 MiB; like EVERY call an OpenCode run's script makes to its own server, each page BOUNDED by one helper of the script's, `openCodeBounded` (`bounded`/`unbound`: the call a grandchild whose end is a status file its subshell writes, never `kill -0`; ended `SIGTERM` first and `SIGKILL` past `JobStopGrace`, and reaped; ended at an end request too where it comes before the run), one constant a kind of call (`openCodeCallWait`, 10 s, a call whose answer is a few KiB; `openCodeSessionCallWait`, 30 s, one whose answer holds the session or a message of it; each set from the slowest measured on OpenCode 2.0.32 to 2.3.0 with a box's cores kept busy), each past its bound going the way a call to a server that is gone goes, and the calls on an end request's way out held under `JobEndWait` (`openCodeEndPath`)), the first settled message that measured a prompt being the figure (`measuredPrompt`, the rule an export's meter reads by too); `harness_context_unmeasured` says why where there is none. A job that made no request of its own carries the figure along its copy's lineage and says where it came from (`MeasureSource`; `continuedLaunch.measure`, written by the attach from `BoxRef.Context`, the in-place copy's read by the reset into `PriorBox.Context`), and `collectClaimed` merges the read's onto the claim by the record's rule (`LaunchRecord.measured`). SO ARE THE READS THE JOB'S BRIEF LEFT OUT (`BriefTold`, `LaunchRecord.BriefOmitted` and, beside a brief that continues a session, `FreshOmitted`), by their places in the round's log, written with `Brief` by the attach that names the job's command only where the launch carries a round on (a send-back's round restarts its log), each at most `MaxBridgeCalls` and refused past it before the row is written, and read by the brief the job's run read (`LaunchRecord.OmittedCalls`: by `Brief`, then `SessionStart`, the union where the box never said); a capture placed by its own read states them (`SessionCapture.Omitted`, held by `Bounded`, copied with the point by `placeAtNewest`), since a session holds what its brief told and never what its read cap left out. The answer is SEALED on the run's row under the fleet keyring (`seat.run.answer`, answerseal.go), bound to the run, its question and its deliveries, and its words and answerer exist there only inside the seal: every reader opens it first, one under a key the node does not hold waits recorded and owed, and one that does not open is handed to no turn, attributed to nobody and let go of. Every HOST-SIDE ACCESS to a local box's files (read, write, directory made, file removed) goes through its home (or its records) opened as an `os.Root` (`boxLayout.openHome`), by a name relative to it, every directory on the way opened as a directory (a pipe there refused at once, never waited on), never a path checked and then opened: a directory the box made a link out of the home is refused at the access, whenever it was put there, and `hostbox.SafeJoin` only words the refusal (`directBox.explain`). A WRITE REPLACES THE FILE WHOLE (`replaceIn`, over `hostbox.ReplaceFileIn`): staged beside it under a fresh name, synced and renamed over it, so a reader in the box (the git credential helper reading the token file the poll rewrites under a running job) finds the old file or the new one, never one truncated and half refilled, and the rename writes through no link or other name at the path; only a regular file is replaced, anything else there is refused and left as the box made it, and nothing staged outlives a failed write. An E2B box's write is envd's upload under a fresh name beside the path and its filesystem `Move` over it (`envdClient.writeFile`), because envd writes an upload in place. A STREAMED write (`Sandbox.WriteStream`, for a session archive carried into a box, which grows with its session) is the same replacement fed a piece at a time and never held whole (copied into the staged file on the engine host (`replaceFrom`), and on E2B the upload's multipart body written through a pipe by a goroutine the call stops and waits for (`envdClient.uploadStream`), chunked and so sent once) and a source that fails part way leaves the file as it was; `sandboxtest.Box` holds every backend to it, and `sandboxtest.HostWrites` holds the engine host's boxes to refusing what is not a regular file for both writes. A `direct` command starts in its home and its own shell enters its working directory (`inWorkdir`), so the engine never walks into it. A resume the budget refuses is HELD (`HeldError`, `PendingRun.HeldFor`/`HeldUntil`), its refusal naming the window its relaunch was refused below its floor in (`Refusal.Window`), which the release counts onto the job's record for the turn's next relaunch, a turned-back answer's too, and keeps until that relaunch replaces the record (`Release.RelaunchRefusedIn`, `LaunchRecord.RelaunchRefusedIn`, a `BudgetWindow`: a period and a label, which compare across nodes), the row back to running, skipped by the waiter and refused by every claim (`PendingStore.ClaimForResume`, an announcement made before the hold and still queued behind the resume that held it included) until its window turns or the budget is readmitted (`PendingStore.LiftHold`), rather than abandoned. A job a collection READ BACK and handed back to running (`LaunchRecord.Collected`) is announced again by the poll with its paused box left ASLEEP (`Waiter.reach`), never woken or kept alive: the job is over, and the next collection wakes the box to read it. A completion whose resume met a MODEL'S LIMIT after its claim is held by THE NODE THAT MET IT, in its memory (`keepRefused`, refused by `stillRefused` until the limit's instant), NEVER on the row, which every node reads: the limit is that node's models', so a restart or `ReleaseSeat` forgets the hold and the seat's next holder asks its own models, and a hold kept after that release (the release waits for no resume, and the resume returns once its review has run) asks for the seat's lease once kept and is dropped where the seat is gone; written on the row, it silenced the job for every holder until the instant. A person's pause with `stop_running` ENDS the seat's launching and running runs at once (`Coordinator.StopSeat`, `stop.go`): a decided ending like any other, with WHO stopped it recorded at the top of the row in the deciding write (`PendingRun.Stop`) so the seat's next holder finishes the same stop after a crash; finished by closing the bridge session, ENDING THE JOB and reading the run's spend off its box (always by this node's own retry of the kept ending, never on the frame that decided it, whatever `conversation_session.enabled` says (`Coordinator.endRun`); on another node's host by asking that host, a silent host asked again until it answers, has been silent for the box timeout by the asking node's own clock since it first asked it, or the stop is the box timeout and twice `objstore.AssumedClockSkew` old by that clock (`Coordinator.hostGivenUp`)) and charging and publishing it ONCE (`PendingStore.CollectStop`), reclaiming the box, announcing `executor_stopped` (never `executor_failed`, which is a failure by type) and closing its turn as stopped through the engine (`CoordinatorOptions.CloseStopped`: a parked turn's close, and for EVERY stopped run the turn's end, which the ending records after its read has taken the job's session). A LOSS (an ending that names why the run was lost (`PendingRun.lost`)) hands the run to the engine as a step of the ending, after its announcement and before the delete (`CoordinatorOptions.TurnLost`), which answers the colleague whose ask the lost turn took up: no frame of that turn is left to, and best effort, because the turn is over whatever the asker hears. It is NOT the seat fence: a seat that moves leaves its run running; a stop leaves resumed and parked runs alone (`Stoppable`), and a job a collection already read and handed back to its held or retried resume, which is its turn's (`LaunchRecord.Collected`; the store refuses a stop of it) and whose WAIT the stop ends instead, the row's hold lifted and a completion this node kept re-fired (`Coordinator.endCollectedWait`), the engine's gate admitting a stopped seat's completion past its budget and its models' limit (`Engine.seatStopping`), a resumed turn whose call meets a limit or the budget as the stop lands settled as stopped rather than reverted to wait (`Engine.resumeWaits`), and a keep the stop raced re-fired rather than held (`stopLanded`, signalled by the stop), and a launch asks its turn's fence again once its row exists (`LaunchRequest.Fence`); a stop decided on a launching run is kept for the frame launching it (`Coordinator.LaunchStopped`), which closes its turn from it rather than from the pause — and leaves the turn's end to the run's ending, after the stop's read. Two real backends behind one contract (a remote VM per run (E2B, cloud or self-hosted) and the engine host itself) held as a CATALOGUE keyed on `Placement`, because `providers.sandbox` configures which boxes exist and the `providers.llm` entry a launch picks places its run with its `run_in` (else `default_run_in`). The row carries a person's NOTES to the run's turn (`RunSteer`, `notes.go`): filed by any node, sealed by the engine, each WAITING until the write that hands it over records it read, the launch for a brief (`PendingRun.BriefNotes`, taking over a lost take's read where its brief names that take, `RunNote.LostTake`, at that take's place), a take pinned to the job, its statuses, an undecided ending and its lease (`TakeNotes`), or a hook's SERVE (`ServeHook`), read only once its job's session shows it (`SettleHook`), every such write numbering itself among the record's reads (`RunSteer.Reads`, `NoteRead.Order`), the order the turn read them in, every take naming itself on its reads (`NoteRead.Take`) so one whose call answered an error is SETTLED by that name (`SettleTake`): its reads answered, marked UNANSWERED where its reader never had them (`NoteRead.Unanswered`, read by the record's word and held by no capture), and the record written even where the take recorded nothing, in either mode, so a write of it still in flight never lands after, and nothing answered where an attempt lost its race to the record's removal, and a note a poll serves a harness marked SERVED to the job before the poll answers it (`RunNote.Served`, `ServeNotes`), kept once the note is read, bounded by the caller's figures (except the first segment's box's own, filed waiting by the write that creates the record, `PendingRun.HandedNotes`, which the box already bounded and answered accepted), watched by the node serving the run's bridge (`WatchNotes`), kept by every reset and park, a marked note a launch's brief tells again recorded read by the job that launch opens, told again, at the marked take's own place among the reads, the take's read kept beside the telling (`PendingRun.RetoldNotes`, `NoteRead.Retold`), and reported by the ending before its delete as the record knows it (`CoordinatorOptions.SteersEnded`), every note no step reported reading (`PendingRun.EndedNotes`), in this order: delivered where a brief told it again and its job started (`NoteRead.Retold`), unconfirmed where a job's harness was served it and never confirmed (`RunNote.Served`), missed where a take whose answer was lost recorded it read and no later launch whose job started told it (`NoteRead.Unanswered`, or the take's read a telling whose job never started kept), and expired where it still waits or was read first by the brief of a job that never started, which the launch says it wrote the moment it did (`LaunchRequest.Recorded`), STARTED being the job's own record (`LaunchRecord.Started`, written by the attach that names its command), never the row's command, which a park's teardown and an expired pause clear from a job that ran. EVERY Claude Code run is started with a settings file of its own that HOLDS ITS BOX'S RETENTION SWEEP OFF (`executor`, `claudeSettings`, `Paths.ClaudeSettings`, `--settings` on every release: `cleanupPeriodDays` at `claudeRetentionDays`, a hundred years, since the box's harness sweeps its configuration directory five seconds into every run by each file's own mtime, a carried session keeps the mtimes it was packed with and is as old as its conversation, and the harness takes no 0). A run that starts a hook of the engine's has those settings with `disableAllHooks` written FALSE in that file's place (`claudeHooksOnSettings`), so a repository's `true` cannot switch it off: a run handed skills, from the release that runs their plugin's hook (`Paths.ClaudeSkillsSettings`, `claudeSkillsGate`, 2.1.118, far above 1.0.68, the first release whose schema knows the key, since a file holding a key its release does not know is ignored whole), and one wired to its notes. A Claude Code run READS its notes between its steps through HOOKS in a settings file of their own, those settings again with its hooks (`claudeNotesSettings`, `Paths.ClaudeNotesSettings`): two `mcp_tool` hooks calling the bridge's unlisted `steer_take` after each batch of the main agent's calls and at Stop, `disableAllHooks` written FALSE so a repository's `true` cannot switch them off, no field newer than the `mcp_tool` hook (print mode ignores a file it cannot validate), passed only to a release at or after 2.1.163, an older one starting on the first file (`claudeNotesFloor`, read off `claude --version` first: an older release drops what the Stop hook answers), and with them the run's standing instructions TOLD OF NOTES (`Paths.ClaudeNotesSystem`), an older release is handed them as they stand (`Paths.System`), so the gate (`claudeNotesGate`) chooses the system text with the hooks, its brief on STDIN rather than argv, `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE` and `CLAUDE_CODE_SAFE_MODE` unset by the wrapper (`Output.Unset`), the one place that reaches each, `CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE` beside them (`claudeHostedOff`: it starts the harness in the mode of Anthropic's hosted environment, which no box is, whose resume gives a record whose parent the transcript does not hold an earlier record as its parent, by timestamp; unset on every run, so a relaunch runs as its job ran), and a `hook_stopped` terminal reason read as a FAILURE rather than an empty success. An OpenCode run's CONFIGURATION is written beside its other artefacts (`Paths.OpenCodeConfig`), never into the checkout, and handed to its server alone as `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT`, which OpenCode ranks above a repository's own `opencode.json` and `.opencode/` (`OPENCODE_CONFIG` would rank below them), so the repository's file is left as it is and loads beneath it; every entry the run owns states `disabled: false`, and every MCP server value (url, header, environment) is a `{file:}` reference to a file of its own under `Paths.OpenCodeValues`, a directory the clear removes whole, so the content holds paths and no value; a home that text cannot name (a brace, which opens or ends a `{env:}`/`{file:}` reference OpenCode replaces before it parses, a quote, a backslash, a control character, or a relative path) is refused before anything is written. An OpenCode run READS its notes at its next step through the engine's own PLUGIN (`opencode_steer.js`, embedded, written into `Paths.OpenCodeSteerPlugin` before the server starts and named by the configuration with the notes route as a `{file:}` reference): it polls the bridge's notes route only while the run's own session (named to the server alone as `CREWLET_STEER_SESSION`) is executing, admits each note as a steer with `resume: false` under its job-derived inbox id (`OpenCodeInboxID`), moves its cursor only past an admitted note, confirms only what OpenCode reports delivered, backs off from one second to thirty, never polls again sooner than a second after a poll that admitted nothing, stops for good on a refused token or a missing route, and writes nothing; once the run has ended its script CANCELS every steer the plugin admitted and OpenCode never delivered (`openCodeLeftoverSteers`), because the session's inbox outlives the server and would hand a continued run the note its brief carries too; and the run's answer is its LAST message's text, never every step's joined. The standing instruction about notes, and the engine's account of a late call (`lateCallHead`), is its harness's own (`noteReader.NoteInstruction`: `ClaudeCodeSteerInstruction` names the hook-context headings Claude Code puts a note under, `OpenCodeSteerInstruction` a user message), ADDED BY THE HARNESS rather than the runner (`withNoteInstruction`), only to a run wired to them that its harness's reader is started for, and a run that succeeded without once asking (`RunSteer.Pulled`) is said at its collection (`steer_reader_never_ran`), only where the runner says its harness reads notes and the record names a seat. A LATE CALL is filed, unsealed, for the job working on its round by the append or opening that marks it (`fileLateItems`); `FileNote` refuses one. A run's ROW AND ITS LOG ARE EACH HELD TO A BYTE BUDGET (rowbytes.go): each is one value in the coordination store, held to `RowBytes` — what one key holds, `coord.MaxValueBytes`, the largest whose leader read crosses a leaf's link, which the store refuses a write past — split into shares that close, in the bytes the store's encoder writes, escapes counted (`kept`). AND A WRITE OF ONE IS GIVEN ITS CROSSING BESIDE ITS CALLER'S REASON (`RowBudget`): every write sends the value whole, and the store keeps a caller's deadline as it is, so a write the engine bounds for a reason of its own — a resumed turn's carry and a session start's record, five seconds; a capture's stamp, thirty for every attempt; a teardown's writes of the row, the thirty-second grace (`Coordinator.detachedRecord`, never the box call's `detached`), and each call an ending makes of the store on the row its own, since an ending runs with no deadline of its own (`detachedEnding`, `Coordinator.endingCall`); the waiters' writes of the row, their task's or their duty's bound (`Waiter.rowContext`) — is bounded by that reason and what the store says a change of a row at its bound takes to cross this node's connection, its read's reply and its write (`PendingStore.RowCrossing`, thirteen minutes on a link at the floor, none in process), and a capture another node keeps, its stamp of what came of it included, and a read standing by for one, count the slowest node's (`SessionCaptureBudget`, `standbyBudget`). Once the coordinator stops, every such write is held to its reason alone (`Coordinator.rowContext`), and everything its own stopped work makes on a context of its own — a retry's writes, stamps and box calls, one after another — to ONE grace from the stop, carried on its lifetime as a value every detachment keeps (`detach`, the one detachment the package's steps make), so no write still crossing, nor a retry's teardowns one after another, holds a node's stop past the grace it is given. The LOG's calls have theirs (`BridgeLogBytes`: two hundred calls, each held to `MaxBridgeCallBytes` with its largest argument values left out and its output cut past their shares, the newest successful submission kept whole up to `MaxSubmissionArgsBytes` for the collection's replay — chosen as the collection chooses, never a late one, of two at one place the one taken last — and the calls the log counts without listing kept as IDENTITIES, `ElidedCall` — the middle dropped past the count (`BridgeLog.ElidedCalls`), and a bridged call's own work, a `delegate`'s workers (`AbsorbedCalls`) — each folded by tool and then into one count past its bound, and both READ THROUGH `Unlisted`/`Absorbed`, never off the fields: a count no identity accounts for, a build without them having dropped the calls, is read as calls of no known tool; the round's, a new round taking only the absorbed ones its collection did not read), beside the turn's earlier rounds' tool names (`MaxPriorCalls`) and the room a later build adds keys to the log in. The ROW's are the parked state (`ExecuteStateBytes`, condensed by the park and refused past it, `ErrStateTooLarge`) and the rest (`RestBytes`, every field held by the write that puts it there: a run's titles to `MaxRunTitleBytes` by the write that creates the row, a recorded answer and its hand-back to `MaxAnswerBytes` as the row will hold them, the claim fields at their widest, `ErrAnswerTooLarge`, a chat reply past it recorded as its question's REFUSAL instead (ADR-0114, below), and an answer by turn held to a page, `MaxAnswerTextBytes`, by `answer_executor` and again by the record on ONE STRING, the answer trimmed and before any secret in it is redacted, so one the tool took is never refused after it for a redaction marker, a held call's sealed arguments to `MaxHeldArgsBytes`, `ErrHeldCallTooLarge`, a note's seal to `MaxNoteSealedBytes`, a question's seats to `MaxAudienceHandles`, a capture stamp to the executor log's bounds, `ErrCaptureUnbounded`, a change's title an executor asks to open to `MaxChangeTitleBytes`, the call a person approved kept as the log keeps it (`approvedCall`), the turn's read of its conversation to `ConversationBaseMaxBytes` in a place of its own, and `ExtraRowBytes` stated for the fields a later build adds); every call is kept again on every append, so a call an older build recorded whole is held to its share too; an append whose session could not tell whether it landed is carried WHOLE by the session's later appends (`BridgeAppend.Owed`, `CarriedCall`) and listed once, the log knowing a call it holds by its place, instant, name and identity together (`BridgeLog.lists`), so an append asked again lands once; EVERY CALL'S ENTRY IS ON THE LOG BEFORE IT RUNS (`BridgeLog.InFlight`, `AppendBegin`), its one update taking it out and listing the call in the same write (`AppendRecord`, found by its place, its instant and its tool, `inFlightAt`), at most `MaxCallsInFlight` of them, each its identity and the id its harness gave it (`keptEntry`), a call in flight counted by `LastSeq` and making the fold of a round incomplete (`foldPriorCalls`); EVERY COLLECTION WAITS FOR ITS ROUND'S CALLS IN FLIGHT before it reads the log (`drain.go`, `drainCalls`: a completion's, a park's, the cap's, a lost job's carried on, and an ending's before it decides the turn's end), ending the job's session first and waiting on the run's log through a watch of its own key (`PendingStore.WatchLog`), never the bridge's node-local registry, so whichever node collects waits alike, up to the first of the call's update, the turn's cap, the call's own bound (`BridgeCall.Until`) and its runner's taker lease held at no epoch, read as the runner first appears and every two seconds (`runnerGone`, `callsDrainTick`), a call still in flight then named (`sandbox_calls_unfinished`) and read as one whose outcome is unknown; an ending that may not wait hands a call still waited for to its retry (`drainEnding`, `errAskAway`), a seat's retirement leaves such a run, its session ended, to its next tick rather than to that retry, which the seat's release drops, and answers it as WAITING beside its error, never inside it (`RetireSeat`, `SeatRunsWaiting`: a wait is no failure, so the mailbox duty keeps the mailbox, says it at info and fails no tick for it, while a failure beside it still fails the tick), and the delete names what is still in flight (`sandbox_run_deleted_with_calls_in_flight`); and `TestTheWholeRowFitsItsBudget` and `TestTheWholeLogFitsItsBudget` build every field at its bound, held to every field the row and the log carry by `TestTheWorstRowSetsEveryField` and `TestTheWorstLogSetsEveryField` (a walk of the type that fails on any field either model leaves zero and does not name with why, `zeroInTheWorstRow`, `zeroInTheWorstLog`), with the row's two measured figures PINNED (`measuredOtherFields`, `measuredSpare`, compared exactly), so a field the row gains is measured and its table restated in the change that adds it. Each call carries its IDENTITY (`BridgeCall.Digest`, the digest its run's call log admitted it under), which a resume seeds by — but for a call held for approval and the one approved (`BridgeCall.Withheld`), whose arguments ride sealed and whose digest neither the log nor the row ever holds in the clear. And the ID ITS HARNESS GAVE IT, where its box sent one (`BridgeCall.CallID`, `call_id`): the box's word, on the call's entry and on its one update alike, held whole or not at all (`ValidCallID`, at most `MaxBridgeCallIDBytes`, the log holding every writer to it again as it keeps a call or an entry, `keptCallID`, so an id past it is none and never cut) and NEVER PART OF ITS IDENTITY — `lists` and `inFlightAt` never read it, so two calls under one id are two calls — and none on the call a person approved (`approvedCall`); read under the key the runner of the job's box declares (`CallIdentifier`, `Manager.CallIDMeta`), which the job carries (`OpenedJob.CallIDMeta`, named by the launch from the runner it starts the job on), the executor's runner answering it from its harness's built-in profile (`executor.Runner.CallIDMeta`: Claude Code's `claudecode/toolUseId`, none for OpenCode), and a fake's from `FakeRunner.CallIDKey`. Every write to a run's ROW is a decision handed to the coordination store's read-decide-write (`coord.SandboxRuns.MutateSandboxRun`, coordstore.go), which takes it again on a lost race, within the bound every fleet key shares (`coord/kv`'s `casRetry`): one row takes every tool call its box makes over the bridge, from as many as twenty subagents at once, beside its seat's own writes, and that bound — twice the writers a fleet key is sized for, each attempt after a loss waiting a window of round trips — outlasts their overlap, where an immediate retry a fixed few times reported that busy row as one that "kept changing" and dropped the write. The coordinator's own loops over OUTCOMES (a seat's recount, an answer's record, an ending's rounds, a let-go, a launch's create-or-reset) each carry a named bound with its reason. A run's placement is on its ROW: the turn that collects it may be another process on another node, and reconnecting to a remote box through the local backend reports a box that vanished. A `direct` or `container` box is its LAUNCHING NODE'S (`PendingRun.LaunchNode`, boxhost.go, ADR-0065), because no other node can reach it: there are TWO WAITERS (`Drives`) (the fleet's, under the duty, for the boxes every node reaches, and every node's own for the boxes on its host, which also reclaims a box no run names any more off the run each box records (`Waiter.sweep`, `HostBox`)) and a seat's holder that is not the box's host asks the host to read it back over a signed channel (`BoxReader`, `sandbox.box_read`, its remaining wait carried as a duration (`BudgetMS`); a busy host or one this node could not ask is a silent one (`ErrBoxHostBusy`, `ErrBoxNotAsked`); the host reads only what the row licenses, `Coordinator.ReadHostedBox`), leaves the box's reclaim to its host and tells it (`Nudge`), and relaunches in a fresh box of its own; a relaunch's row keeps the previous job's box with its host, and none of that job's command or session, until it attaches a box of its own (`BoxRef.Node`). A launch node away for the box timeout, watched within one stint of the duty (`Waiter.forgetAbsences`), is found gone by the fleet's waiter, which records its running jobs lost with it (`PendingRun.Lost`, `launch_node_gone`), never a node merely restarting inside that bound. A JOB LOST WITH ITS HOST IS CARRIED ON, NEVER COLLECTED (`lost.go`): a `direct` job dies with the restart that replaces its engine (its container, its systemd unit's control group, its host) leaving what a crash leaves, so every local box records its job's WITNESS, the engine process that last saw it running by pid and kernel start time (`local_witness.go`), and a job that is not running ENDED while that process is still there and was LOST once it is gone too (`JobState`, `Sandbox.Job`, the runner's `Poll`); a `container` box asks its runtime's listing whether the container is up before it asks the job, so a daemon that is away is no answer rather than an ended job. The waiter that drives the box records the loss (`engine_restarted`, `box_gone` for a box its backend says is gone after the give-up window, `launch_node_gone`) on the row before announcing the completion, and the seat's holder reads what the job spent where its box is left (asked again as a collection is, left behind past the give-up window) charges and publishes it once, noted cut off, and resumes the turn with `ResumeRequest.Lost` to relaunch the same round (`PendingRun.Continues`), at most `MaxRoundLosses` (2) times a round (`PendingRun.RoundLosses`, counted by the relaunch that carries a lost job's round on); a lost job that had asked is parked on its question. A run whose `submit_work` or `ask_and_wait` lands over its bridge is polled at once and on a short schedule after (`Waiter.Nudge`); the 15 s poll is the fallback. The closed set `run_in` accepts is asserted against the switch that builds them. A run row carries its turn's CODE HOLDS (`PendingRun.CodeHolds`, at most `MaxCodeHolds`): `BeginLaunch` UNIONS the launching turn's set with what an earlier launch of the same turn wrote, `AddCodeHolds` adds to a live row only (a mutate, refusing past the bound with `ErrCodeHoldsFull`), and `ListRuns` lists every row whatever its status, because Gitlet's expiry must count every unended run, a status a newer build added included. `ShellQuote` is THE ONE QUOTING of a value written into a command a box's shell reads — the harness's invocations and artefact paths (`executor`), the engine's reads of a run's workspace and the clone commands a checkout answers alike — because each kept a private copy of the same two lines, and a copy is a second chance to disagree about a quote (ADR-0008). A repository on Gitlet is a GRANT, never task context: `Spec.Repos` (`RunRepo`, at most `MaxRunRepos`, held by `RunRepo.ValidateFor` the purpose it is given for — an executor's on its branch, a check's one commit on none and read only, a value a purpose does not read refused rather than ignored — whose cone grammar `ConePathProblem` is the one a repository's `.crewlet/checks.yaml` names its check's cone in too, so a file never names a cone a box refuses at setup) is added to the job's row one checkout at a time by `PendingStore.GrantRepo` (`GrantAdded`; `GrantHeld` for the same repository on the same branch and access, answered again for a box that lost its clone, which the engine clones at the branch's head where it exists; a conflict otherwise), never cloned at launch — the checkout answers the box `CodingSteps` to run itself — and a launch opens with none but a round carried on, which `BeginLaunch` opens with its job's grants and floor, the rest REPLACED by a relaunch — a job's grants are its own, unlike the holds — and `RaiseCodeFloor` raises the floor only on the launch that holds the row, the caller judging which floor is higher because this package reads no log (`CodeFloor`, `CodeLaunch` and the colleague seat the launching turn worked for, `CodeCeiling`, are carried as tokens: `sandbox` links neither `gitlet` nor `statelog` nor the configuration, and seals an answer through the leaf `recordseal` alone, `TestTheSandboxLinksNoGitletConfigurationOrStateLog`); and whom that turn works for, as its checkout composed it, `CodeWorkingFor`, which the box's pushes and the collection's open carry and by which a credential's box is held to the seat's own work: ONE REQUESTER A JOB, set by its first grant and replaced by a relaunch with the rest, a later grant made for anybody else a conflict, and one for the same credential holding fewer apps narrowing it, never widening it (`GrantRepo`, `sameRequester`). A run's bridged tool log (`BridgeLog`) is kept BESIDE its row, never on it, bounded at `MaxBridgeCalls` with the middle elided: every flip, every listing and every git request of a coding box reads the row whole, and a full log on it was moved by each of them (`internal/coord/kv`'s `BenchmarkABoxRequestOnItsRun`). The log holds the condition its appends are taken on (`BridgeLog.Launch`, the job it takes calls from, and `BridgeLog.Round`), so `AppendBridgeCall` is one compare-and-set of the log and reads no row, deciding once what it takes an append for (`BridgeLog.take`): its job's own, its spend then written to the row; a LATE call of the round — its job replaced by a relaunch that carried the round on — listed, marked (`BridgeCall.Late`), its spend nobody's and answered so (`ErrJobReplaced`, beside recorded), what it committed folded into the round's union of what its calls committed as every call's is (`BridgeLog.Committed`, read with the listed calls' own by `BridgeLog.Changes`, so the turn's outcome counts it listed or dropped), given up by the middle's drop only once no ordinary call is left and counted when it is (`BridgeLog.LateElided`), and never the call a person approved, which the store appends for its own round; or a record of an earlier round, late and with no place (`takeEarlier`). A call NOBODY WAITED FOR by the time it returned — its harness had cancelled it, or its job's session on the bridge had ended — is marked so by the bridge (`BridgeCall.Abandoned`) and kept as handed, its spend its job's, what it committed counted as a late call's is, held by no save point as a late call is and kept and counted by the middle's drop as one is (`BridgeCall.unheld`); and an opening marks late, or carries into a new round, every call the log took after the read its relaunch's briefs were built from (`PendingRun.BriefRead`, transient, off the launch's `LogRead`), but the call a person approved, counting late any of them its middle dropped since (`BridgeLog.lateAfter`); `BeginLaunch` opens it to the new job after the row's reset, in the one write that decides what of the round the job keeps and where a person's answer cut it (`AnsweredThrough`, on the log), and a NEW ROUND's opening folds the earlier round's calls into the log in that same compare-and-set (`BridgeLog.PriorCalls`, `PriorCallsElided`), so every reader of a run's calls reads that one log; `BridgeLog` reads a job's log and answers no calls for a log that is not the job's; and the run's end removes both through the store. `CloneRecipe` is THE ONE WAY a box is given such a repository (an executor's blobless clone of the default branch ALONE — a repository's other branches are mostly other seats' work, which a clone of every branch would send to every box and a default refspec pull into it on each fetch — its base fetched by id wherever it is; a check's single commit, fetched by id at depth one, blobless, in a container cell as in an e2b one — no box is given a Gitlet node's copy to read in place, a borrow crewlet/crewlet#496 would measure before any box takes one), after `GitVersionStep` holds the box's git to the client floor; git's FILE-SYSTEM MONITOR is THE CELL'S to give, never a clone's: `CodingSteps` — the version check, each coding clone, and `MonitorStep` after them — gives the monitor's step only in a cell whose boxes own their kernel (`Placement.OwnsKernel`: `e2b`), since every `direct` or `container` box spends the engine user's inotify watches and instances, a rootless runtime's user namespace included (the kernel charges a namespace's watches to the user that made it too), beside the other seats' harnesses; the step turns the monitor on in each checkout's OWN configuration where the box's git is 2.55.0 or newer and EVERY checkout at the top of the box — an earlier job's in a reused box too — takes at most HALF of the watches (the least of the host's and the namespace's) and of the instances, else off with any daemon stopped, never a failure: the half is headroom for a tree that grows past its clone, since a daemon refused a watch dies and every later command starts it again, slower than no monitor (`BenchmarkBoxWorktree`'s fsmonitor=starved); measured in as the index settings are (a warm status at 100 000 files 62 ms against 142), and never through the environment, which would start a daemon for every repository the box's git touches; the steps ride `LaunchRequest.Clone`, apart from the operator's setup, because a REUSED box is given them alone, each skipping a checkout it holds. A BOX WITH NO HARNESS is a check's (`Manager.AcquireBox`): `Acquire` less the runner's install, applying exactly the steps it is handed and never `providers.sandbox.setup` (coding boxes' provisioning, often credentials, which a change's own code would read), and refusing a harness, a login, a run's grants, the catalogue's default cell and `direct` before any box exists.  `LaunchRequest.RunEnv` is asked once the store has named the job, and the launch's environment reaches every setup step — the telemetry endpoint alone is minted at the job's start, its token's span running from its minting. A box is KEPT ALIVE while the engine provisions it (`keepProvisioning`: its timer refreshed at once and every `provisionRefresh`, from its creation or a reused box's reconnect until its steps are done, and stopped before any teardown), because the waiter's keepalive reaches only running rows that name a box, and the install, the operator's steps and the clones run one after another, each to its own timeout — a sum past the timer the box was made with, which had the provider reap it mid-step. The coding clone writes NO HOOK: a run's commits are given their `Crewlet-*` trailers by the attribution hook below, the one hook that stands in for the repository's own while the run works, because a second one written into the clone's `.git/hooks` is skipped by every repository that sets `core.hooksPath` (husky's) and would write `Crewlet-Run` a second time with another value. A box's git on Gitlet is ENVIRONMENT, never a file in the box nor an operator's setup step (ADR-0093): `BoxGit.Env` hands the launch its run's token and base (`GitTokenVar`, `GitURLVar` — the launching node's `BoxGitURLVar`, read through `ParseBoxURL` like the other two addresses a box dials) and git's configuration through the environment, the operator's own `GIT_CONFIG_*` entries KEPT and seven of the engine's appended after them (five for a check's box) — an empty `credential.<base>/.helper` that resets every helper configured before it for that URL, a helper answering git's `get` with the token read from the process environment when git asks, so `store` keeps nothing, the seat's identity — none for a CHECK'S box, which acts as nobody, a name without an email or the reverse refused — protocol v2, and index version 4 with the untracked cache, MEASURED IN by the rule a box-side setting is adopted by (`internal/gitlet/host`'s `BenchmarkBoxWorktree`: a 30 % cut of a warm status at 100 000 files, the checkout 1.5 % longer) — refusing an operator's count git would refuse, since every clone would fail on it. What a run PUSHED to Gitlet is the PUSH RECORD's account, never the run's (`Result.Pushed`): a collection of a run given a repository there to push to reads it through `RunPushes` — declared here by its consumer, REQUIRED by `NewCoordinator`, a coordinator without it handing every such run back silent, which reads as "nothing pushed" — and the collected executor's record says each ref as it ended (built from the RUN, whose grants decide whether it says "nothing"), the run's own report having been told not to name those pushes (`RunRequest.ReportedRepos`). A record that cannot be read is SAID (`PushedUnread`) and never retried, because a collection held until Gitlet answers holds a finished run's seat for as long as the host is down — except a read the collecting node itself cancelled, which is the collection interrupted. The refs share `MaxDeliveredRefBytes` with the run's own, the list a PREFIX in the order the run first pushed each, so `MaxPushedRefs` (that bound over the shortest line a ref renders to) is every candidate a capped reader must hold. An executor may WORK ON A CHANGE (`RunRepo.Change`: its source branch, from its head) and ASK TO OPEN ONE (`RunRepo.Open`, its target resolved at launch): the collection asks the engine through the same interface (`RunPushes.Changes`) to name each pushed branch by the live change it heads (`PushedRef.Change`, on the phase record too) and — for a run that FINISHED alone, succeeded and asked nothing — to open each asked change, once a launch (`Result.Opened`), never at a box's first push, which would put work in progress in front of its reviewers; a run parked on a question is recloned with its ask, and the changes unread hold nothing back. A run re-seeded in a fresh box after its answer is relaunched with the same grants, each repository on the branch it worked on, because the default branch need not be the one its work was pushed to. A run's bridge session is opened once its JOB is named (`LaunchRequest.Opened`) and before any box exists, so a launch the bridge cannot serve is refused with nothing started, and it numbers the job's calls after the ones the run's log keeps (`OpenedJob.LastSeq`), so a job carrying on one that asked a person goes on in the round's order rather than starting again at one beside the asking job's calls; and the coordinator tells the engine of every run it recovers still RUNNING (`CoordinatorOptions.Recovered`), which re-opens that job's session on this node with the surface rebuilt from the run's row, a reopen that cannot be made logs `bridge_not_reopened` and never fails the recovery. A Direct box may be CONFINED (`LocalOptions.Confine`): every command its job starts runs under sandbox-exec, bubblewrap or Landlock (the engine binary re-executed as `sandbox-confine`), kept from the paths its caller names, every other box and its own records, and refused outright on a host that can enforce none (ADR-0054). WHERE A LOCAL BOX LIVES is one rule (`BoxRoot`): the company's `providers.sandbox.local.state_dir`, else `DefaultBoxRoot` (a seat's boxes under its `boxes/`, and the Dashboard Assistant's confined deep-work boxes, which the engine makes under `assistant/` beside them, a directory no seat's backend lists, reattaches or reaps) and never the store's directory, which is the volume holding the store and the stream. Every process a local box runs goes BEFORE ITS ENGINE when memory runs out (`oom.go`, Linux): raised to the engine's own `oom_score_adj` plus the kernel's whole scale of 1000, held to its ceiling (`boxOOMScoreAdj`), because that is the one lead at which the kernel takes the box first whatever the two hold, and relative because the engine's value is its deployment's decision, a `direct` box by its first process, which raises itself and then execs the command, AHEAD OF ANY CONFINEMENT (Landlock leaves a box nothing to write outside its home) and never by a write from the engine after the start, which would race the box's first fork (`oomFirst`); a `container` box by the runtime's `--oom-score-adj`, which both runtimes give every exec, spliced before `run_args` so an operator's own wins. A seat's executor box on the `direct` cell is NOT confined: its run is the seat's fully permissioned harness as the engine's own user on the engine host, which is why a shared host places its seats' runs in `container` or `e2b`. An address the engine hands a box (the bridge, the telemetry receiver, an HTTP MCP server, the run's notes route) is written in the ENGINE HOST'S network, so a box with a network of its own (a `container` box) is handed a loopback host as its runtime's name for the engine host (`EngineAddress`, enginehost.go, applied in the one runner `Start` and in the telemetry merge); a `direct` box is handed it as written, and so is a value an operator declared for the box itself. The bridge's address is the engine's choice per CELL (`mcpbridge.Bridge.Reaches`): a `direct` box dials its launching node's own loopback listener where that node has one, and every other box (a `container` one included) dials `CREWLET_MCP_BRIDGE_URL`, which reaches a `container` box under that name when it names loopback. The telemetry receiver's is chosen by the same rule (`OtelReceiver.Reaches`, from the spec's cell in the telemetry merge): its host address is taken ONLY WHERE THE NODE FORWARDS (`OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` set), since what reaches a node with no upstream is dropped, and a box no address reaches is handed no endpoint at all. Each job's record names the sign-in its harness was handed (`LaunchRecord.SignInID`) and why it follows the job before it (`FollowUp`), and a cell's readiness is probed with the backend's own functions (`probe.go`), never by making a box. A run's COMMITS CREDIT CREWLET THROUGH GIT, never through an instruction a model may forget (`executor`, attribution.go): where its request asks (`RunRequest.Attribution`, which every launch path takes from `Manager.CommitAttribution`, on unless `providers.sandbox.commit_attribution` is false, naming a seat's run by its turn and deep work by nothing), the runner writes one hook script into the box, links it under every hook name git runs, and the wrapper appends `core.hooksPath` to the run's `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` entries after the profile is sourced, which outranks every git configuration file without writing one; the script hands each hook on to the repository's own (its `core.hooksPath` included), and prepare-commit-msg adds `Co-authored-by: Crewlet <noreply@crewlet.ai>` and, for a seat's run, `Crewlet-Seat`, `Crewlet-Run` (its turn, the identity its page and a push's record name it by) and `Crewlet-Work-Item` (`none` for no item, written rather than left out) only to a commit whose author is the identity the run commits as, so a person's commit the run amends, cherry-picks or rebases is never credited; each Crewlet trailer is REPLACED rather than added, because a rewrite by another run must leave one run named, and the co-author line added only where it is not already there. No other Go file of the binary spells a trailer key (TestNothingButTheAttributionSpellsATrailerKey), so nothing in the engine can come to trust one. A run is CHARGED WHAT IT SPENT, never its session's spend, which both harnesses report on a run that continues a session: Claude Code's per-run `model_usage_since_last_result` summed over the run's results (2.1.310+; read a model at a time, `eachKey`, for at most `claudeResultModelsMax`, 64, models: a result naming more, or writing the figures as anything but an object, null among it, is read for none, and the run is charged as one whose release states none, never by a sum that leaves some of its calls out), else its result less the one the job before it left in the box (`Paths.BaseResult`, kept at `Start` before the clear, where the run continued its session and the release recorded in `Paths.SessionRecord` restores the session's totals, 2.1.277+); OpenCode's session report after the run less the one its script read before it (`crewlet.usage_before`). A run's HARNESS SESSION IS NAMED BEFORE THE HARNESS STARTS (`executor.Harness.Session`, returned as `RunHandle.SessionID`) and RECORDED by its script (`Paths.SessionRecord`: the session the harness ran, whether it CONTINUED the one it was asked to, and the harness's `--version`, `Result.SessionID`, `Result.Continued`, `Result.HarnessVersion`; and, for an OpenCode run that continues its session, WHERE THE RUN BEGAN IN IT, the newest message the session held before the run, asked of the run's own server before the run as a page of its message list newest first and recorded by that answer's cursor as `from` before the release (`openCodeRunStart`), read whole or not at all (`runStartOf`: a `next` cursor of a descending list, exactly its three keys, a `ValidMessageID`) into `SessionArchive.RunStart`, which the take keeps spelled as the kept export spells that message's id or drops (`keptRunStart`, `keptString`, the rewrite every string of a kept document passes) and the capture's stamp carries (`SessionCapture.RunBegan`/`RunAfter`, `RunStart`); a session the box neither holds nor can restore is never resumed, the run starts the one minted in its place (`Session.Fresh`) and says why, on its error stream and in its record (`Result.FellBack`), since Claude Code fails a run on a session it does not hold and OpenCode creates an empty one under the id. The record is a file in the box, so it is READ WHERE IT LIES, a line at a time and no further than its four keys (`parseSessionRecord`: split into its lines, a record of empty lines cost sixteen times its size on every collection and every take), and every piece of it is held where it is used: the reason to one sentence where the run's result is built, redacted and cut to `sandbox.MaxFellBackBytes` (1 KiB, `sandbox.BoundFellBack`, and again as the launch's record is published), an id to what a harness mints (`sandbox.ValidSessionID`, which the harness's own reported session is held to as well), and a release to a harness's MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH in at most the 64 bytes the executor log records one at (`releaseOf`, `sandbox.ValidRelease`) — the two rules the launch's record holds the id and the release to again as it is published, trusting no runner or host of another build to have held them. Claude Code's session layout is PINNED by the wrapper over whatever set it (`Harness.Pins`, `claudeSessionLayout`: `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` the box home's `.claude`, `CLAUDE_CODE_PROJECT_DIR_NAME` `crewlet`, auto memory off, the conversation compacted at the window of the run's model, `ClaudeCodeWindow`, with a fallback for a model this build does not know that the launch logs, and the variables that switch compaction off or move the point it compacts at unset, `claudeCompactionOff`; from 2.1.310 the run's settings hold the same window and blank the two that move the point, and the two switches a resume is started without, over a repository's own settings `env`, through each settings file's pinned copy, `claudeSettingsPin` and `claudePinGate`), a run is started with `--session-id` or `--resume` and never both, a resumed one with `--system-prompt-snapshot off` from 2.1.267 (`claudeSnapshotFloor`) so it reads THIS launch's standing instructions, and without the switches that move what the resume hands its model (`claudeResumeSwitches`: the interrupted turn run again before the brief, a transcript past 5 MiB read by another rule), and the transcript's trim is on from 2.1.304 and unset before. Once the harness exits a run PACKS its session whatever the exit, and says whether the harness exited cleanly (`Result.HarnessCutOff`: a session packed after a harness killed while its wrapper lived on may end mid-record) (`Paths.SessionCapture`, `Result.Captured`, streamed by `Runner.OpenCapture`): Claude Code's transcript, sidecars and task list as a gzipped tar (`claudeCapture`), OpenCode's `session export` through its own server, written to a file and gzipped from there (`openCodeCapture`), NEVER PIPED, since 2.0.32 and 2.1.4 stop writing into a pipe after a 64 KiB multiple and exit 0 (so every answer of OpenCode's the run's or a pack's script reads goes to a file, a rule a test holds every rendered script to), never a login, the harness's own state, Claude Code's file-edit snapshots (`file-history/`) or OpenCode's database, which holds the login. A run handed a session packed in another box (`RunRequest.Carried`, refused unless its harness, format and session are the run's: `refuseCarried`) has it written in with `WriteStream` after the clear and restored by its script: Claude Code's three paths unpacked under the pinned layout, and on every resume, restored or held in place, the transcript and EVERY SUBAGENT'S THREAD under its sidecar directory, at any depth, cut back to its last whole line, a file with no whole line left as it is (`claudeSessionGate`'s `torn`: a thread a pack read as its subagent appended ends mid-line, and the harness's next record to it would run into the torn line and be lost; a thread with no whole line 2.1.310 continues from nothing a restore could make of it, measured — a fragment, an emptied file and a removed one alike are no transcript of the agent to it — so nothing is ever written onto its fragment; a box without `find` cuts no thread and says so), OpenCode's export imported with `session import --standalone --directory "$PWD"` before its server starts and required to say it imported that session, which also starts its instruction state over. A box that already holds the session is compared BY ID AND BY THE ARCHIVE'S DIGEST (`Paths.SessionDigests`: the POSIX `cksum` of every archive of a session the box packed or restored, kept for the box's life): its copy is never written over with an archive it holds, which it may have run past after a capture that failed — unless the archive was taken of a session its harness was cut off in (`Session.Replaces`, from `CarriedSession.Replace`), which replaces the copy whatever it holds — and is replaced by one it does not, from a box the session went on in, Claude Code's files removed once the archive proves to hold a transcript, OpenCode's copy deleted with `session delete --standalone` once the export unpacks — and a restore is CONTINUED ONLY ONCE ITS DIGEST IS RECORDED, the line written and read back once the archive is restored (`recordDigest`), a run whose box cannot keep it starting a session of its own and saying why (`restoreDigestUnrecorded`), since a copy continued with no line of its archive was later restored over by that archive carried in again. Whether a box can continue one is its harness's release, read in the box by one login-shell exec (`Runner.CheckCarry`, `carrier.Continues`): Claude Code 2.1.310 and a writer of 2.1.300, OpenCode 2.0.32, never a box older than the writer, never across harnesses. A run can be handed SKILLS OF ITS OWN (`RunRequest.Skills`), which its harness lists and loads with its own skill tool, each listed as `crewlet:<name>` on both harnesses (`SkillNamespace`, `Skill.Listed`) so the engine can name the skill to load without knowing the harness: a Claude Code run takes them as a PLUGIN of its own (`executor`, `--plugin-dir`, `Paths.ClaudeSkillsPlugin`) whose `hooks/hooks.json` holds one PostToolUse `mcp_tool` hook on the Skill tool calling the bridge's unlisted `skill_loaded` with `${tool_input.skill}`, on a release at or after 2.1.118, the first that runs an `mcp_tool` hook, an older one refuses a run with a REQUIRED skill, whose covered tools it could never call, and starts any other without its skills; an OpenCode run takes them as a directory its configuration's `skills` names, a directory per skill named as it is listed, and a second embedded plugin (`opencode_skills.js`) reports each completed `skill` load to the bridge's skill route from an awaited `execute.after` hook. Its body is the harness's own skill: Claude Code reads it as a template (`` !`command` ``, `$ARGUMENTS`), OpenCode as written. A run handed skills its harness cannot take (`skillHolder`), with nowhere to report a load (`SkillLoads`), or under a name outside the Agent Skills grammar (`ValidSkillName`) is refused before anything reaches its box. EACH LAUNCH'S HARNESS SESSION IS KEPT (`session.go`, ADR-0073): taken out of its box on the box's host, before the box is paused or reclaimed — at a completion's collection, a stop's read and a loss's ending (`readBox`, `keepLostSession`) — through the runner's `SessionTaker` (the script's archive, or one packed now), sealed and uploaded (`SessionKeep`, the engine's), and recorded on the executor log through `SessionWrites`, the ESTATE ROUTER's writes, never a data node's own, under the operation its turn, launch and place derive, asked again under it for `sessionRecordBudget`. `SessionWrites` speaks THE SANDBOX'S OWN WORDS (`BeginRecord`, the turn's first read of its conversation, answered as a `BeginAnswer` that says whether the row holds its base; `CaptureRecord`, carrying that first read where no begin landed; and `EndRecord`; each answered as a `LogAnswer`: landed, passed over, refused or unsettled), and the engine's `executorRecords` states each as the log's record and reads the log's answer back, because the sandbox links nothing of Gitlet, no configuration and no state log (`TestTheSandboxLinksNoGitletConfigurationOrStateLog`); what both must agree on byte for byte, a turn's ending and a session's bounds and sealed shapes, is `executorkeep`'s. Each launch is numbered in the write that opens it (`PendingRun.LaunchSeq`), and each capture reserves its place on the row and is stamped there landed, refused or unresolved (`PendingStore.StampCapture`, `PendingRun.Capture`/`Kept`: the place only grows) — a read whose place another read of the same job holds, its reservation refused, or unanswered and the place found another's (`abandonPlace`), or that capture found begun — a loss's read too, which passes over only a final capture that has SETTLED (`keptWhole`) — STANDING BY rather than down (`Coordinator.standBy`): named on that capture's stamp (`PendingStore.StandBy`, `SessionCapture.StandbyAt`, carried by every later stamp of the same take, `stampOnto`) before it spools, waited for by every capture wait (`captureInFlight`; the end's for its window and its takeover's life from first sight, `awaitEndCaptures`), keeping nothing where that capture lands and its own in the next place where it does not (`Coordinator.takeOver`, `PendingStore.TakeOver`), waiting for it on its own clock for that capture's whole life (`standbyWait`), never by its stamped instant, and then deciding on one more read of the row: still begun or refused, it takes the place after it; unresolved, it stands down (`Coordinator.waitedOut`) — one whose record went unanswered asked again first (`settleCapture`), taken over only once the log refuses it — and withdrawn where it cannot keep what it took or its taker is gone (`PendingStore.StandDown`, `standDownGone`), a standby or a takeover the store never answered settled rather than left on the row (`standBy`, `tookOver`), and a read of the job ended beside a capture taken as the job stood taking the place after it instead (`besideAnother`), so a session either read took whole is kept wherever either could have kept it — the stamp naming how far the run's bridged log had reached: its SAVE POINT, how many of the round's calls the log had taken (`Through`, `bridge_through`, a pointer since none taken is a save point), every call the log takes numbered in its round by the one append that takes it (`BridgeCall.Appended`, `BridgeLog.Appends`), so a call is after the save exactly where its number is past it, whatever its place among the calls the run made and however much middle the bound dropped below it — but a late call and one nobody waited for, which no save point holds — with the arrival places an older build reads (`BridgeSeq`, `BridgeOpen`) written off the same read and read by nothing here, since the bound's dropped places fill `BridgeOpen`'s slots and hide a call in flight below them. Read from the log before the take, and only for a capture a read places: a log that cannot be read refuses it, since a session whose place cannot be stated is not kept, and a kept capture stating no save point (an older build's) is not continued (`followedCapture`). The TAKE OF A JOB STILL WORKING (`readCapture`'s not final: one its box would not end) and every capture of a job CUT OFF (`readCapture`'s `cutOff`: a harness killed, crashed or told to stop, or a loss's read, which cannot say how its job went) have no such read to be placed by — only a job whose harness ended its own run, its script packing it, is placed by its read, made once the job is over and so after that pack — since Claude Code makes a call, and can have it answered, before it writes the call to its transcript (2.1.310, measured), so one killed while a message streamed never wrote calls it made, and OpenCode's export leaves out a step that has not settled: each is RESERVED AT ZERO and raised once its take's count names the session's newest compaction (savepoint.go, `SessionCapture.placeAtNewest`) to the save point of a capture its launch's session holds whose newest compaction is that one, its launch's own newest landed capture (`launchKept`) or the one it continued (`LaunchRecord.Continued`), the places an older build reads left zero; it reads no log, so a log the store cannot read refuses none of them, and a refusal of one states no save point. A SAVE of a session its job still works in is placed the same way. A capture is TWO HALVES: the TAKE (`Coordinator.takeCapture`), all that happens before the box is paused or reclaimed — packed where it must be, scrubbed, sealed into a spool on the node's own disk (`SessionKeep.Seal`, `crewlet-session-*` in `CoordinatorOptions.SpoolDir`, removed with its keep, and swept by every take once past the longest a keep may still wait to open it and an hour for a step of the machine's clock, `spoolOpenedWithin`) within `SessionTakeBudget` — and the KEEP (`Coordinator.keepCapture`), its two reads of the coordination store each held to a room of its own (`keepReadRoom`, a listing past it deciding nothing), the upload and the record, off the read from the moment its take is spooled (`readBox` starts it there, never behind a collection's harness-end record, its pause or the pause's record, which is held to its own room, `Coordinator.markBoxPaused`), on the coordinator's own lifetime, which `Stop` drains. A STOPPED OR LOST JOB, AND ONE ITS TURN'S CAP PASSED WHILE IT STILL WORKED, IS ENDED BEFORE ITS SESSION IS TAKEN (`endjob.go`, `Coordinator.endJob`; the cap's in the collection's own read, `readBox`, since the cap is an end, so no pack is made beside a live harness, or an OpenCode export beside a live server, but of a job a kill could not reach): its read asks the job's script to end it (the runner's optional `JobEnder` reads the pid the script recorded and the backend signals it, `Sandbox.EndJob`: SIGUSR1, never SIGTERM, which is a teardown's), waits for it at most `JobEndWait` (135 s: the pack's two minutes and three `JobStopGrace`s), and then kills what is left (`Sandbox.KillJob`: TERM, a grace, KILL, a grace — a direct box's recorded group, a container's job group, recorded at its start or read off its first process where an exec's own session leads it, never `kill -1` there and never its first process alone, which is the runner's wrapper rather than the job, so a job whose group neither names is signalled nothing (`ErrNoJobGroup`), an E2B box's every process of its user), at once for a script that answers no request; so a stop's, a loss's and a capped collection's capture is FINAL wherever the job ended, the script's archive or one packed now (`SessionTake.Pack` false), and only a job a kill could not reach is taken NOT FINAL; a capped run still reads as cut off at its cap (`endedAtDeadline`) wherever it was SEEN working past it — a poll that answered running, or its script hearing the request to end (`endJob`'s `working`), RECORDED on the job's record the moment it is seen and before anything is asked or killed (`LaunchRecord.EndedAtDeadline`, `PendingStore.MarkEndedAtDeadline`), which every later collection of the job reads, since a read cut short as it ends the job kills it and the kill can take the script before its own record; or the script's own record that a request ended it (`Result.JobEnd`), which only the cap asks of a collected job — never off whether its job is over after its end, and a job that finished by itself before its cap's read is neither ended nor cut off; and whether it ran out of context is judged on the run as its collection records it (`asCollected`: no question, no success). A loss closes the run's bridge session before its read, as a stop and the cap's collection do. A cross-node box read waits by what it may do (`BoxReadBudget(run, read)`: a collection of a turn with no cap about 4,700 s, for the take, its reservation's room, a teardown's grace for each of the take's two records and one for the pause (`Coordinator.takeRecordsRoom`, `Coordinator.pauseRoom`), and the crossing of each of the four changes of the run's row its host makes, at the slowest node's pace (`boxReadChanges`, `coord.MaxValueCrossing`); a stop's and a loss's about 4,893 s, the job's end on top (`JobEndBudget`), and every collection of a turn with a cap about 5,974 s, the end on top and the record that the cap ended a job it saw working (`recordsTheCap`), since the host decides on its own clock whether the cap has passed; a Claude Code collection 36 s more for its main thread's read (`MainThreadReadWait`), a read holding a hook's serve that, its verdict's 10 s and a crossing (`asksReceipts`); its host's share holding the take's whole `SessionTakeBudget` and its reservation's room after an end and its record at their bounds), a stop's capture and a loss's (`BoxReadLoss`, asked of the box's host) are handed to the ending's retry rather than spent on the frame that decided them, and a pack that fails is a refusal, never "no session". A capture is PREPARED only where the node's own revision keeps sessions (`Coordinator.capturing`, `SessionKeep.Keeps`) — a launch's seal, a loss's read of another host's box — and KEPT only where the fleet's does (`SessionKeep.Policy`). The scrub's values are what the LAUNCH HANDED the box FROM A CREDENTIAL POSITION (`HandedSecrets`, handed.go: what the engine names in `LaunchRequest.Secrets` — every value a reference resolved to and every credential-named variable, the entry's sign-in among them — its MCP servers' env and headers and their url's references (`MCPServer.Secrets`), the run's telemetry token, the seeded login; never the seat's identity facts, a setting or a setup step's own words), collected from the launch request and SEALED under the fleet keyring in a part BESIDE the row (`handed`, up to 64 KiB sealed: what a job can make large moves beside the record every box request reads whole), named for its job and written once the attach that names the job has landed, never over a later job's set, read by a capture through `PendingStore.Handed`, removed with the record and swept with the run's other parts, CARRIED FORWARD by a job that continues in the same box (`PriorBox.Handed`), the login read again in the box and on the host at the take, bounded (`maxScrubBytes`); a run without them refuses the capture. A TOKEN THE POLL RENEWS IN A RUNNING BOX IS RECORDED IN THAT SET BEFORE IT IS WRITTEN (`RefreshFunc`'s recorder: the waiter's `Record`, the engine's `Coordinator.RecordHanded` over `PendingStore.AddHanded`, a read of the row that finds the job running undecided and then one compare-and-swap of the part; at a kept box's launch, sealed with the job, since the job's set is written only with its command), a token the store does not take NOT WRITTEN on that poll: in `Values` itself, where an older build's scrub reads it, each value whole and by every string a JSON document of it holds (`secretValues`, so an AWS session's token printed bare goes, the launch's own values split the same way, a JSON-valued secret's settings with them, since a document's credential is told from its settings by nothing but its members' names, but for the constants a Google service-account key's format fixes, `serviceAccountConstant`), its lapse beside it by a digest (`Expires`), dropped `handedLapse` (an hour) past it; a set its renewals take past the bound, or one this node cannot open or seal again, is MARKED (`handedSet.Incomplete`, an empty seal an older build refuses as this one does, never replaced by the job's own late write) and the token written all the same, every later capture of the job refused (`ErrHandedIncomplete`); the part and the set it seals each carry a later build's keys through a renewal's write (their `Extra`, wirekeys.go), since every polling node reads, changes and writes both back. Every turn's END is OWED ON THE ROW (`PendingStore.OweEnd`, `PendingRun.OwedEnd`) by whatever decides it — the engine's resumed turn, or the coordinator's endings no frame reaches (a loss, any stop: `TurnLost`, `CloseStopped`), each handed the run's bridged log as its ending read it, once (`RunCalls`): a log the store could not answer is a delivery NOT KNOWN (`DeliveryUnknown`), never a turn that did not reply — and recorded by the run's ending (`Coordinator.publishEnd` → `recordEnd`) once the turn's captures have settled, with its newest unkept capture (`unkeptOf`) and whether the turn went on past the session the log's row names (`wentOnPast`: the run's record does not hold its last word — the final capture of its last launch whose job ran (`lastWord`) — kept, so every unkept capture rides it, and the row keeps whatever session it names, a capture the log took after a settle refused it among them, and says so), naming that last word where it was the turn's newest capture and not kept (`lastWordOf`), which every reader holds the row's place to, before the row's delete, which refuses while an end is owed (`ErrEndOwed`) — by the ending's own retry, armed at once, wherever the ending was decided on a frame somebody waits on (a completion's resume, a stop, a seat's recovery: `errAskAway`), since a capture still being kept and the log's write after it are not that frame's to wait on; a seat's retirement, which holds the lease and a budget of its own, decides and records on its own frame. So a resumed turn's row outlives its resume by the settling of the turn's captures (each capture the end sees bounded by its whole life from the moment the end first sees it, `captureLife`, every step of a take or a takeover counted at the slowest node's crossing, and one more read of the run's row once that has passed, which decides, an hour and twenty-five and a half minutes at the bounds, and a read standing by by its window and its takeover's life, `standbyWindow`, each with that read, about four hours and sixteen and a half in all, each on the end's own clock, `awaitEndCaptures`, while every node reads a stamp's age within its one-clock bound and twice `objstore.AssumedClockSkew`, since its instant is another machine's clock's, `SessionCaptureBudget`, `standbyBudget`; and for a capture whose TAKER is gone by its lease's TTL and one read of the log: every process holds a taker lease of its own (`coord.ClassTaker`, `taker.go`), claimed as the coordinator is built for the seat lease TTL and never past `coord.MaxDutyTTL`, renewed `coord.KeptRenewals` times a TTL so one unanswered renewal costs no keep its window, held through a drain and released by `Stop` only once the last keep has ended, through a lease store the engine makes a step of its stop's one allowance; every stamp of a take names it (`SessionCapture.Taker` and `TakerEpoch`, read again right before the place is reserved, the holder refused past `maxIncarnationBytes` at a stamp and at `NewCoordinator`, which also refuses a holder without a store or a store without a holder), and a take with no lease held is refused untaken (`takerUnheld`); every wait for a capture (`awaitCapture`, the end's and a relaunch's) asks whether a begun capture's lease has lapsed or moved to another epoch — one `coord.Get`, an unreachable store never gone (`takerGone`) — and settles it from the turn's row at `RelaunchRead`, landed where the row names its place and refused where it does not, written only over the begun stamp it was decided from (`settleGone`, `PendingStore.SettleBegun`), so a stamp the taker wrote meanwhile stands; each settle a wait decides (that one, a gone standby's withdrawal, the stamp of what an ask of an unanswered record settled) is this node's own write, given its own room past the wait rather than cut at its end, kept on the wait's copy where the row cannot be read back after it, and an ask that leaves the record unanswered stamps nothing (`settleContext`, `standDownOnto`, `settleCapture`); and a keep is FENCED before its upload and before every record attempt by its own renewals (`takerLease.fence`, `coord.ExclusiveThrough`), each attempt cut at the window's end, uploading and recording nothing once its lease no longer covers it and stamping the capture refused, or unresolved where a record was already sent (`fenceKeep`, through `finishCapture`) — so no attempt outlives the window, and a draining taker's capture, whose lease outlives its presence, is never settled under it.
