A record the taker had already handed on can still land after a settle read the row: NO REFUSAL GOES TO THE LOG for it, since one would have the applier refuse that late capture and lose a session, so a late record the log takes before the turn's end is kept and read as any other — the end names it unkept at the row's own place, which releases nothing, and every account of an end tells an unkept capture only where it is newer than the row's place (`executordomain.Session.Unkept`, through the one shown form, `Session.Redacted`), and where it was the turn's last word names it so (`lastWordOf`), so the row that holds it reads as all its turn did rather than as one the turn went on past (`TestALastWordTheLogTookAfterItsSettleIsReadAsTheTurnsLastWord`) — and one decision alone goes by the stamp: the relaunch that follows the settled job continues only what the run's record keeps, so every attempt at it starts without the capture — the sessions read, and the relaunch's own reason, which reads the turn's row once for a capture its record refused (`continuedWithout`), saying the log keeps it (`namedThoughRefused`), and its run told what the row names is there to read (`ContinuedSession.KeptTurn`) — as one that will not wait out a slow keep does (`relaunchCaptureWait`): in the base the turn froze, where it has kept nothing and its row still held that base when the relaunch read it, else in a session of its own or its job's box's; a keep still under way past that wait is no kept capture and goes the same way (`TestACaptureRecordedAfterItsSettleIsKeptThroughTheTurnsEnd` drives the end through the shipped applier, `executordomain/executordomaintest`; `TestARelaunchStartsWithoutACaptureItsRecordSettledRefused`, `TestARelaunchOfATurnThatFrozeNoBaseReadsItsRowForACaptureItsRecordRefused` and `TestARelaunchThatWillNotWaitOutAKeepContinuesTheBaseTheTurnStillHolds` the relaunch). Presence answers whether a node may take new work, and a drain gives it up at once while its keeps upload: it is not what says a process still keeps what it began) and the end's write, the seat held until it goes: its ending kept for the retry (`oweEndingAfter`), a recovery that finds the ending decided and another attempt on the node finishing it keeping it too (`endRun`), and the two recounts of the seat that run at once then — the resume's frame's and the retry's — ordered by the seat's moves, so whichever is written second reads the store again first (`syncSeat`, `moved`). A recovery reads each run it fences again, and finishes an ending the seat's last holder decided between its listing and the fence, and takes over no run gone by then (`fenceRun`); an ending's wait for a capture takes a read that fails for no answer, never recording the end off a row it could not read (`awaitCapture`); and a relaunch asks the capture of the job it follows again where its record was never answered, as the end does, before it decides (`settleUnresolved`, `ContinuedCapture`). DECIDED ONCE, and marked once recorded (`PendingRun.EndRecorded`); ONE ATTEMPT AT A TIME finishes a turn's ending on a node (`finishOnce`); an ending decided while the turn's RESUMED FRAME runs on the node leaves the turn's end to that frame (`framing`, `errAwaitsFrame`); a seat's retirement finishes its endings itself (`RetireSeat`, mayAsk); an end a newer build owed is deferred, never re-encoded (`publishEnd`); and a frame that finds no row left records through `EndTurn`, which owes the end on the row where one is there after all. The runner's TAKE (`executor/take.go`, `scrub.go`) is the session's way out: `Runner.TakeSession` streams the script's archive or one `PackCapture` packs now with the harness's own pack (`carrier.Pack`, so every pack keeps its digest — recorded and read back before its archive is moved, so a pack reports its archive made only once the box holds that line (`packDigest`) — staged in a `Paths.SessionPackStaging` named for that pack alone, which the clear removes by its pattern, so two reads of one job at once — a stop's and a collection's — never clear each other's files or move each other's archive; every such pack, of a job that is over or still working, moved to its own `Paths.SessionPackHeld`, its read's alone, removed once that read is done (`dropPack`, within the take's own deadline, `releaseContext`, so a box that stops answering once the archive is read holds the read no longer than its take's time), and NEVER over `Paths.SessionCapture`, which only the job's own script writes, once, as its harness exits — so a Claude Code take, which opens its archive again by its path for its second pass, never meets another read's archive or the end pack's moved in between, and a read of a job that left its archive streams one nothing moves over; its exit says a session there was nothing of, `packNothing` — for OpenCode only on its own not-found answer (`openCodeNotHeld`) — from one that could not be packed, `packFailed`, which is an error, carrying `packDetailLimit` of the pack's last word), or a SAVE as `Runner.PackSave` packed it while the job still worked (`SessionTake.Save`, the path that save was packed to; packed by nothing in the take, so the scrub's values are read after the pack: every executor harness a `saver`, OpenCode's exporting beside its run's server; `Paths.SessionSave`, a place of that save's own named after a digest of its taker, `saveTag`, which its take alone streams and removes, so two takers of one box never share a file and two saves of one taker at once each hand their take the archive they made, where over the taker's one path a take was refused whenever the other's move fell between its passes, each save staged in a `Paths.SessionSaveStaging` named for it alone, so two saves of one taker at once never clear each other's lists, and NEVER `Paths.SessionCapture`, which every read of a job that is over takes as its final archive; a save that fails leaves nothing; every `session-save*` file goes in the clear before every run), refused past the take's `MaxBytes` before a byte is read, SCRUBBED file by file as what it is — every secret the engine put in the box by value, raw and JSON-escaped, a PEM armour line left for the shapes (`redact.Unarmoured`), then `redact.Secrets`: a transcript line string by string, a document a token at a time with every key rewritten and a string or a number under a credential's key masked (`credentialKey`, the key's words read with its separators dropped, so `aws_session_token`, `SessionToken` and `sessionToken` are the session token's one key and AWS's `X-Amz-Security-Token` header is read by its own words, the security token's, and an ID token's `id` only as a word of its own, `keyReading`, since `paid_tokens` spells it, and a Docker config's `auth` only as the whole key, since an `author` is none), a `.json` file that is not one value as text, a loaded tool definition whose name the scrub put a marker in cut out of its record's line as written, every other byte and the keys' order kept (`definitionCut`: Claude Code reads a session file past 5 MiB by where each line's keys sit, 2.1.310 taking a record for a subagent's only where `"isSidechain":true` is in the line's first 256 bytes and the prune under `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_PRECOMPACT_SKIP` finding a record only by its line beginning `{"parentUuid":`, and a record written again from a map, its keys sorted, can fail both), text as it streams — a tar read twice so each member's rewritten size is known before its header, its members' names scrubbed by value and by shape, its headers stripped to kind, name, mode, size and time, and anything but a file or a directory left out; WITHIN BOUNDS whatever the box wrote (`scrubBounds`: the plaintext of one reading; `jsonscan.MaxDepth`: a JSON value's depth), a session past them refused; and `Keep` records the scrubbed archive's POSIX `cksum`, computed in Go, as one the box holds, and a meter beside the scrub reads what the session's last request carried (`measure.go`, `TakenSession.Context`; an OpenCode export's messages read as the transcript reads them, as `openCodeMessage`, each value the measure reads decoded by its exact keys and held to the one rule its message list is read by, `measuredPrompt`), which the capture records (`SessionCapture.ContextTokens`) for the engine's launch floor. EVERY PACK BESIDE A HARNESS THAT MAY STILL WORK — a Claude Code pack on demand of a job still working (`SessionTake.Pack`, handed on as `carrier.Pack`'s `working`), and every save — is held to `claudeHeldTar`'s rule rather than the run's own end's, which a pack on demand of a job that is over keeps, its harness gone, which takes GNU tar's exit 1 wherever "changed as we read it" stands: Claude Code rewrites task files, a subagent's metadata and its own transcript (a trim's rename, a withdrawn message's rewrite in place, grown back), GNU tar pads a member that shrank with NULs and busybox's cuts its archive inside one and says nothing of growth, so the pack asks the box for its tools (`heldTools`, naming one it lacks), sets EVERY FILE OF THE SESSION STAMPED IN THE BOX'S FUTURE to the box's present before its marker, leaving every earlier time as it is (find's `-newer` against a reference it writes, `touch -c`, never through a link, which `touch` would follow out of the session: a box cannot hold a file from its own future, which every check would read as written until the box's clock passed it, whether a box whose clock ran ahead stamped it, the session carried here with its times, or this box did before its clock stepped back, before a resume or after one, so the pack sets it, the one step that reads a session file's time; TO THE PRESENT, NEVER AN EARLIER TIME, so a file it sets reads as no older than any write made before it was set, and no write ends up behind a marker, this pack's or that of another pack of the session running beside it — two takers' saves, a save and the take of a job still working, or two takes of one job at once — where the reference's time would let a walk that ran late set a file the harness rewrote under the other pack's tar back behind that pack's marker, kept torn; what one pack's walk sets after another's marker costs that other a pack, never a torn file; a `find` comparing whole seconds, as busybox's does, leaves one within the reference's own second, which it never reads as newer than the marker either), writes a marker, waits until the box's own `find` reads a file written after it as newer (busybox compares whole seconds; a box whose `find` never does is refused after `heldClockWaits` sleeps of a tenth of a second rather than packed blind, and one whose `sleep` takes no tenth is refused saying so — every wait the engine's scripts make sleeps a fraction of a second at a time), lists every file's inode and size, refuses before the tar a session past what the scrub reads (`heldPlainMax`), and after the tar, whatever its exit 0 or 1 said, refuses the pack naming the check and the file — an archive that does not list back (busybox's cut one) or lists no file, gone, replaced, shrunk, missing from the archive, written (a file that is no `.jsonl`, find's `-newer` held to files and to members), the session's own transcript or another `.jsonl` written since the marker that is not a byte prefix of the live file, read back out of the archive as a stream, never onto the disk of a box whose harness still writes, and LAST a member gone, which a file removed while the checks ran is — and no check rests on a tool's word that what it wrote landed (every list and answer read through a pipe, each `find`'s own exit held, written by the shell and read back by its `cksum`, `keep`), so a check that cannot be made (a list that cannot be made or kept, a comparison `grep` cannot make, its exit 2 or an exit 0 naming nothing) refuses as a difference would, never read as nothing having changed; the session's own transcript is read back on every pack, whatever any list says, since Claude Code rewrites it in place (a withdrawn message) and a clock stepped back as the pack ran could stamp that rewrite before the marker, and of the rest only a `.jsonl` written since the marker (a subagent's thread, which Claude Code only appends to), each read back a decompression of the whole archive, which `sandbox.SessionPackWait`'s first minute covers for some ten; that narrowing rests on the box's clock as WRITTEN does, and a clock that steps back while a pack runs costs at most a thread appended to as it was packed and missed, kept with a partial last line the resume's cut removes, and a file that is no transcript written as it was packed, rewritten in place or still being written, kept torn or cut short. A KEPT SESSION READS BACK AS ITS TRANSCRIPT through the same knowledge that packs it (`executor.ReadTranscript`, transcript.go, a method of the `carrier` interface beside its format): Claude Code's transcript a record a line, then each subagent's sidecar under its own `thread`, through one buffer reset per member; OpenCode's export a message at a time, the decoder holding about one message (`heldValue`), each message read by the kind OpenCode 2 gives it in `type` (`openCodeMessage`, the ONE type a reader of an export takes, never 1.x's `info.role` and `parts`, which no 2.x message has), every record read by its keys exactly as written and loosely by type, its message, its blocks and an OpenCode step's items as much as the record (a build before `TranscriptVersion` 3 matched a key by case), and every list it holds an element at a time through the layer's walk, never decoded whole first, entries of eight kinds (`EntryKinds`), the harness's bookkeeping and the model's thinking none (OpenCode's own messages among the bookkeeping: its notices in a person's turn, an instruction update, a skill or a shell command a person ran, an execution's end, a switched agent, model or directory), a NOTE or a LATE CALL known by the heading the engine opens each with at a line's start (Claude Code's own records are searched for it, not read by a shape a release can move; OpenCode files one as a person's message), and a part of the record it could not read an `unread` entry where it was: a line past a bound, a line or a message of no shape it knows, an OpenCode message of a kind it does not know, a transcript that is no plain file (a link). Which entries there are is `TranscriptVersion`'s, raised whenever an ordinal moves, so a page counted by one build is never continued by another. A read is ONE PAGE from an ordinal, read again from the archive's start every time, BOUNDED (`TranscriptReadBounds`: 200 entries and 1 MiB a page, 16 KiB an entry, a line to the stream's own 32 MiB, 256 MiB of the archive as it inflates (eight archives at their stored bound, so an archive a box packed to inflate stops there) and 256 MiB APART of what a tar's members hand out, since a sparse member hands out holes the archive does not hold), stopping where its context is done and saying why it ends (`TranscriptCut`), every entry's text REDACTED BEFORE IT IS CUT and handed out as AN OPENING OF WHAT ITS WHOLE TEXT REDACTS TO, what can reach the cut and a private key's length past it, redacted inside as much again and kept only as far as that redaction and its own agree, so a redaction that shrinks what comes first (a key's block a marker) never pulls the window's unsettled end into what is handed out (`redactedWithin`), rather than the whole of a line it hands 16 KiB of; every entry STATING THE SIZE OF WHAT ITS WHOLE TEXT REDACTS TO wherever the read redacted all of it, and of a longer text its size as recorded rounded up to a KiB (`TranscriptEntry.Bytes`, `statedSize`), never the size as recorded to the byte, which beside a text shown whole said how long each value the redaction took out was; a failure of the source (its open, or a read of it (`sourceReader`)) is returned as itself, never as malformed, and an archive that read whole and is not its format is a `MalformedArchiveError`, which says so in this package's own sentences and never in a decoder's, which would quote what a box packed. A CLAUDE CODE THREAD READ FOR WHAT IT DECIDES, rather than shown to a person (by the reader that confirms a hook's note reached the model, by the one that tells a relaunch which of its calls a kept session holds, and by the one that bounds what a continued session's harness counts before its first request), is read through ONE LAYER (`executor`, claudethread.go): a member named EXACTLY (`claudeMainThread`, `claudeSubagentThread`, `claudeSubagentMeta`, `claudePrecompact` (a session's saved compaction, one kind under both names Claude Code reads it by), and `claudeMemberOf` back, a subagent's id held to one rule both ways), never by `claudeThread`, which takes any session's `projects/crewlet/<x>.jsonl` for the main thread; a line at a time in ONE scanner pass that never holds it (`threadReader`), its facts within `threadLineMax` and each value read whole within `threadValueMax`, the last piece with no break never a line, and a member or a line past a bound the end of the reading, never a skip, the member's bound an error even where it falls right after a line break, where `io.LimitReader` would end cleanly; EVERY line read to its end before anything reads it, a late fault beating its head, with no keyword prefilter, every value read as the harness's own JSON.parse reads it (`threadDecoding`'s grammar: every key EXACTLY as written, never matched by case as encoding/json matches a struct's fields, a key written twice by its last value, and a string's invalid UTF-8 or lone surrogate as the replacement character, never refused, since Claude Code writes a lone surrogate where it cuts a text inside an emoji, and the options a run's event stream, its result and a person's transcript read every record by too), and `parentUuid` handed over as written, because ABSENT IS NOT NULL (the harness's bookkeeping carries none, and only a root carries null); a list read AN ELEMENT AT A TIME and a value no reader reads passed over unheld, in the one pass; outside the layer, a list a box wrote walked an element at a time (`eachElement`: a run's event stream and a person's transcript), and an object whose keys are open, as a map's are, A KEY AT A TIME (`eachKey`), never decoded into a map of every key first, its name handed as bytes in a buffer the walk reuses so a key costs no allocation, and its value with where it sits (a result's spend model by model); a key only one reader reads held to its type in that reader's accessor, so a release that reshapes it costs that reader alone; and A HOOK'S FIRING IS NEVER A CALL'S ID: no id leaves the layer as a call's but through `Message().Calls()`, an attachment's `toolUseID` leaves it only as a key (`AttachmentToolUseID()`), since a PostToolUse hook's firing carries the id of the call it followed, and a task notification's `<tool-use-id>` leaves it as the leg its text claims (`Message().Notification()`), which its one reader matches only against legs whose call it read. WHAT CLAUDE CODE'S RESUME HANDS ITS MODEL of such a thread, which records and in what order, is modelled once (claudechain.go, `threadChain`), from both releases' shipped code (2.1.310 and 2.1.322, a set and never a range: `claudeResumeRead`), per variant of the release and the flags a reading cannot see, with three answers (handed, not handed, cannot tell) and the reason for each; its type doc is the model, and a thread holding a shape no model holds is cannot tell whole. The take's context meter (`measure.go`) reads the main thread through that layer too, in one walk of its lines (`walkThread`), and decides what a continuation of the capture is told by what Claude Code counts the session at (`resumedReading`), and reads the launch floor's figure (`contextReading`), the last request's input, off the last main-thread reply's usage through that layer, by the one rule the count reads a reply's usage by (`threadLine.Usage`), a line the layer refuses and a reply whose usage it cannot read counting nothing. A PROCESS DECODES AT MOST `TranscriptReadsAtOnce` (2) TRANSCRIPTS AT ONCE, whichever socket or REST caller asks: one read can hold a line at its 32 MiB bound, as much again while the slice gathering it grows and its decoded copy, about 100 MiB, so two come to about 200 MiB at their worst. `ReadTranscript` is handed what OPENS the archive, never an open one, so its slot (`transcriptReads`) is taken before the object opens (an opened object would hold its store's connection while the read waited) and given back only once the read has closed it; a read past them waits, nothing opened and no decoder held, for as long as its caller's context lasts, and a slot frees within one bounded read. A REPLY IS MATCHED ONLY TO A RUN STILL WAITING ON ITS QUESTION, by the store's own rule (`PendingRun.WaitsOn`, which the record's `takesAnswer` reads and so does the match, `Reply.Best`): a run whose ending is decided keeps its status until its row is deleted and takes no answer, so a reply to it is the ordinary message it is at once, where matched on its status it lost the record on every read and came back deferred until the ending finished. A REPLY TOO LARGE TO BE THE ANSWER IS ITS QUESTION'S REFUSAL (ADR-0114, answerowed.go): past `MaxAnswerBytes` it is not taken, and `PendingStore.RecordRefusal` records, by the answer's own compare-and-set and in its place, an answer naming the reply's deliveries and posted instant, sealed with who sent it and no words, beside `PendingRun.AnswerRefusal` (the ids, the message count and both figures, a key of its own at the top of the row, read through `Refused`, which binds it to the answer's deliveries, and opened only from a seal of that very refusal, `AnswerWords.Refusal`) — so while the refusal is the run's the question no longer waits and nothing written after it is taken as its answer; the refusal is then owed, retried, taken, revived and let go of exactly as an answer is, the resumed run handed it where an answer would be (`AskedRun.Refused`) and never an answer with no words, raised where the reply was posted off the address the refusal keeps (`AnswerRefusal.Address`, ids, held to `MaxRefusalAddressBytes`) and none of its words; the delivery, the reply's only copy, is kept (`AnswerRefused`, a deferral at the head of the inbox) and spent only once a turn that took the refusal keeps it (`Coordinator.spendRefused`), never at the take, which a turn can give back, or at a let-go, after which it comes round as the ordinary message it is — the delivery in hand spent on the first attempt's own account where its turn kept the refusal (`Coordinator.attemptOwed` reports it), never on the completion ledger's, which fails open, and otherwise decided off the row as that attempt left it (`Coordinator.refusedReply`, read again), where a relaunch that carried the round on from the refused question says another attempt's turn kept it (`PendingRun.carriedOnFromRefusal`); a turn that died holding it has it spent by the reap of its claim (`Coordinator.spendTaken`); a refusal let go of (declined after its attempts, or a seal that does not open) leaves the question waiting again under its old anchor, as an answer let go of does, and is announced as a chat `declined` with its figures (`announceRefusalLetGo`), and an ending that hands one back says so (`PendingRun.EndingReturnsRefusal`); its `executor_answered` `too_large` is one record per run, question and delivery (`tooLargeRecord`), published again by every copy that finds it, never beside a `resumed`; and the relaunch that carries a round on from a refused question keeps its figures for the round's reviewer (`PendingRun.RoundRefusals`, `maxRoundRefusals`), which reads the round from its calls and finds no reply there. A call to a tool at Needs approval parks the run as an ask does (ADR-0077, heldcall.go): `PendingRun.HeldCall` beside the question, its arguments and result AEAD-sealed (`CallSeal`), answered only by an answer by turn naming it (never a chat reply), and made at most once by the resume, which marks it under the claim's fence first (`MarkHeldCall`, `RecordHeldResult`): the result is written on the row first, then the call appended to the run's bridged log under the job's launch as a call of its round, once, by the held call's id (`BridgeCall`'s `held_id`), so a retry that finds the result made appends only where the log lacks it. EVERY RELAUNCH CONTINUES THE TURN'S KEPT SESSION in whatever box it gets (`restore.go`), and A CONVERSATION'S NEXT TURN THE SESSION ITS LAST TURN LEFT (`conversationbase.go`): the turn's first launch freezes the read its dispatch made on the record it creates (`PendingRun.Base`, a `ConversationBase` written by `BeginLaunch`'s create alone, its sections carrying a later build's keys), records it as the turn's `begin` (`recordBegin`, under `executordomain.BeginOpID`, asked for `beginRecordBudget`, fifteen seconds, on the launching frame), and continues the base in a box of its own only while the turn's row holds it (`continueBase`: the turn's own row and the base's read again at `RelaunchRead`, `BaseContinuityOf`, the base's object fetched and opened against the base's own row); a relaunch of a turn that has kept nothing continues the same base on any node, never a head read again (`ContinuedCapture`, `frozenBase`); every capture carries the read the first launch froze (`recordCapture`); and the seat's budget pre-check counts the base of every live turn that holds one (`overBudget`). ONE DECISION says whether a kept session can be continued (`ContinuityOf`, of the turn's row and its run's record: the turn live and not ending (no ending decided, end owed or end recorded on its record, `PendingRun.endDecided` — every launch refuses a row from the ending's decision on), the newest capture of THE JOB THE RELAUNCH FOLLOWS kept — never an earlier job's, which holds none of what the jobs after it were told — the row naming it, and a runner restoring it — `ArchiveRestorer.Restores` of the seat's chain for the launch's caller, of the member the launch picked for the launch, and of the member the node holding the seat would pick for a reader (`RelaunchTarget`): its harness, its archive format, a writer release whose sessions another box continues), which the launch's caller asks before it writes the run's briefs (`Coordinator.ContinuedCapture`, after waiting for the job's capture to be kept for at most `relaunchCaptureWait`, never its whole budget, on a frame that holds the seat; answering what that job's session's last request carried, kept or not) and the launch asks again of the record as it opened the job and against the box it got (`continueKept`: the turn's row read through `SessionRestore` at `linearizable` as the leaf's `executorkeep.TurnRow`, since the sandbox links no state log, the box's harness asked `CheckCarry`, the object fetched SEALED onto the node's disk and opened whole once before any of it reaches the box, its `restore-` spool swept with the captures', the restore carrier built there alone); what only the launch can say — the box's harness, the seat's budget — no reader states, and the read surface's `continuable` on a seat's sessions is a thin call of them through `ReadContinuityOf` (internal/api/queries), which says WHICH launch it is about (`ContinuedBy`: `relaunch` for a live turn, `next_turn` for an ended one, or one whose run is over or ending — undecided until its end is on the log — `next_fire` in their place where the conversation is a schedule's, the same decision told in the schedule's words, which the caller says by the key since this package cannot import notify, and none for a turn of no conversation): an ended row is `BaseContinuityOf` of the row where it is its conversation's head as the read that answered it named from the same snapshot (executordomain's `Page.Heads`, `SessionRead.Head`; a row that is not names the head, and one that names no session says which of four reasons, `executordomain.Session.NotKept`), for the member the node holding the seat would pick for a launch of NO turn — the revision live when the next turn comes, never one a segment of the ended turn still pins (`TargetAsk`); a live row is the relaunch's decision over the row read at the relaunch's level (`executordomain.RelaunchRead`, the row made the sandbox's `executorkeep.TurnRow` by `Session.Kept`, read again only where the row names another capture than the record keeps, or the record none, `RereadsRow`), for the member the node holding the seat would pick for the turn (`RelaunchTarget`, asked per turn, `RelaunchAsk`), and, where the turn kept nothing of its own, whether a relaunch in a box of its own continues the base its first launch was handed instead (`relaunchedBase`, the holder asked about the base's archive), with the half a person's answer takes beside it, carrying a parked run on in the box it parked in by the launch's own tests of a box it continues in (`PriorBox.ContinuesIn` and the host test of `Coordinator.hostsPrior` against the holder, `ContinuesIn`) and only where that box ran the row's own session (`PendingRun.SessionRan`), and undecided — never a no — wherever a relaunch would not decide now either (a job still working, or a claim whose read has neither taken its final capture nor begun its turn, a capture in flight or one whose record awaits the log's answer, which the relaunch asks again and continues where it landed (`captureAwaitsAnswer`, until its object is past `objstore.RecordWithin` and twice `objstore.AssumedClockSkew` on the reader's clock; the ask itself is made whatever the object's age, the log refusing a record past its window on its own clock), a target that cannot be said, a round lost a second time, a collected job's resume that only it decides) — until the resumed turn says what it does with the round as it begins, on the job's record (`LaunchRecord.ResumeCarry`, `ResumeCollects` or `ResumeCarriesOn`, `PendingStore.MarkResumeBegun` at `ResumeRequest.Begin`, best effort), on which the claim is decided for its whole turn. A capture's place is ITS TAKE'S (`sameTake`: the job and the instant the take stamped it at): only that take moves it on, a stamp the row already holds is answered taken (`holdsStamp`), so a reservation answered too late is the take's own when asked again, and one cut off at the take's deadline is settled by one stamp decided on the row as it stands, asked again within the read's time (`abandonPlace`, `askStamp`). The launch tells its caller how it decided before the run starts (`LaunchRequest.Decided`, `LaunchDecision`), so the caller arms the run's guard for that session; and a run that continues a session is WIRED TWICE (`RunRequest.FreshWiring`, from the job's other bridge token, `Opening.Fresh`), its script installing the wiring of the session its box started and removing the other before its harness starts, so the token the harness dials is the box's word on which session it started, recorded once on the job's record (`LaunchRecord.SessionStart`, `PendingStore.MarkSessionStart`) for every later rebuild of the guard, which takes the launch's armed skills only where it says `continued`, and which names the session the job ran (`PendingRun.SessionRan`: the launch names the one its script starts of its own beside the one it continues, `LaunchRecord.FreshSessionID`, and nothing a box's file says names it — a parked run keeps the session its launch named, never the one its collection read — and a row with no word for a launch that continued one names none), the one a person's answer or a replaced sign-in carries the round on in; the job's record carries a later build's keys as every growing section does (`LaunchRecord.Extra`, wirekeys.go), dropped only with the job by the relaunch that replaces it; a capture whose harness was cut off (`SessionCapture.Interrupted`: the engine packed it, or the script packed it after a harness that did not exit cleanly, `Result.HarnessCutOff`, or on its way out of an end a stop, a loss or its turn's cap asked of its job, `Result.JobEnd`) replaces the box's copy, and where the job before ran in this very box and its harness exited cleanly nothing is fetched. A launch given briefs told by a reading of what that capture holds beside the ones told by its save point (`SessionContinuation.Exact`, `ExactTelling`) hands one only to a box whose harness, asked by `CheckCarry` before the brief is chosen, answers exactly the release the reading was read for (`ExactTelling.Resumer`, the writer's; an unread answer matches none) and for which the telling wrote a brief, never where the box's own copy is continued unasked; otherwise it hands the brief told by the save point and logs `executor_session_holdings_unused`; the job records what the brief it handed left out either way (`continueSession`'s one assignment of `continuedLaunch.told`), and its caller is told which (`LaunchDecision.Exact`). A relaunch continuing such a cut-off kept capture can ask which of its round's calls the session holds (`Coordinator.HoldingsOf`, holdings.go; internal/agent/runner asks it once per relaunch, through the engine's launcher, of a capture whose first request it judges, and hands the briefs told by it beside the default as `ExactTelling`): inside one wait taken before the run's record is read (`holdingsReadWait`, thirty seconds), only of the capture that record keeps, cut off, where `ContinuityOf` still continues the turn's row with the runner that reads it (`HoldingsReader`), which is handed an opener streaming the row's object (`SessionRestore.Open`): a second read of the object, never spooled, so the node's scratch disk holds only the launch's own spool of it; every failure is no reading, never a refusal, and `SessionHoldings.Holds` holds a call only through its one live result whose failure and text are the log's: every byte of an output the log kept whole, and the size and opening of one it cut (`HoldingsOpeningBytes`, inside what the log always keeps of an output). Claude Code's runner answers it (`Runner.ReadHoldings`, executor/holdings.go and claudeholdings.go): only of a session a release at or after 2.1.310 wrote, the last writer its row records and every record in a chain as Claude Code's resume reads one (a user, assistant, attachment or system record whatever its `parentUuid`) by the release its `version` names, since a session continued across releases holds the records of each (`holdingsWriterFloor`, the oldest its rules were measured on, distinct from the carry floors, and a release it cannot read, a number past what an int holds among it, or none named, counted below it; such a record in a subagent's thread leaves that subagent not whole, its calls told under it), only for a session continued by a release whose resume the model of it was read from (the gate: `HoldingsAsk.Resumer`, the release that wrote the session, the relaunch's best word for the one that resumes it since its box is acquired after its briefs are written, held to `claudeResumeRead`, a set and never a range; an unread one is no reading for every reader, `Coordinator.HoldingsOf`; the gate reads the release alone, so a managed settings file that switches on the resume's interrupted turn, which acts at the continuation's first request, is what it leaves), inside the seats' one transcript slot within the process's two and never a pool of its own, the archive opened only once those are held; it reads the main thread and each depth-one subagent's thread and metadata, through the line layer, the archive metered at `threadPlainMax` and each member it reads handed what is left of that budget, reads no archive naming a member absolutely or through `..`, or holding a member it reads twice, and holds a call only where Claude Code's resume hands its model both the call's records and the request a resumed session sends pairs them: which records the resume hands, and in what orders, is the model's (`threadChain`, which the reading cites and never restates; in a subagent's thread the model of that subagent's own load, `newThreadChain(agent)`, the one conversation a SendMessage continuation hands its model), and the reading adds two rules of its own alone, THE PAIRING RULE (`holdingsPairing`, the conservative rule (C): the use's record carries a reply id; one use and one result of the id over every user and assistant record of its thread's file, chained or not, the use naming a tool of the bridge's own MCP server (`claudeBridgedToolPrefix`), and no batch's list, split id or id Claude Code's request writes of its own folding it; both records handed under every variant; and on every order the model hands, nothing but plain records of the use's reply and plain results records from that reply's first record to the later of the two, with the result after that first record, no earlier result of a call of that reply, and no compaction's boundary at or after it), so the results of calls made at once off the chain are held, as probe T measured, an attempt the harness withdrew and left in the file is not, and a shape the request pairs that the rule cannot place is told, and THE LAST RESTART (in the main thread a result, a leg's own answer and a task notification each count only past the thread's last restart, a forward guard beside the model's truth; a subagent's thread that restarts is not whole); no reading where the model cannot place the main thread (`threadChain.Unknown`: a marked checkpoint, a sidechain's record, a fork's briefing, a file's snapshot, a record of no lowercase canonical uuid or of another's, a result the resume may hang under another record than its parent, a thread past 5,242,880 bytes a large-file reader may read otherwise, the thread past `threadChainMaxRecords` or `threadChainMaxCalls`, or a progress record, which Claude Code's load drops and links the records after it past, among them); and none a content-replacement record names, since behind a flag its servers set Claude Code hands its model a preview in such a result's place and again on resume, and decides only once the store's stream has reached io.EOF. A call in a subagent's thread is held only where the reading reports that subagent (rule 2: its metadata, read by exact keys, links it to one Agent or Task call of the main thread; its thread is whole: never restarts, ends in no torn piece, holds no reply its model refused and no record taking records back, which no load of a subagent's thread filters, its own load's model places it, and every record of its own that no record of its own continues is that load's leaf or one it hands, `wholeOf`'s strict rule; every leg, that call and each SendMessage to it its own result did not refuse, has a live answer in the main thread past its last restart, the call's own completed foreground answer, which the pairing rule pairs with the call, or a task notification the resume hands its model under every variant, which a continuation hands its model verbatim as probe T's T10 measured, a SendMessage under an id the log keeps none of being a leg nothing answers, and an answer carrying a handback (`carriesHandback`, `textHandback`) answering none; its file starts no more legs than that, and along every order its own load hands each ends on a reply whose text says something and with which its model ended its turn, its stop reason `end_turn`, no API error and a model other than `<synthetic>`, Claude Code's own whole test of one, since a request that failed for good leaves a reply of the harness's own on which it ends the subagent and answers its call completed, and every reply the harness writes in place of a model's is of that model), and the pairing rule holds of the call on every order that load hands, and is told under that subagent's heading where the subagent is not reported; bounded at `holdingsMaxAgents` subagents, `holdingsMaxLegs` legs and their notifications, `holdingsMetaMaxBytes` a metadata and `holdingsDescriptionBytes` a subagent's description, the one free text of the box's a reading hands on (declared `untrusted:"name"`), and keeping no id the call-id rule refuses, which nothing could link, answer or notice; a subagent's `Why` holds the reading's own words, ids that rule holds, and the first line of a decode's error, redacted and cut as a reading's errors are. A capture stamps what its session had been answered and told — its save point, how many of the round's calls the run's log had taken when the capture read it, after its script's pack, where its harness ended its own run, and else the newest its launch is known to hold (`SessionCapture.Through`, `BridgeLog.Appends`, savepoint.go), beside the arrival places an older build reads it by, off the same read or the same capture (`BridgeSeq`, `BridgeOpen`, `openPlaces`), and the notes the run's record says it holds (`notesHeld`: every note the record says was read where its harness ended its own run, up to the first read it marks unanswered and not past it, whichever job's take that was, none where it was cut off, whatever its job read) — and its place and its outcome are stamped through a store's bad moment (`reserveCapture`, `stampSettled`). The run carries every brief its restore can come to (`RunRequest.FreshBrief` beside its continuation), its script hands its harness the one the restore chose, and the launch records which (`LaunchRecord.Brief`, `BriefKind`: `continued`, `continued_new_box`, `continued_conversation`, `fresh`), published on its collection beside `Result.Continued`/`FellBack`. No reason a session is not continued fails a launch: each falls to the session the box holds, where the launch reuses the job before's box and may continue its copy, or else to a session of its own, and is logged once that fallback is decided, its outcome naming it (`executor_session_not_continued`, `continueSession`). A box's FILE CONTRACT is four reads, every backend held to them by `sandboxtest.Box`: `ReadFile` (whole, refused past `MaxFileBytes`), `OpenFile` (a stream front to back), `ReadTail` (a stream's end, with its size) and `OpenTail` (a stream's last n bytes STREAMED, with its size: a suffix range on envd's download, a seek on the host — what a failed check's log is kept from, so keeping 64 MiB never costs the gigabytes before it). A SEAT'S OWN READ OF ITS SESSIONS goes through the transcript reader too (`executor.ReadTranscriptAs`, `ReadBySeat`), never a copy of it: held to `SeatTranscriptBounds` (fifty entries, 32 KiB of text and 8 KiB an entry, the rest of a 64 KiB tool answer left for the row, the fences and the JSON), narrowed by a `TranscriptFilter` — some kinds of entry, or the entries whose WHOLE text holds every term (the analyzer's whole terms), DECIDED ON WHAT ITS READER IS SHOWN AND ON NOTHING ELSE: an entry whose recorded words could hold every term (`Reading.MayHold`, a test no entry whose redaction shows them fails, which decides nothing) is redacted whole and is a hit only where what the redaction shows of its own words, never a marker's (`redact.OwnWords`, `Reading.FindIn`), holds every term — a tool call's words read through its input's JSON escapes (`readingOf`, `textindex.JSON`), since its text is that input as JSON and read as written a string's `\n` glued its `n` to the word opening the next line, and every other entry's as written — its window cut from that redaction around the earliest term it shows (`windowAround`, an end inside a marker moved off it, away from the word; `EntryPlace.TextFrom` an offset in the redaction), and a full page stops at a hit, decided before the page's bound is asked — decided on the recorded text, a word only a credential held counted toward a hit beside a shown one, a word a marker ends was missed unless a credential held it whole, and a page stopped where the recorded text let an entry through, each of which confirmed guesses at a redacted value; every entry counted toward the ordinals whether kept or not (`Transcript.Places`, `Next` the first entry neither handed out nor passed over), so one cursor serves a filtered page and a whole one, and a part the read could not read (`EntryUnread`) handed out whatever the filter asks, never passed over as continuity; and seats' reads take at most `SeatTranscriptReadsAtOnce` (one) of the process's two slots, a seat slot first, so a node whose seats page transcripts still reads a person's. A REPLY IN THE CONVERSATION ANSWERS ONLY SOMEBODY ITS QUESTION WAS PUT TO (`Reply.Writer`, `Reply.putTo`, ADR-0077's amendment): a seat of the question's audience or the run's requester, the seat the engine's party registry resolves the reply to, whose holder may act when it arrives (`AudienceResolver.MayAnswer`, asked only once a run would take the reply, so ordinary chat reads no directory, an unknown keeping the reply's place); anybody else's reply runs as its own turn and the run stays parked, and a row naming nobody admits nobody. AND ONLY A REPLY WRITTEN FOR WHOM THE RUN WORKS FOR (`Reply.WorkingFor`, read off the newest event by the engine's `writtenForIn`, and `Reply.writtenForTheRun`): nil, a writer who spoke for itself, always qualifies; otherwise both sides are read as the ceiling reads them before it asks the directory and compared by kind and by handle or login, never a credential's apps. THE TURN'S LEADS ARE KEPT BESIDE THE RUN'S ROW, a part of their own as the bridged log is (`coord.RunLeads`, `leadsPart`, leads.go), what carried each session of the run on: sealed once by the launch whose brief first told it (`LeadSeal`, domain `seat.run.lead`, leadseal.go, `Coordinator.sealLeads`), taken in by the attach that names a job's command BEFORE its write of the row, once each by its identity and numbered as taken (`leadsPart.took`, `KeptLead.Appended`), held to `MaxKeptLeadBytes` and `MaxKeptLeads` with the oldest dropped first and still counted (`leadsPart.Appends`), and outliving every relaunch as the row does, while the row keeps only each job's marks, the numbers of the leads its brief told (`LaunchRecord.LeadMarks`, `BoxRef.Leads`, cleared by the reset), the run the part is of (`PendingRun.LeadsRun`, named by the create, so a part an earlier run of the turn left is none of this one's and the next take replaces it, and a launch of an ended run is refused) and how many leads the part has taken (`PendingRun.LeadsAppended`), so the resume's read inside its frame (`PendingStore.Leads`, `TurnLeads`, `Coordinator.leadsAt`) tells every lead the part cannot give back as not kept, never as none, and where it cannot read the part at all hands the job's marks on by number for the next job to mark in turn (`TurnLeads.Unread`, `BriefTold.ToldUnread`, `sandbox_leads_part_unread`), so one failed read drops no lead from the chain; `LaunchRecord.ToldLeads` names none for a session its job's box started of its own; a held call's result rides its lead as its row sealed it (`HeldKept`, `CallSeal.OpenKept`). WHAT CLAUDE CODE COUNTS A CONTINUED SESSION AT is bounded from above (`resumed.go`, `ResumedCount`: its last counted reply's input, cache and output and every chained line after the reply's first record at `ClaudeCharge`, two bytes a token), read whole off the session's own main thread at both places a copy is read whole, the take's meter over the kept archive (`SessionCapture.ResumedCount`, `TakenSession.Count`) and ONE read of the job's main thread in the collection, on the box's host before the take and within `MainThreadReadWait`, written with how the harness ended in the one write (`EndRead.Count`, `LaunchRecord.ResumedCount`, `LaunchRecord.counted`) and never carried to the next job (hooks' receipts settled apart, before a collection's, stop's or loss's take: `settleHookReceipts`); what its bytes cannot bound is a mark in precedence order (`CountMark`, `CountMarks`), each read as a session that may compact; beside it the least window the run reported (`Result.ReportedWindow`), which only lowers, and the uuid of the thread's newest restart, blind only where the thread was not read whole or not by the rules of its release (every other mark is the bound's, and leaves it known), which the launch records as its job's basis (`LaunchRecord.BasisRestart`, `RestartBasis`, `PriorBox.Restart`, `BoxRef.Restart`) so a job that restarted its session after its brief is known by identity, never by count (`RestartedSince`); `ExecutorWindow.SendsWhole` is Claude Code's own threshold (the window it holds the run to, `Standard` per `ClaudeRoute`, lowered by the reported window, less its 20,000-token reply reserve and 13,000-token buffer, and `claudeTurnAttachments`), beside `Carries`, which keeps the floor's sendable request and never the session uncompacted. In `sandbox/executor`: the one walk of a thread's lines every reading of it shares (`mainthread.go`, `walkThread`, `threadReading`), the count's reading by the harness's anchor rule (`resumed.go`, `resumedReading`, `claudeCountFloor`, never below the settings pin's floor), the meter now reading through the layer (`meterArchive`, its floor figure's rule kept in `contextReading`), and the least `contextWindow` off the result lines (`claudeEvents.reportedWindow`). |
| `hostbox` | The primitives for running somebody else's process on the engine host. Three subsystems must do it IDENTICALLY — the subscription harness backend, which drives a harness per seat with its own HOME, the local sandbox provider (a harness in a box, and the container runtime's own CLI), and the MCP client's stdio children — and each primitive is a guard, so a second implementation is not drift but a hole. THE ENVIRONMENT IS ONE ALLOWLIST: `PassthroughEnv` for every child, `HostUserEnv` beside it for a child that runs as the host user in no box of its own (a box replaces those), `ContainerRuntime` for a container runtime's own CLI — the sandbox's and a `command: docker` tool server's alike, one rule so neither copy loses `DOCKER_HOST` — and `Environ` to render it, which is NEVER nil because os/exec reads a nil `Cmd.Env` as the engine's own — and the engine's own is where Tier A's keyring and token values resolve from. The MCP client handed every stdio server that whole environment, every container-runtime call reached its CLI the same way, and the CLI backend's private copy of the list had drifted by five names. It is not isolation: a child runs as the engine's user and can read what that user can, so what the allowlist removes is the engine HANDING its secrets over. A SHARED LOGIN OF SEVERAL FILES IS WRITTEN AS ONE JOURNALED STEP (`WriteCredentials`), and the write NAMES ITS STEPS (`CredentialStep`) to an observer its caller hands it (`CredentialSteps`), nil in every write the engine makes: it is the kill matrix's seam (`hostbox/hostboxtest`), not dead code, and a reader finishing a write reports none, since what a reader killed part way leaves is what the write killed at the same step leaves. EVERY READ AND WRITE OF A SHARED LOGIN FIRST REMOVES WHAT A KILLED WRITE STAGED: a write stopped between staging a file and its rename left the copy, credential bytes no logout removed, so each hold of the login's lock removes the files staged inside its directory and the journal's copy beside it, staged under a name of its own for that reason; never the directory's parent, where other writers stage their own files under other locks |
| `hostbox/hostboxtest` | The kill matrix every writer of a shared login passes: it SIGKILLs the re-executed writer at each named step of its journaled writes and judges what the kill left. |
| `flock` | The one exclusive cross-process file lock (`store`, `queue/jetstream`, `hostbox`, `gitlet/cache`), plus a pin: a lock on a file description a child inherits, held while any process holds it. |
| `sockcount` | What a TCP socket's kernel holds unacknowledged and has had acknowledged, read from the connection's own descriptor (Linux and darwin). |
| `procgroup` | Signals a child process tree as one process group, tells whether a recorded group is still the same one, and sums a group's CPU |
| `gitlet/gitproc` | The one wrapper that starts git: every git process the engine runs, with its built environment and configuration, lane slot, process-tree end and classified failure, plus the git version floors (ADR-0087). |
| `gitlet/cache` | Each Gitlet node's disposable bare repositories, one a repository it serves, derived by convergence from the code log's rows and the object store, from which real git upload-pack answers clones and fetches. |
| `gitlet/compact` | The maintainer's three loops over a repository's packs: compaction bounds their count, publication writes its bitmap layers, expunge drops what no ref reaches. |
| `gitlet/host` | Gitlet's smart-HTTP git surface for persons and boxes: admission, fetch and push serving, the one landing every push and engine write takes to an applied row, cache repair, verifies, merge chains and downloads. |
| `gitlet/gitlettest` | The one suite every Gitlet topology passes, real git through a real code log, and the data-node and Gitlet-node blocks every topology is built from |
| `gitlet/gitletfault` | The named fault points of Gitlet's push, sync, maintenance, write, check, expunge and mirror paths, in order, and the injector the crash suites arm at them; nil in production. |
| `gitlet/gitletcluster` | Test-only and solo: Gitlet on three data nodes through the production estate router, running the fleet suite (`gitlettest.RunFleet`) and the commit-chain benchmark. |
| `gitlet/home` | A repository's home Gitlet node at work: the loop that schedules each repository's change, check, merge-queue and registered workers from the rows, and the merge previews beside it. |
| `gitlet/checkrun` | Runs a repository's queued checks on every Gitlet node, each in a box with no harness, and the home's sweep of attempts whose runner is gone. |
| `gitlet/mirror` | A repository's import, pull-mirror syncs and push-mirror pushes, run as a worker of its home Gitlet node's loop. |
| `gitlet/query` | Server-side git reads of a repository by object id on a Gitlet node's pinned cache copy: trees, files, diffs, histories, blames, a change's revisions, code search and find. |
| `gitlet/readaudit` | The ledger that folds a node's reads of `visibility: unit` repositories into one `code_fetched` record per reader a flush, off the read's path. |
| `deployfiles` | THE CLOSED LIST of a repository's files the atlas may be told about, decided BY A FILE'S PATH ALONE (`Classify`), reading no file and importing nothing of the engine, so Gitlet (which lists and serves the files) and atlas/codescan (which parses them) hold one copy. On it: a ROOT Dockerfile and Procfile, listed BY PRESENCE and never read (`Format.Parsed` is false: their lines are build arguments, environment values and commands); compose files and their overrides; Kubernetes manifests under a directory named k8s, kubernetes, manifests or deploy; a Helm chart's Chart.yaml; `*.tf` and `*.tofu`; fly.toml; a root render.yaml; package.json, go.mod, requirements files, pyproject.toml, Gemfile and Cargo.toml. NEVER ON IT, whatever else matches: a file or a directory whose name starts with a dot (every .env file), a values file (no schema, and where a chart's passwords are written), a variable or state file, a manifest named for secrets or credentials, a Helm template, a vendored, test or example tree, and a path with a control character or past `MaxPathBytes`. The bounds are here too, each with its reason: `MaxFileBytes` (64 KiB, the checks file's), `MaxFiles` (200 a repository). `Listed` is the one selection of a tree's files (on the list, by path, the first `MaxFiles`), which Gitlet's listing and a forge's both take. |
| `codeindex` | Gitlet's trigram index as pure functions over bytes: per-blob segments, tip maps, the plan of a regexp over them, and the query syntax, so a code search reads only the few files that can match. |
| `codesearch` | Code search of every repository an asker may read, on the Gitlet nodes that hold them: one repository a git read, more one signed scatter with an exact merge and a coverage of what was not searched (M6-D14). |
| `learning` | What a seat remembers: episodes, diary, counterparty profiles and self-taught skills, all best effort. `memsync` carries the rows across a fleet; `memread` answers a screen from the holder. |
| `schedule` | Role- and unit-scoped cron work: at-most-once delivery, missed-tick catchup, manual runs, and a conversation per schedule per seat. |

## The edges

| Package | What it is for |
|---|---|
| `api` | REST, the sockets and the dashboard, served inside the engine's process over its own store, broker and coordination plane; every request is guarded, and each surface states its authority where it mounts. |
| `api/webhooks` | Inbound vendor webhooks (GitHub, GitLab, Jira, Confluence, Slack, Datadog, Forge) and the OAuth landings, each verified before the delivery is recorded |
| `runtoken` | The signed, self-describing per-run credential (OTLP receiver, tool bridge, Gitlet box git, artifact view ticket) that any node of a fleet can mint and verify; its key derivation also feeds the broker credentials. |
| `recordseal` | A record's seal under the fleet's keyring (ADR-0031): the frame, `Signer`, `Verifier` and three verdicts (`Verified`, `KeyUnknown`, `Tampered`). A leaf that imports nothing of the engine, so every sealing channel can use it. |
| `signedask` | The one rule for a request one node asks another to carry out over the queue's ephemeral verbs, and its reply: sealed with the fleet's record seal, bound to an id and deadline, never replayable (ADR-0031) |
| `api/mcpbridge` | Serves a detached box run's tool surface (a seat's executor or Dashboard Assistant deep work) to its harness over MCP on HTTP, dispatching through `tools.Surface`, fleet-addressed and relayed to the seat holder. |
| `mcprelay` | `crewlet mcp-relay <socket>`: the stdio MCP server a harness starts for a seat's tool session, copying stdin to the engine's per-run unix socket and back; reads only its argv |
| `mcpreview` | Which MCP tools a server changed since their levels were decided, and which of them ask a person first (ADR-0078): ONE pure reading over the fleet's listings and the accepted definitions (`coord.MCPAcceptedTool`), which the node filing a tool asks at each call (`Index.Unreviewed`), the node listing a server first accepts its first listing by (`Baseline`), the alarm counts by (`Holds`) and a server's page shows (`Changes`: added, changed with the parts that moved, removed, and LOOSENED where the hints the server declared claim less risk than before). A server is BASELINED once any record names it, so a removal accepted keeps its record; a removal is judged only where every live listing is whole. No store, no clock and no configuration but the levels a caller hands in, so four readers cannot disagree |
| `toolsession` | One phase's tools served to one harness run over MCP, on a unix socket per run: the engine's end of `crewlet mcp-relay`. Transport only; the tool loop decides every call. |
| `toolsession/toolsessiontest` | The one contract suite every `toolsession.Dispatcher` (the tool loop's, the bridge's) runs, through a real MCP session, so the gates both hold are written once |
| `whsec` | The Standard Webhooks signing-secret format, and the one place that decides what a valid one is: `whsec_` plus padded base64 over a 32-byte key, with the DECODED bytes as the HMAC key. Three places need the rule and none may disagree — written twice it drifted, and a `${VAR}` holding a 16-byte key was refused as a literal and silently accepted as a reference |
| `atlassianhost` | Decides once whether a Jira or Confluence address is Atlassian Cloud or Data Center (`Of`), and which API gateway a cloud id is read through (`Gateway`), for config validation and both clients |
| `api/authapi` | `/auth`: how a person becomes a principal (sign-in, second factor, step-up, invitation and reset links, password change, token exchange, sign-out); owns the HTTP shape and the refusals. |
| `api/iamapi` | `/iam`: the company's identity directory over HTTP: people, service accounts, seats, invitations, tokens and password links, always guarded. |
| `api/workapi` | The human write surface over Worklet, Doclet and Chatlet: `/worklet/*`, `/doclet/pages`, `/chatlet/channels`, as adapters over the same tools a seat holds. |
| `api/configapi` | `/config`: the versioned company document, its revision history and diffs, entity routes over seats, units and charter, and the one write path onto it (`/setup`, the charter and the engine's own writers use it too). |
| `api/setupapi` | `/setup`: connecting an integration from the dashboard, admin-only; it writes through `/secrets` and `PATCH /config`, never on its own. |
| `api/llmapi` | The model routes Settings › Models writes through: a credential tested before it is stored, model checks run on the node's own time, retries, host-login checks and Codex device sign-ins, each audited |
| `api/mcpapi` | Settings › MCP & tools' own routes: the catalogue, a server's detail, restart, the tests, tool levels, write-only values and the OAuth sign-in, each guarded and audited |
| `api/operator` | The operator catalogue: Worklet, Doclet, Chatlet, code and health tools, built per call and served to the people running the company through one dispatch over MCP, the dashboard act route and the write surface. |
| `assistant` | The Dashboard Assistant's orchestrator: a person's question answered by a model through `agent/toolloop`, every read made as that person and every write a proposal they approve; not a seat (ADR-0050). |
| `assistant/wire` | The Dashboard Assistant's protocol vocabulary, a leaf: event kinds, closed sets, frames, cards and route bodies, and the stream's one encoding, held to the dashboard's TypeScript contract both ways |
| `assistant/catalog` | The Dashboard Assistant's tool catalogue: curated tools with their own schema, card and preview, and the generic API tools that reach any route the person may use, each call a proposal or read. |
| `research` | The web pages Ask Crewlet reads (ADR-0151): `https` on the hosts `assistant.research` lists, `GET` with no credentials, redirects only to listed hosts, 2 MiB in 15 s through the guarded dial, text in 12 KiB parts, cached ten minutes per node. |
| `assistant/exec` | The ordinary request an assistant tool call becomes: `Request`, `Response`, and the `Dispatcher` and `Grant` seams, a leaf `api/auth` implements |
| `enginedocs` | The engine's own `docs/` and `adr/` pages, embedded (the module root's `Docs`), searched with `textindex`'s BM25 and read by section; `Anchor` is the one GitHub-slug rule the docs gate also uses. |
| `assistant/cards` | Turns a tool's answer into the engine-built cards the Dashboard Assistant shows (table, entity, open in app, error, notice), never authored by the model |
| `assistant/proposal` | The pure logic of a Dashboard Assistant proposal: the digest binding an approval to the previewed request, the legal state moves, expiry, and the card's rows drawn from the server's preview. No I/O (ADR-0051) |
| `assistant/proposal/approval` | Carries a person's decision on a Dashboard Assistant proposal out: approve, decline, Stop on a parked turn, and a page action's claim and report (ADR-0051) |
| `assistant/live` | A Dashboard Assistant turn's live stream: a bounded in-memory frame log on the running node, and the fleet half that lets another node follow it and stop it |
| `assistant/assistanttest` | The Dashboard Assistant's test-only support: golden files, a turn-frame builder, example args, a sample company and the suite rig over `assistant/evals` |
| `assistant/evals` | The assistant RUN WITHOUT A NODE, as PRODUCTION code: a real `assistant.Service` over in-memory stand-ins for its seams (`Store`, `Dispatcher`, `Revoker`, `Grant`, `Models`, `Budget`, `Audit`, `Metrics`, `Catalogue`), the ONE scripted provider (`Model`, `Round`), `Assemble` (the rig every suite and every case share, taking the App's real dispatcher and delegations for internal/api's suites), and the evaluation cases `crewlet assistant eval` reads (`LoadSuite`, strict through `config.ParseMember`) and judges (`RunSuite`). It never imports `testing` or `net/http/httptest`, so the binary links it and no test scaffolding; it answers a case's seeded routes with net/http's own pattern matching on a request value, never a transport (the dispatcher walk reads this package too). An evaluation measures what a model does with the tools it is offered, never the API's authority, which internal/api's suites hold |
| `api/assistantapi` | `/assistant/`: a person's assistant conversations, turns, streams, files and approvals over HTTP, guarded and refusing in the engine's envelope. |
| `api/secretsapi` | `/secrets`: the company's credentials, written through a running node because the coordination broker has no socket; admin-only, reveals are break-glass and logged, a write re-activates the revision that reads it. |
| `api/stream` | The `/ws/stream` socket: pushes, plus a request/response query channel that is a thin adapter over the SAME function each REST route calls. WHO IS ASKING IS THE GUARD'S ANSWER, read from the context: the handler used to re-read the credential through the Tier A arm alone, so a browser signed in with a session cookie was refused here while every REST route beside it served them — and no identity travels beside the query as an argument, because two identities on one call are two chances to answer about different people. The in-flight query budget is PER SOCKET (`MaxInFlightQueries`, four): a tab's share of the reader floor `internal/store` derives as "two full dashboards at four queries each", so the two are one decision stated twice, and a person's tabs each have their own. A burst past the pool waits in the pool's own queue, and the one read that must never wait behind it — resolving who is acting — runs on the store's reserved connection; that reserve is why the per-PRINCIPAL budget it replaced, refcounted across a person's sockets behind a `sync.OnceFunc` release, was machinery that bought nothing. The HANDSHAKE'S `Origin` is judged by the writes' own rule (`auth.CSRF.RefuseCrossSite`), because a WebSocket handshake is a GET the write gate passes as a read while the socket it opens streams the company's state, and the library's own check compared `Origin` with the request's `Host` — refusing a second hostname in `api.auth.allowed_origins` and every browser behind a proxy that rewrites `Host`. A socket is AUTHENTICATED AT ITS HANDSHAKE AND ENDED BY WHAT ENDS ITS CREDENTIAL (`lifetime.go`), because a handshake decision alone let a revoked session's tab go on receiving the company's state for as long as it stayed open — and never on a timer of its own: the 60-second re-check it replaced read the directory per tab per minute and still let a revocation stand for that minute. Each event is handled the cheapest correct way. An identity move that NAMES the credential — a session's lineage, the person's id, or the login a Tier A token's binding row holds, `token:<id>` (`CredentialsMoved`, every node serving the API hearing its own applier) — decides exactly the sockets it names by the guard (`auth.Guard.ResolveOpen`, a session's idle deadline set aside — a socket can never be re-issued a cookie, and an open live view is activity), each on its own goroutine with a direct read. A move that NAMES NOBODY — the fleet session generation, a record the node retained, a replaced estate, the node passing the stall grace (`identityvouch.go`) — closes EVERY socket 1013 (try again later) WITHOUT READING ANYTHING, and the reconnect's handshake decides: a read per socket there was one identity read per open tab queued at once on the store's one reserved identity connection, and the per-credential decision queue built to soften it (`decisions.go`) is gone with it. A published company is decided IN MEMORY against the roster just published (`Options.SeatOf`): a seat that company no longer holds as a human seat closes 4403, a seat that answers to another handle 1013, and the watch is decided again, with no identity read. The credential's own end is a timer per socket (`auth.Lifetime`), re-armed while the guard still serves it, and every socket is decided once as it starts listening, so a record that landed between the handshake and that moment is not missed. A credential that names nobody closes 4401, a person whose seat is gone 4403, and one this node cannot decide 1013, so the reconnect's handshake answers 503 before any snapshot — there is no `identity` frame and no degraded hold. A decision that resolves SOMEBODY ELSE — another login, another seat or none, other grants — closes 1013 too, so the reconnect's handshake builds the snapshot for the new grants and the tab asks who it is (`viewer`) again: kept open, the socket answered the new principal while the tab went on naming the old one, and a snapshot re-sent in place for a changed audience moved the pushes and left that answer behind. WHAT a socket receives is decided per KIND: every push kind declares the NEED its question takes beside its route (`stream.Need`: `event` is an `events` row, so `admin`'s; `inbox_changed` is the SEAT ROOM's (`NeedInbox`), which names no grant: the watch that put a socket in the room was decided as a read of that person's inbox, and the frame names the domain whose half of it moved, so an app in front of the kind would be one domain's permission before every domain's news — WHICH watcher a frame reaches is its half's app's, the tracker's Worklet's and the code log's Gitlet's, decided per reader by the frame itself (`InboxChange.ForReader`, its `App` named by the producer and never on the wire, a frame naming none reaching nobody; ADR-0110); every other company kind is the OVERVIEW, everybody's — ADR-0033), a client carries the AUDIENCE its principal's permissions describe, and what of the overview it receives is decided PER PAYLOAD (`stream.Shaped`) — a seat's live call masked to its status for a reader that seat's trail is not open to, by the rule `audit.seat.read` decides, and the snapshot — and its four REST mirrors, `/agents`, `/org`, `/tools` and `/stream/snapshot` — is built for that audience. It used to decide only that the caller was somebody, which was harmless while every credential was an operator token and a firehose the day a session cookie resolved: a person who could only write work items received every phase's prompt and response. Targeted kinds are ROOM-ROUTED through ONE keyed index (`RoomKey`, `seat:<handle>` or `artifact:<id>`), and a room frame with no room is dropped, never broadcast. `inbox_changed` is the seat room's kind, pushed by `InboxChanged` (timed into change visibility from the movement's broker instant, which the frame does not carry) to the sockets whose `watch` of that seat was ALLOWED — the watch's name RESOLVED first through `iam.OwnerOf` and the same directory `work_inbox` reads, so a login is installed on the record its holder's notices are pushed to rather than on the login as sent (where no frame ever arrives, and on which a lead was refused as leading nobody), and the directory asked only once the watch is decided as far as it can be without it; then decided as the composed `inbox` question about the seat is (`authz.ActionInbox`: its holder or whoever leads it, with no app, or `admin`), since its frames announce every native domain's notices, and three-valued: a refusal is an error frame on a socket that stays open, never a 4403, which the dashboard reads as the whole channel withdrawn; an undecidable answer installs nothing; and every decision of the socket's credential, and every published company, re-decides the watch, withdrawing it only on a refusal. AN ARTIFACT'S ROOM (`rooms.go`) is joined by `artifact_join`, decided like reading the artifact (`get_artifact`, three-valued, through `Options.Rooms.Access`; refused is `not_found` — `artifact_trashed` for an artifact disclosed to the reader and in the trash, the code a datastore question about it answers too, as the REST routes' `409` — undecidable `unavailable` with its hint, and neither closes the socket) and answered with its roster in the presence hub's delivery order (the socket joined and answered inside `presence.Hub.Join`'s hand, as a chat room's join is, so no `artifact_presence` frame overtakes the answer and none taken before it is lost), left by `artifact_leave`, told what the socket is doing by `presence` frames (held to ten a second per socket on the read loop, then handed with the joins to ONE room goroutine per socket so a leave never overtakes its join), and pushed `artifact_changed`, `artifact_data` and `artifact_presence`, the first two from the appliers the engine's live relay says this node hears (its own while its copy serves, a sound peer's while it does not) and the third from its presence hub. A join is DECIDED AGAIN on every decision of the credential and on every share, trash, transfer, purge or reset of the artifact: the movement SUSPENDS the room's datastore pushes on the applier's goroutine and a goroutine of the service's decides each member, withdrawing a refused one with an error frame naming the artifact, keeping an undecidable one, and telling a kept one that missed a push to re-read. A datastore push is SHAPED PER READER (`artifactdata.Readable` over the role each join decided and the reader's record, one encode per set of rows a reader may see, a reader who may see none sent nothing) and names its documents instead of carrying them past `PushFrameMaxBytes`. Every switch of the relay's source reaches the rooms as the two domains' own reset, which pushes every room a `reset`, decides it again and tells its datastore to re-read. At most `MaxRoomsPerSocket` rooms per socket. Two walks hold the kinds: every room-routed kind needs a `PushSeat` or `PushArtifact` producer, and the dashboard's `PushKind` union equals the declared kinds in both directions. A refusal on AUTHORITY — a query's or a watch's — carries the rule's `reason` and the `grants` that would have admitted the caller (and an artifact's needed `role`) on its `unauthorized` error frame, under the REST envelope's own keys, worded by `authz.RefusalFields` through `RefusedFor` and never spelled here — no relation to an artifact is `not_found` with nothing beside it, and a code the mapping gives that this vocabulary lacks (`step_up_required`, `token_refused`) is the fault it would be: the app used to reduce it to the question's name on the way here, so the dashboard's live data channel was the one transport that could not say what would change the answer. CHAT'S LIVE HALF IS THE `ChatHub` (`chatpush.go`), and EVERY FRAME IS FILTERED BY ITS RECIPIENT'S OWN VISIBILITY before it leaves: the eight `chat_*` kinds need the reader's `chatlet` (`NeedChatlet`; an audience's app terms are DERIVED from `iam.Apps`, so the next app is one line in `iam` rather than five edits here), are routed `RouteViewer`, which `Hub.Broadcast` refuses, and are held to the reader's need and posture FIRST (`Client.admits`, so a socket a frame will never reach costs no visibility read), then decided per recipient by `chatlet.Visible` at the committed batch's own position, then leave by the ONE fan-out every push takes (`fanOut`: need, posture, shape, one encode per posture), so a degraded node sends none. The viewer is the seat `iam.ActorFor` names the socket's principal as — the function a chat write's author comes from — decided ONCE at the join and never re-resolved, because a principal that moves closes the socket (`lifetime.go`). A person's read state is pushed to their own sockets from a WATCH of the coordination record on every node serving sockets, never from a scatter the writer sends. A CHAT ROOM'S PRESENCE (who is viewing, typing, working) is the presence hub's (`internal/presence`, ADR-0038) like an artifact's: the chat room's `presence` frame (`room: "chat"`, `channel_id`) IS the join — a tab looks at one chat room at a time, so a frame naming another room moves the socket and an empty one leaves — admitted once `chatlet.Visible` lets its viewer see the room (the decision a committed frame takes, so a refusal leaves nothing and says nothing), pushed as `chat_presence` from the hub's fan-out to the sockets in the room and nobody else — its roster handed in the presence hub's delivery order (`presence.Hub.Join`'s hand, which places the socket and sends the roster under the chat hub's ONE LOCK, the lock every fan-out of the room's presence chooses its sockets and sends under), so a socket is shown a fan-out wholly before its roster or after it and nothing after the roster that took it out — and decided again by every committed record that narrows the room, which takes out whoever it shuts out with a roster naming nobody, and by every gap, which may have held such a record and so decides every socket in a room again — keeping one it cannot decide, where the record fails closed, because a gap is only the possibility of a change; a seat's working indicator is its agent member there (`SetStatus` touches, `ClearStatus` withdraws), raised on whichever node runs the turn and carried between nodes sealed. The unsigned fleet-wide scatter it replaced, `crewlet.chat.presence`, let anybody on the cluster port show anybody typing in any room. A batch the hub cannot keep up with is dropped and owes every screen a `chat_room_changed` naming no room, which is also how a gap in what the engine hears is told. The hub is the PROCESS's (`api.EngineChatLive`), attached to the engine after `New` on every node, and only an API mounted over it makes it say it serves sockets. AN UNSERVED SURFACE IS ONE TYPE (`Unserved`: its own code, its words, and when to ask THIS node again — zero where waiting clears nothing), read by `UnavailableOf` for both transports: a node with no company yet (`ErrNoCompany`, one value of it) was a sentinel with an arm of its own in each transport's mapping, and a node serving none of Gitlet now was answered over REST alone, by routes of its own — so a REST 503 and the socket's `unavailable` frame could say different things about when to come back. |
| `observe` | The observability edge: what becomes a durable event-store row, what reaches the live dashboard, and the custody that lets a node without `data` keep its events on the fleet's data nodes. |
| `eventfan` | Answers the fleet's turn-level history (events, turns, traces, tool calls, outcomes) by asking every data node its own store at query time and merging exactly, with a coverage naming nodes that did not answer (ADR-0021) |
| `tokens` | Rolls LLM spend into the one breakdown a dashboard renders (phase, model, provider, worker, seat, time), for the live window and for named windows read from the replicated `usage` domain. |
| `coalesce` | How a node publishes a record it makes many times a second: occurrences folded per key, sealed into one retry-safe event a flush, published off the caller's path (ADR-0008); `auxspend` and `gitlet/readaudit` own the records |
| `auxspend` | The seats' auxiliary-model spend, recorded: the `Use` attribution, the per-turn `Tally` and `Budget` meter, and the `Ledger` coalescing calls into `auxiliary_spend` records |
| `tracing` | OpenTelemetry wiring: one always-installed TracerProvider configured from `OTEL_*`, and the conversion between OTel spans and the event envelope's trace ids |
| `notify` | The backend-neutral notification spine: conversation keys, digest coalescing, party resolution, the rate valve, wake-body fencing, and thread reads and status writes across chat surfaces. |
| `backup` | A restorable copy of a node's store, streams and objects, taken from inside the engine because it alone reaches all three; the manifest, written last, is the claim. |
