| `maintenance` | The retention sweep for short-horizon tables, state-log operation ledgers, coordination removal markers, removed seats' chat read state and mailbox retirement; a fleet singleton, never a replicated row |
| `mattermost`, `slack`, `gitlab`, `github`, `jira`, `confluence`, `datadog`, `cloudflare`, `expo`, `digitalocean`, `scaleway`, `googlecloud`, `atlassian`, `aws`, `ssh` | The vendors. Each contributes only what is genuinely its own: a client, a parser, a transport, a prompt, a provisioning reconcile, and no more, which is why Jira has no transport (an agent writes through its own MCP tools) and why its reconcile and GitHub's report rather than mint (neither vendor issues a credential on a provisioner's behalf). Datadog is the ALERT one and the shape shows it: a monitor is addressed to nobody, so it routes on the monitor's own TAGS with a company-wide fallback seat, and its deliveries cannot be signed at all. Scaleway is the one whose provisioner reaches its whole Organization: no condition can keep a key that manages applications to the agents' applications, so what bounds an agent is its fixed level table and the ledger's application IDs, and the connect form says so. Google Cloud is the one with no provisioner credential to paste: the engine mints its own ES256 key, the owner uploads only its public half to a workload identity provider, and the provisioner it impersonates is contained by three conditions on each project, one hasOnly list per level, so every write touches one list; projects are discovered from those grants rather than listed, an agent's token is issued at use and never sealed, and every read after the engine's own write waits through one bound within which a stale answer is never drift. SCALEWAY, AWS AND GOOGLE CLOUD TAKE GRANTS BESIDE A LEVEL as Cloudflare does (each package's `grants.go`, `seataccess.Granter`), each bounded where its vendor bounds it: a Scaleway permission set rides the seat's own policy, never a second one; an AWS grant is an AWS managed policy attached under the seat boundary, which stack version 3 lets the provisioner attach and list; a Google Cloud grant is a role a project's GRANTS LIST names, one more hasOnly condition the owner adds, written one role at a time so one condition allows each write, and a list naming a role that could mint, act as another identity or grant access unoffers the project. SSH is the one with no vendor at all: the engine is the certificate authority, servers trust only its public line, and a seat's level and hosts are the document alone, so its access sync takes no step (ADR-0105). SSH IS ALSO THE ONE ATLAS SOURCE THAT RUNS COMMANDS (ADR-0134): on a server whose `inventory` is on, and on no other, the engine itself runs the closed set `config.SSHInventoryCommands`, each on a certificate of purpose `inventory` signed for that one command under the principal `crewlet.<host>.inventory`, which no seat's certificate carries and a server accepts only once its setup ran with the inventory on; the gate runs the command a certificate names, never the client's. `ParseOpenPorts` and `ParseContainers` read the columns they keep and no other, so a container's command line never leaves the parse, and `AtlasSource` is handed to the collect duty by the engine, with the node's runner, rather than registered from init, because every command it runs is recorded on the access log. Slack is the one with no reconciler (its apps are created from the command line), so it is registered teardown-only and carries no reconcile status. A vendor's PROMPT states only what the engine knows and the model cannot: why the seat is reading this, the item by the reference a person would paste, the vendor facts (mention markup, subscription mechanics, what a merge or a close means) and how a seat declines what it HOLDS on that surface (reassign the item assigned to it, hand a review request on), never offered to a seat asked about somebody else's item, which is not its to move, while "stay silent" is shown only to a seat the prompt's own `Addressed` reads as NOT asked. How to review a diff or work an alert is the model's own, and that an ask may not end in silence is the executor's contract and the decoder's refusal, so neither is restated per vendor Cloudflare is also THE FIRST ATLAS SOURCE (ADR-0129, `atlas.go`): the account's name, every zone, every A, AAAA and CNAME record and every certificate pack, read whole an hour under the provisioner token's own Read groups, TXT content never decoded; and its reconcile hands the seat access session the inspection it made (`Result.Inspection`, `seataccess.OpenInput.Reads`), so one pass lists the zones and permission groups once. ITS WORKERS, PAGES PROJECTS, R2 BUCKETS AND TUNNELS ARE READ BY NAME WHERE THE TOKEN HOLDS FOUR OPTIONAL READ GROUPS (ADR-0145, `atlasoptional.go`, `atlasPermissions`), which are in no level and no provisioner list: no agent's token carries one, a token without them is whole, and the connect check and the pass say a missing one as a suggestion (`seeMore`), never a refusal. Datadog is THE SECOND (ADR-0130, `atlas.go`, `inventory.go`): the organization, every monitor with its state, the service catalogue, the hosts and the Synthetic tests, each listing behind its own permission (`Listing`) and a refused one a finding; a monitor watches what its `service:` and `host:` TAGS name and nothing its query says, a test's monitor watches the address it requests, and what is watched is named, never claimed. The same `Listing` table is the credential check's fourth stage, a warning on a working verdict. `companyClient` is the one constructor the seat access session and the read share. GitHub's half of the ATLAS (ADR-0131) is read through the agents' own installations, the only access to GitHub the engine holds that reads: one token an installation, minted Read-only of what it holds among Metadata, Actions and Deployments and revoked when the read ends; a part no covering installation may read is unread FOR THAT REPOSITORY, an installation the read cannot be made as carries everything of a repository the read did not list, and the rate limit fails the read whole. A workflow and a deployment environment are pipelines keyed after their repository (`#workflow:`, `#environment:`, a name one exact key segment), what a deployment put its code behind is named and never claimed, and the rows decode no login, email, payload or description. GitLab's half (ADR-0135, `atlas.go`, `inventory.go`) is read with GET alone under `integrations.gitlab.token`, and under the provisioning token only where no read credential resolves: the group first, which proves the credential, then every project of it and of its subgroups and each project the settings name; a project's pipeline and its environments are parts PER PROJECT, a 403 or a 404 leaves one unread for that project, and a 401 after the group answered or the rate limit fails the read whole. A project is ONE pipeline (`#pipeline`, claimed once one finished on the default branch) and each available environment another (`#environment:`), the twenty nearest production by GitLab's own tier; a row decodes no person, description, runner token or import address. Google Cloud's and Scaleway's halves (ADR-0137, each package's `atlas.go`) are read with GET alone under the credential that already provisions the cloud: A PROJECT IS A `vendor_account`, keyed as the settings key the identity project and the Organization and named by the read only where the settings do not; an API enabled on a Google Cloud project says what the project is used for as a CLOSED capability word through `capabilityOf`, which leaves out every API a new project already has enabled, and the API's name is never a value; a project whose services Google refuses is a finding, as a 403 on Scaleway's one listing is, and the listing of projects itself proves the credential. `googlecloud.Client` WAITS OUT A READ'S 429 by `httpx.VendorRateLimit` AND NEVER A WRITE'S, since a create at a spent quota answers the same status. SCALEWAY'S PRODUCTS ARE READ UNDER AN APPLICATION OF THE ATLAS'S OWN (ADR-0144, `atlasapp.go`, `atlasproducts.go`), only where `integrations.scaleway.atlas` is on: the company pass mints it with the provisioner key (one application, one policy of nine named read-only sets on every project, one rotated key sealed under fixed names bound to no seat), never makes again one somebody deleted at Scaleway, and reports every trouble with it as an advisory, never a failed pass; each product is a part that can be refused alone, the decoders hold only the fields the atlas keeps, and no user data, kubeconfig, environment variable, endpoint or object is ever asked for or decoded. Atlassian's half (ADR-0138, `atlas.go`) is the one workspace listing under the organization key: A SITE IS A `vendor_account` keyed by its address's host, as the settings key the site Jira or Confluence works in, named by the read only where the settings name none there; A PRODUCT IS WHAT ITS ACCOUNT DOES, a closed capability by the first word of its key (`capabilityOf`), of its site's account or the organization's; nobody who created or owns a workspace is decoded, and a 403 on the listing is a finding. `atlassian.Client.Workspaces` reads the listing to its END by the cursor its next link is or carries, for `DiscoverSite` as well, and every call waits out a 429 by `httpx.VendorRateLimit`. Slack's and Mattermost's halves (ADR-0139, each package's `atlas.go`) say THE WORKSPACE'S OWN ID AND NAME on the account the settings already name, and nobody in it: one `auth.test`, or one GET of the team by its slug, under the bot token of the first agent by handle that is switched on (the provisioning token last at Mattermost); a token the vendor refuses is passed over, a workspace every credential is refused is a finding, and the bot user that asked, a team's contact address and its invite id are never decoded. A seat's access is its level PLUS ITS LIVE GRANTS (ADR-0150): one vendor permission each beside the level, stored in the seat's block of the document and saved through the Agents tab with the engine stamping who and when, never applied past its expiry, the loop armed at the earliest lapse (`integration.Paced`) and a sweep dropping lapsed grants once off every credential; a vendor takes them by setting `LaneSpec.Grants`, implementing `seataccess.Granter` (its live catalogue, its never-granted permissions left out) and reading them back in `Observe` (Cloudflare reads a token in the terms its last sync applied, so grants that make up a higher level never flap), and an agent's request for one (`request_access`) is a `seataccess.GrantRequest` on the coordination store (`coord.GrantRequests`). |
| `origin` | WHERE A FAILURE LEFT THE ENGINE'S OWN CODE: `defer origin.Stamp(&err)` wraps the first non-nil error with the deferring function's name, read off the runtime's frame and never the error, and only for a function under the module's `internal/` tree; its file is a base name. THE DEEPEST STAMP WINS, so a mid-level stamp is a fallback for failures nobody below stamped. TRANSPARENT: the message is the error's own and `errors.Is`/`errors.As` see through it, which is why a stamp goes only where every caller uses them, never `==` or a direct type assertion. A failed turn's record carries the stamp (`error_function`, `error_file`). The rejected alternatives (an error's type, a message's prefix, a program counter in every constructor) are in the package doc. |
| `issuereport` | Opt-in ANONYMOUS REPORTS of the engine's own defects, filed by a relay as GitHub issues (ADR-0101). THE ALLOWLIST IS A TYPE: `Report` holds no plain string, each is a named type a validating constructor makes, because a redactor over an error message fails open on the words a company writes, and `TestAReportHoldsNoPlainString` fails on a field added later. One `Catalogue` table, exported to `schema/issue-report-catalogue.json` for the relay to vendor. A class's shape is immutable after a reporting tag (`schema/issue-report-history/`), and a site is a stable token a test maps to a live function, so an older release's reports keep validating. Detection is a publish listener over the typed failure records, the dispatcher's dead-letter hook and every panic `panics` contains, its counters this node's memory, each subject hashed on arrival. A panic in a package that parses input before it is authenticated (`BeforeAuthentication`), or one served on an unguarded route, is reported with its site alone, never its frames. A CRASH IS THE NEXT PROCESS'S TO REPORT (`NextStart`): a copy of the applied setting beside the node's database arms the runtime's crash output, and the first revision the next start applies reads it (on) or deletes it unread (off), as it does a release's failed migration (`store.MigrationError`, closed values only); a crash with a frame in code an outsider or a model's arguments reach is held in the record (`held`), never sent. The SENT RECORD is `coord.IssueReports`, one compare-and-set record per fingerprint, because every node detects and one may send. It is off until a revision turns it on, and a revision that turns it off cancels a send under way. ONLY A BUILD ANYBODY CAN READ SENDS (`Origin`: a tag, a `main-` stamp, a module pseudo-version or a `go install`), because a development build's defect is often its developer's own unmerged change: any other binary stays inert with the setting on. AN OUTAGE IS NOT A DEFECT: the turn loop's `error` kind is what nothing classified, an environment condition being its own kind (`builtin.ConditionKind`), and a signal an outage can cause (`Signal.Shadowed`) counts nothing while an alarm in `ShadowAlarms` stands anywhere in the fleet, read every alarm beat off this node's tracker and every node's presence beat (a node without `data` evaluates none) and failing closed when it cannot be read; a hand-back (`Signal.HandBack`) counts nothing while a node drains or the live nodes run more than one build (`coord.NodeStatus.Build`). A panic is never shadowed. |
| `panics` | THE ONE WAY A CONTAINED PANIC IS READ: every `recover()` that keeps a defect from ending the process calls `Recovered`, which names the function that panicked from the frames under the LAST `runtime.gopanic` (the original, since a rollback that re-raises sits above it), its kind from the runtime error's type and fixed text (never the value's words), and the module's frames, and tells the observers (`Observe`, the issue reporter while its sender runs). A LEAF over the standard library, so the queue, the coordination store and the API can call it. A site that recovers only to re-raise does not call it, so each panic is read once. |
| `integration` | The app-neutral reconcile spine for external surfaces: the finding/phase vocabulary, the who-acts schedule, the fleet-singleton worker, the disconnect intent and the tool roll-up; `integrationtest` certifies every reconciler. |
| `capability` | What one agent seat can reach, as the seat is TOLD it: a pure snapshot of its access (each granted integration lane with its level in the vendor's words, GitHub's permissions in effect, those requested and not in effect yet, and those the seat's app holds outside the grant, its scope and tool state; the lanes not granted; its MCP servers' tools by level; the company's policies; its box setup steps by name and brief digest; the boxes its runs use, each with its cell, the memory it is held to and, where a run read it, whether it has a browser and a container runtime), the closed list of what counts as a change to it, the difference between two snapshots, and the two prompt sections that say them: "Your access" on every turn and "What changed since your last turn" once. BOTH SAY WHERE ACCESS IS DECIDED: Crewlet narrows every credential to the grant an administrator sets in Crewlet, so each line names that grant and the screen it is changed on (`seataccess.GrantPlace`), and what GitHub's app holds outside the grant is said but is never a change. CANONICAL JSON with a SHA-256 digest, so equal access is equal bytes on every node and the section is byte-stable while the access is. The baseline a change is measured against is what the seat was last told, never the previous revision, so many revisions are told as one, a revert as a change back and a rotation as nothing. NO I/O and NO SECRET: level words come from the vendors' fixed sets (`seataccess.KnownLevels`) or a live read the caller hands in, and a credential, a variable's value, a sealed name or a setup step's files, commands or environment never enter a snapshot |
| `fleetsecrets` | The company's credential store on the coordination KV: this package owns the sealing key, coordination owns the bytes; an operator view and a separate engine view over one bucket. |
| `compact` | Fits text to a size budget by having the seat's auxiliary model rewrite it, never by cutting it; refuses with an error rather than degrade to a cut. |
| `textcut` | Shortening a string on a rune boundary, with one marker and one rule for every cut: the last resort, never a way to fit text a model or person acts on |
| `activityline` | The executor activity transcript's line grammar: a numbered call, its whole input where cut, its timed result; written by `sandbox/executor`, read by `assistant` and the dashboard. |
| `tabular` | A table of rows as a DOWNLOAD, CSV and XLSX, one implementation of each (ADR-0008): a cell a spreadsheet would run as a formula (opening with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return, and not a plain number) is prefixed with an apostrophe in both, the rule moved out of `artifactdata`'s export so the datastore's export and the assistant's table card downloads share it; XLSX is minimal SpreadsheetML over `archive/zip` with the standard library alone; a table card downloads with its columns, rows, total and an `As of` row (`CSV`, `XLSX`, `Tables`), a cell past the format refused rather than cut, and a walk holds every CSV writer in the tree to this package |
| `named` | What a sentence the engine writes NAMES (a work item, a change, a commit, a page, a turn), carried beside its words as typed refs, each a kind, an id and the words that spell it, built by one builder so the two cannot drift. |
| `words` | Says a set in a sentence (`Or`, `And`, `AndMore`, `Said`) instead of Go's bracketed slice, and gates the module against brackets, hand-joined lists and em dashes in person-facing text. |
| `jsoncarry` | How a record round-trips through a build that only half understands it: a decode keeps the fields the struct has no home for and the next encode folds them back in; a carried field loses to a known one. A leaf over `encoding/json` and `reflect`. |
| `httprelay` | Carries one HTTP exchange from the node that received it to the process holding what answers it, over the broker's ephemeral request and reply, as signed, acknowledged frames. |
| `plainjson` | JSON READ AS TEXT, one encoder: `Marshal(v, indent)` writes `<`, `>` and `&` as themselves and ends where its value does, and is otherwise byte for byte `json.Marshal`'s (or `json.MarshalIndent`'s under an indent). `json.Marshal` escapes those three into `\u003c` and its kin so its output can sit inside an HTML document, and nothing here is one: a tool call's RECORD (`tools.RecordArgs`) is read by a person in a `pre`, where a URL argument read `?a=1\u0026b=2`; a TOOL ANSWER (the builtins, a delegated worker's report) and a PRIOR-WORK LINE (`agent/ledger`) are read by a model, which was shown `if a < b` as six-byte escapes it had to decode back and charged five bytes of context for each; and a harness run's transcript (`providers/llm/harness`) shows the model its own earlier calls, which it reads as written, so it was shown its own `<` as an escape; and a command's `-json` is read at a terminal, by a script and by the loop fixing a document from `crewlet validate -json`, so every one goes through ONE printer (`cmd/crewlet/jsonout.go`) — a node's answer DECODED, every number kept as the digits the node sent, and printed again, because the wire escapes at its one writer and a copy of its bytes is exactly those escapes — and a walk refuses another encoder there — and so is a tier SCHEMA (`config.Schema`, the committed `schema/*.schema.json`), whose descriptions an editor shows on hover and a model authors against. A LEAF over the standard library, held to it by a test, because its readers sit at heights no other package spans — the tool layer imports the agent runtime, MCP and coordination; the ledger is held by the turn context, the prompt builder and the API and imports nothing from crewlet but this; a provider backend sits below the tool registry — and kept in the tool layer the rule had to be copied into the ledger, so two copies of it sat a package apart with nothing comparing them, and the provider backend would have needed a third. `coord/kv`'s activation encoder also writes unescaped and stays separate on purpose: its contract is a payload embedded byte-equal to `coord.CanonicalPayload`, a fact about a wire rather than about how text reads — and so does `sandbox/executor`'s `jsonStringify`, which spells a server's name as OpenCode's own `JSON.stringify` printed it, U+2028 and U+2029 literal, which `encoding/json` always escapes, to find it in OpenCode's answer. And JSON that did NOT decode is read as text too (`Unreadable`): every surface that decodes a caller's JSON — a body, a query's JSON parameter, a tool call's arguments on every transport (`toolargs.Decode`) — says which field held what where something else belongs, or where the document stops being JSON, and never encoding/json's own sentence, which names Go's types and struct fields; a second value after the first is `ErrTrailing`, said the same way |
| `jsonscan` | ONE JSON TEXT READ A TOKEN AT A TIME, NEVER A STRING WHOLE: one value from an `io.Reader`, each name's and string's decoded text and each number's literal handed over through `Read` a piece at a time (never ending a read inside a UTF-8 sequence), `InvalidUTF8` to say whether that text as written held a byte no UTF-8 holds, which its decoded text cannot, `Skip` to pass a value over, `Capture` to hold one as written up to a bound its caller names, and `Depth` and `Offset` for a reader that models a byte-level load by where a key sits. Its readers are a kept session's (the take's scrub, the Claude Code thread layer, the context meter, a person's transcript), whose lines reach 56 MB on one whole-PDF read and have no bound at all on a prompt. NOT jsontext AND NOT encoding/json, because both hand a string over as one token, held whole before its first byte reaches the caller, so every reader on either carried a bound of its own past which it refused a session or read nothing. ONE GRAMMAR, BOTH DECODERS': differential fuzzing holds it to `json.Valid` and a `UseNumber` decode, to jsontext under the thread layer's options (a name written twice allowed, invalid UTF-8 and a lone surrogate read as U+FFFD, 10,000 levels and no more), and the two decoders to each other. They agree everywhere today (in Go 1.27's default build encoding/json is built on jsontext; doc.go says why no other build is held), and WHERE THEY EVER PART THE SCANNER FOLLOWS JSONTEXT, never encoding/json: the thread layer's acceptance must not move by a line, and its digest of a tool result's decoded text is compared with a log's output, so encoding/json's side would come as an option a reader asks for. IT VALIDATES WHAT IT PASSES OVER, every byte of a skipped media string included: a reader moved onto it keeps the acceptance it had, and a reader that decides from a line has to know the line was one value. A refusal is a typed `*Error`, one of five faults (no value, cut, a refused byte, more after the value, too deep) at its offset, kept apart because the thread layer reads each as a different line of Claude Code's load (a leading NUL or byte order mark is a refused byte at offset 0, told apart by the character it begins). Its memory is 67,072 bytes whatever the input (a 64 KiB buffer, and a struct holding the open containers at one bit each), kept across lines by `Reset` and held by a test at 64 MiB strings, names, numbers and blanks and at 10,000 levels; a `Capture` that outlasts one fill of the buffer adds what it holds, at most its bound, in a buffer the scanner keeps for its life, `Reset` included, and reuses, so a reader that once captured at a bound holds that much more from then on. A LEAF over the standard library, held to it by a test |
| `kept` | WHAT A RECORD KEEPS OF A PAYLOAD TOO LARGE TO KEEP WHOLE, and the bytes a value weighs as the record holds it. A record that is ONE JSON value in a store held to a byte ceiling (a run's row or its call log in the coordination store, each held to what one key of it holds, `coord.MaxValueBytes`) is bounded in what its ENCODER writes, and the encoder escapes: `<`, `>`, `&`, U+2028/9 and a control byte are six bytes, a quote two, and a call's arguments, which the record holds as JSON text inside a string, are escaped twice. `Len` mirrors `encoding/json`'s escape rune for rune (HTML escaping on), exact for valid UTF-8 and never short for an invalid byte, which the encoder writes in three bytes or in six by context (a test holds both halves to `json.Marshal`), so a cut is MEASURED, never estimated with a six-fold multiplier. Two shapes of cut, each SAID in the text for a reader of the text alone and sized beside it for a reader that decides: `Record.Text` keeps an opening on a rune boundary and ends `…` and `(N of M not kept on <record>)`; `Record.Args` keeps a call's arguments' SHAPE, leaving out the largest LEAVES first (then containers, then the whole as `{"not_kept": marker}`), each replaced by `(size not kept on <record>, digest xxxxxxxx)`, `compact.Omitted`'s shape, so an identifier beside a body stays whole and two left-out values are told apart. Never a fragment that reads as the whole, the cut `compact` and `textcut` were written to remove. A reader that COMPARES two calls' arguments recognises what a record left out (`IsLeftOut`: the marker under ANY record's name, its size as `Size` writes one and its digest as `LeftOut` does, never a list of names a new record would be missing from; `IsNotKept`: the whole's one-key object) and reads it as a value that may have been anything, since compared as the text it is, a marker reads apart two calls whose left-out values may have been one. A size a refusal states against a bound is `SizeUp`, `Size`'s units rounded UP, so a value one byte past a bound of whole KiB never reads as the bound itself. A LEAF over the standard library and `plainjson`, held to it by a test, because its two writers sit at heights neither imports the other from: `sandbox` (a run's row, rowbytes.go) and `agent/execstate` (a parked turn condensed to its share of that row). |
| `toolname` | Declares once the engine's own tool names that a package must spell but cannot import from the tool's owner; a leaf, with a suite walking the module's string literals for copies. |
| `toolname/toolnametest` | TEST-ONLY: the set of tool names `toolname`'s walk holds every string literal to, shared with the suites that hold a tool surface to it. |
| `httpx` | The ONE outbound HTTP transport. Leaving `http.Client.Transport` nil is not "the defaults": it is `http.DefaultTransport`, whose `MaxIdleConnsPerHost` is **2, process-wide**, which seven vendor clients were silently sharing. One transport rather than one per client: a per-client clone is strictly worse than the default, since each keeps a private pool and reuses nothing between them. EVERY outbound client is built here, the operator CLI's included; a bare `&http.Client{}` outside this package is the bug, and a round tripper that wraps another takes `httpx.Transport()` as its base, which is the shape that slips through because it names no client at all. `Customized` is for a client whose TLS or protocol genuinely differs — the bucket's, over HTTP/1.1 alone. And the ONE PUBLIC-ADDRESS GUARD on the ONE PUBLIC-ADDRESS RULE (ADR-0072): a URL a remote party named (a page, a server's metadata, a redirect, a file an artifact names on a CDN) is sent only through `httpx.Guarded`, whose dialer judges the address each socket opens, never the name — in its `net.Dialer`'s `ControlContext`, before the connect, against a `Rule` made for that dial on the dialer's own resolver — so a resolver answering twice slips nothing past it, `net.Dialer` still resolves the name, races its address families and splits its bound across them, and a refused address is sent nothing (`ErrNotPublic`, the one error every door refuses with). A proxy the operator set is dialled as itself and only an address written into the URL is judged before it, since the proxy resolves a name on its own. An address the OPERATOR configured stays on `httpx.Client`, because a private one there is the deployment they chose. The rule has three doors: `NewRule(resolver)`, the `Rule` on the network of the resolver a host is resolved with (the zero `Rule`, and a nil one, refuse every address), whose `Public(ctx, addr)` the guard asks of each address it connects to; `ResolvePublic(ctx, resolver, host)`, for a dialer that hands an address to another program to connect to (Gitlet's git), which returns a host's addresses only when every one is public; and `Public(ip)`, for a caller holding an address and making no dial (the configuration's validation, `github.Deliverable`), the verdict on a network that names no NAT64 prefix. An IPv4 address is public unless it is in a block IANA's special-purpose registry reserves (private, loopback, link-local with the cloud metadata address, carrier-grade NAT, documentation, benchmarking, the deprecated 6to4 relay anycast) or multicast, and one written in IPv6 form is judged as itself. An IPv6 address that carries a zone (`fe80::1%eth0`) is refused before any block is asked: no host on the internet is named with one, and netip's own predicates judge a zoned address as if it had none, so a rule judging its bits alone would step around every block. An IPv6 address in a NAT64 prefix is judged by the IPv4 address it carries and by nothing else: the well-known `64:ff9b::/96` always, and the network-specific prefixes RFC 7050 discovery reads from the same resolver's answer for `ipv4only.arpa`. That lookup is made at most once a dial or a resolve, never kept, and ONLY for an address whose verdict its answer can change: an IPv6 one in global unicast or in `64:ff9b:1::/48`, where a named prefix counts (below). No record means none; any other failure refuses each address that needed the answer, with the resolver's error, so the rule is never applied on a guess. An IPv4 address, one in the well-known prefix and one the allowlist refuses elsewhere are judged without it, so an IPv4-only host costs no lookup beyond its own and is never refused for a lookup it did not need; `ResolvePublic` refuses a host one of whose addresses is not public with `ErrNotPublic` even when another could not be judged, whatever order the resolver answers in. A prefix the resolver names counts only where its word cannot widen the rule into a network that is not on the internet: inside the allowlist below, where judging an address by what it carries can only refuse more, or inside `64:ff9b:1::/48`, the block RFC 8215 reserves for a network's own translation prefix. Named anywhere else (unique-local, link-local, documentation, `::/96`) it is ignored and its addresses stay refused, so a DNS64 network translates through `64:ff9b::/96`, a prefix in `64:ff9b:1::/48` or one in its own global unicast space, or its dialer reaches the host through a proxy its caller trusts: through a translator on a unique-local prefix the rule reaches no host that has only an IPv4 address. Any other IPv6 address is public ONLY in global unicast (`2000::/3`) outside `2001::/23`, the two documentation blocks and 6to4: an ALLOWLIST, because a list of reserved IPv6 blocks cannot be completed (IANA keeps reserving more) and missed every form that carries an IPv4 address (NAT64, 6to4, Teredo, IPv4-compatible). Every caller that refuses an address asks it rather than keeping a list of its own, which would drift from the others unnoticed. And the ONE RATE-LIMIT RULE a vendor client waits out a `429` by (`RateLimit`, `VendorRateLimit`): the vendor's answer is the only count that is right, since its limit is shared with every node and every other tool on the account, so a client keeps no tally and waits only when told; a wait is taken in the call only within three tries, thirty seconds and the caller's deadline, and anything longer is handed back for the caller's own schedule. `RetryAfter` reads the standard header; each vendor's own reset header stays in its client |
| `browser` | The oldest Chromium major the engine accepts and the suite certifies (`MinChromiumMajor`), and the gate that no Go but the artifact render's client names a DevTools method. |
| `browser/chromiumtest` | Test support: the two pinned Chromiums a case drives (`Newest`, `Floor`, `Each`), how one is launched, and what its protocol describes (`Methods`), never the machine's own Chrome. |
| `sigv4` | AWS request signing, a leaf on the standard library: Signature Version 4 with an access key, and the IAM Roles Anywhere form that signs THE SAME canonical request with an X.509 certificate's RSA or ECDSA key (`SignX509`). Its own because AWS ships the X.509 form (CreateSession) in no SDK and its one published client needs cgo for PKCS #11 and TPM, which a `CGO_ENABLED=0` build cannot link; one canonical request for both keeps the two from drifting. It also signs each request to AWS's MCP Server, which takes no bearer header. Held to AWS's published test suite (`testdata/aws4_testsuite`, Apache 2.0 with AWS's NOTICE) and the Roles Anywhere guide's own example; the SDK's signer stays wherever the SDK sends the request |
| `modelcatalog` | What a model step SUGGESTS: each harness vendor's models, read from models.dev's public, keyless catalogue (`https://models.dev/api.json`, the one OpenCode reads). NOT A MODEL VENDOR and never handed a credential, so "no HTTP client to a model vendor" holds and no key leaves the engine to name models; whether a plan reaches a model it lists is the test call's answer. THIS NODE'S CACHE of a public document, in memory: fresh for 24 h (`Fresh`), then revalidated with `If-None-Match`; a failed fetch keeps the last copy and says how old it is, with no copy the answer is `source: none` and its reason, and a failure is not retried for 10 minutes (`RetryAfter`) so a blackholed egress costs one 30 s wait (`FetchBudget`) per window rather than one per page view. 16 MiB cap on a body of some 6 MB |
| `api/httpjson` | How every JSON surface answers: the refusal envelope, 503/429 with `Retry-After`, immutable answers, strict body reading, the engine header. |
| `api/opkey` | The HTTP half of an operation id: the `Idempotency-Key` header, the rule a caller's key is held to, and the request digest and canonical encoding that bind a key to the request that asked for it |
| `api/paced` | The per-mebibyte pace a client's connection is held to, both ways, in one package for every streaming surface (project files, artifacts, Gitlet transfers): `Body`, `Writer`, `Send`. |
| `api/pagepolicy` | The browser-facing response policy: Content-Security-Policy, framing, sniffing, referrer and HSTS headers for the API, the dashboard, the artifact origin and repository downloads, plus the one gzip negotiation. |
| `artifactupload` | How bytes become an artifact version: upload intents, reservations, the transfer legs, the verified finalize and the one publish pipeline every surface calls. |
| `artifactupload/edits`, `artifactupload/lint`, `artifactupload/vendor` | Pure exactly-once `old_str`/`new_str` edits against the base version, overlapping matches counted; the publish-time policy linter (allowlisted script and stylesheet hosts at exact versions, the nonce placeholder written into every script element and the rewritten page verified by a second tokenize, inline handler digests, eval flags, remote module imports refused, every import map entry on another origin copied in or refused, warnings); and allowlisted CDN files fetched once per node over https through `httpx.Guarded`, judging each address at the socket `net.Dialer` opens for it with the network's `httpx.Rule` (its NAT64 prefixes asked of the resolver the dialer resolves the CDN's name with, at most once a dial and at the socket of the first address whose verdict they can change, so an IPv4 address waits on no lookup and a failed lookup refuses only the addresses that needed it), so a name's IPv6 and IPv4 addresses still race, an address that never answers costs a share of the dial's bound rather than all of it, and a name answering both ways is dialed at its public addresses alone, integrity-checked, then copied into the artifact's own version, so a view fetches nothing third-party and no object is shared across artifacts |
| `artifactserve` | The artifact ORIGIN, the `api.artifacts` listener, and the frame builder the API mints a viewer's frame through: five routes (a ticket's file, its artifact's asset, the embedded runtime and kit, and the adopted brand's files by hash, served only while an adoption names them) and nothing else, no guard, no session, no cookie read or written (a source walk holds the package to it), no API route, ever. A path-borne view ticket names one principal, one immutable version, the CREDENTIAL it was minted through and the artifact's access epoch; in either origin mode the request's host must be the one the ticket's artifact is served on, and a top-level open is refused. DOCUMENTS ARE DECIDED ON EVERY REQUEST and never read a remembered verdict: the principal re-composed from the identity rows by `api/auth`'s `Lineages` (the guard's own arms, over the credential's NAME), the artifact read through the estate router bounded by the stall grace, `get_artifact` asked again; a subresource rides its document's verdict at most thirty seconds, forgotten the moment this node's applier reports the artifact moved. Anything that cannot be vouched for (a stale or unreachable copy, an identity copy past the grace, a copy older than the ticket, an undecided chart) is a 503 with `Retry-After`, never a page. A real origin only on a site of its own (config's site rule); the version's pinned runtime placed before every script of each HTML page (past its doctype, a bounded scan and never a parse), and the brand stylesheet the version pinned linked after it unless the page's `<html>` opts out; a strict script policy by a fresh nonce substituted per response into the linter's placeholder; every byte read through the object store's checking reader, ranges by length; documents and small gzips kept per node by digest; every write at the object store's pace. `artifactserve/ticket` mints and opens the tickets |
| `artifactrender` | A published version rendered as a viewer sees it, for a seat checking its own page (`render_artifact`): an operator-run Chromium (Tier A `artifacts.render.browser_url`) driven over `artifactrender/internal/cdp`, every request answered in process by `artifactserve` over the node's own origin, the frame minted through a render-only credential (ADR-0035). Reports unresolved custom properties, see-through or covering bars, console lines, failed requests and broken links; writes nothing; bounded by `artifacts`' Render constants. |
| `artifactrender/internal/cdp` | The artifact render's Chrome DevTools Protocol client: one websocket to a browser, every session flat on it, declaring only the commands and events the render sends and reads. |
| `presence` | Who is in each room and where. A ROOM IS ITS KIND AND ITS ID (`Room`: an artifact's room `RoomArtifact`, a chat room's `RoomChat`, ADR-0038), and the vocabulary — `Member`, `Activity`, a `Focus` checked per kind, `Roster`, `Counts` and every timing below — is this package's rather than the artifacts', because what a kind owns is only where its members can look and who may be in its rooms. TOLD, NEVER STORED: rosters in the memory of the node holding the sockets, crossed between nodes on the queue's `Broadcast`/`Listen` (no row, no log record, no coordination key; the hub's broker seam has no durable verb at all) under `crewlet.rooms.<kind>.<id>` (never `crewlet.presence.`, the hub's metric namespace), every frame sealed ONE WAY under the keyring (`signedask.OneWay`, key domain `presence`, the room and a 60-second expiry inside the seal) because it names a person, so a frame the fleet did not seal shows nobody. A node listens on a room only while one of its SOCKETS is in it (one reconciler goroutine listens and withdraws), fans each room out to its sockets at most ten times a second and tells the fleet its own members' moves at most twenty times a second (one short window on each side, so a cursor crosses nodes within 150 ms), re-presents at most ten members a frame (the change waiting longest first, so nobody starves and a socket's bytes stay bounded), rebroadcasts its members in full every 15 s (a peer replaces what it held from that node, so a lost leave costs one rebroadcast), expires a peer's member unheard for 45 s, asks its peers once when it gains a room's first socket and applies each answer as it arrives (`AskEach`), and tells the fleet its members left when it stops. Large rooms present the fifty most recently active and count the rest by kind, back to everybody only below forty. A room's fan-outs reach the sinks ONE AT A TIME IN THE ORDER THEY WERE TAKEN (`delivering`, the one lock held across a sink, taken before the hub's own), and a joiner is handed the room AS THIS NODE'S SOCKETS WERE LAST SHOWN IT, itself beside them (`rosterFor`), because each fan-out is the difference from that baseline: handed the room as it presented at the join, a member who came and went between two fan-outs stayed on the joiner's screen for good, and two fan-outs handed over out of order left a member who had gone. And it is handed IN THAT SAME ORDER: `Join` takes a `Hand` and runs it under `delivering`, the hand placing the socket where the surface's own sink finds it and sending it the roster (a hand that declines undoes the join, `ErrDeclined`), so no fan-out taken after a join reaches the socket before its roster and none taken before it is lost — a roster answered as a return value was sent whenever its caller got to it, and an artifact room's joiner was put back to an older room by the answer to its own join; a caller never joins holding a lock its hand or its sink takes. An agent's tool call TOUCHES the room (present as an agent, viewing or editing, for 45 s after its last call) and never makes its node listen; in a CHAT room an agent holds one member per thread it answers in (`Focus.Thread`), naming its turn and the phrase the room shows (`Agent.Status`) — the working indicator IS that member — and is taken out at once by `Withdraw` when the turn lets go rather than lingering. A chat room's typing member LAPSES: editing is put back to viewing `TypingLapse` (6 s) after nothing re-asserted it, because a lid closed mid-word must not leave somebody typing for 45 s, while an artifact's editing member stands until it changes. Who may be in a room is the socket's decision, never the hub's |
| `provenance` | The one fence around text a model reads that somebody else wrote: a per-turn boundary, an author label and a trust level, one trust rule per surface, and a walk that holds every model call and every declared field to it. A leaf. |
| `trashreap` | The trash reaper duty: purges what has been trashed past its term, as the system, for every domain that keeps a trash (the tracker's removed work items first, then pages, then artifacts), under derived operation ids so a moved duty never purges twice |
| `objectives` | Service objectives as named constants with their reasons (publish success and latency, served first byte, browser-reported ticket-to-ready, datastore round trip and resolution, change visibility, wake latency, best-effort presence), each an EVENT objective over thirty days whose good event is answered without fault within its bound (`Objective.Good`), so an alarm borrows its threshold rather than inventing one (ADR-0015). The `Meter` every recording site calls (the publish pipeline, the artifact origin, the datastore writer, the socket's push of a committed artifact, datastore or chat change, the change feed's every published wake, and the presence hub's joins and peers' moves) classifies once and counts twice from that one call: `crewlet.objectives.events{objective, outcome}` into the engine's recorder, and a minute ledger over six hours the multi-window burn rates are read from (1 h and 5 m at 14.4, 6 h and 30 m at 6, at least two bad events), which `objective_burn` raises. An event stamped by ANOTHER clock — the broker's instant for a stored record, a peer's for a member's move — is recorded through `RecordSince`, which ends it on the meter's own clock and CLAMPS a negative span to zero, since a start a little after the end is the clocks' skew rather than a fault. The `Beacon` admits the dashboard's ready reports (`POST /artifacts/ready`): one per frame, never longer than the frame has existed, a sustained one a second per credential |
| `api/artifactsapi`, `api/artifactdataapi` | The HTTP surfaces for artifacts and for their datastores on the API listener, every route stating its authority where it is mounted. A caller with no relation is answered exactly as an unknown id. Writes carry an Idempotency-Key (datastore writes too, never socket frames); a publish names its base version and a concurrent one is refused with the live version to merge onto. THE API ORIGIN NEVER RENDERS AN ARTIFACT: raw bytes here are attachments, a doc's source is JSON, and a page is opened only as a frame on the artifact origin. ONE ADMISSION (`artifactsapi.Gate`) for the artifacts' and the datastore's routes and read questions alike: it reads the head, decides the verb on its owner and shares before anything else is read, stamps the exact role into the write, and records an elevated read before the admin path's read is answered, refusing it when the record cannot be written. A TRASHED artifact's content is `artifact_trashed` — `409` on every route, the same code on every question (`queries.ErrArtifactTrashed`) and on a room's join and withdrawal — to a reader it is disclosed to, and `not_found` only to one with no relation, the admission deciding before the trash is looked at so nothing about existence leaks; a PURGED one stays `not_found` on the questions and `410 data_gone` on the routes, because a purge is permanent and the trash is a state a restore ends |
| `api/datasetsapi` | A dashboard artifact's datasets, answered as their viewer, plus the engine source catalogue |
| `artifactspike` | Test-only: the artifacts capacity spikes as reproducible benchmarks over the shipped components; they gate nothing and run alone |
| `setup` | What an integration needs before it works, in one vocabulary every vendor and the dashboard share: requirements, the secret-then-pointer-then-epoch write order, vendor passes and credential checks |
| `jsprovision` | The one place that decides how long a replicated JetStream create gets, how a forming cluster is re-asked, and how metadata reads are hedged; shared by `queue/jetstream` and `coord/kv`. |
| `jsinflight` | Waits out a conditional JetStream publish refused as in flight (10164), which decides nothing, so it is never read as a lost compare-and-set race (10071). |
| `jsapi` | Which JetStream API a connection speaks (embedded fleet domain vs external cluster), decided once; leader reads, hedged listings, pulls and write budgets for the JetStream clients. |
| `jwks` | The one cached, singleflight reader of a published JSON Web Key Set (RSA keys only): what the Forge relay verifies Atlassian's signed token against, and the one place that decides what a key set is trusted to say. |
| `logging`, `secrets`, `provision`, `envref`, `envfile`, `redact`, `workkey`, `backoff`, `cron`, `period` | The small shared grammars. Each imports nothing from the rest of the engine but others in this row and `words`, a leaf over the standard library, `secrets` reads `envref`, `redact` and `words`, `logging` reads `redact` (every record passes it on its way to any destination, so a credential a call site did not know it logged reaches neither the console nor the file), and `provision` reads `envfile`, `envref` and `secrets`, which is what lets `config` itself depend on them, and what let the org model validate a schedule's cron with the parser the scheduler evaluates it with, where it had counted the fields because the parser sat in `schedule`, above it. `secrets` seals TWO SHAPES under one keyring: the envelope (`Cipher`, a whole value in one AES-GCM call, base64, for a field) and the SEALED STREAM (`StreamSealer`, STREAM over AES-256-GCM in 64 KiB chunks under a per-object HKDF key, its associated data the caller's, the chunk's index and a final flag, so truncation, reordering and extension all fail as `ErrDecrypt` — and what it hands out before the cut is provisional until `io.EOF`), because a value of tens of mebibytes — a harness's captured session — sealed whole held five times itself in memory, where streamed it holds two chunk buffers a side. `secrets` is also where WHICH FIELDS HOLD A CREDENTIAL is decided, once: a `secret:"true"` tag on the field, `secret:"content"` for a BODY, a setup step's file, read whole at launch through `secrets.ReadContent`, read through `secrets.Field` and shown by `secrets.Mask`, because a tag is maintained by declaring it where a list of paths is maintained by whoever remembers it exists. It also holds THE SIZE OF A VALUE, `secrets.MaxValueBytes`, which every store asks `secrets.CheckValue` at the write as it asks `CheckName` and `CheckSource`: one limit rather than the API route's alone, because a `crewlet secrets set` run with the engine stopped writes the fleet's store directly, with no route in front of it, and every reader of a revealed value through a node is sized from this one number, so a store that took more could hold a credential a reveal through a node cannot give back. `provision` carries a run's ask for a fresh value SPELLED AS ITS CALLER SPELLS IT (`provision.FreshSwitch`: `-recreate-webhooks` from the command line, `recreate_webhooks` from the setup route), one value rather than a bool with a name beside it, because `provision.RefuseFresh` (the refusal of that switch over a value the document owns, a literal with no variable to mint into) names the switch as one of the two ways on, and a pass that spelled the switch itself could spell only one surface's, so a caller of the setup route who sent `recreate_webhooks` would be told to re-run with a flag that surface does not have. `period` is the company CALENDAR, the day, the ISO week and the month a moment falls in on the company's clock, and a window's identity is its LABEL (`2026-09-23`, `2026-W39`, `2026-09`) rather than an instant, because a label is what every node computes alike, what a counter rolls over by comparing, and what sorts as a string. A day begins at the FIRST instant of its date, which `time.Date` alone gets wrong in the zones that move their clocks across midnight (Santiago resolves to the evening before, Amman to the second of two midnights); the relative date tokens (`today`, `eow`, `+7d`, a date, an instant) are RESOLVED here (`Resolve`), so the tracker's filters, the act tool that writes a due date and the assistant's preview of that write read a token one way, and there is one calendar rather than a copy. `redact.OwnWords` is a redacted text's own words, its stretches outside every marker the pass writes: what a search of a redacted text reads, so a word only a marker spells (`password`, `token`) is never a hit that confirms what the pass took out; and `redact.MarkersAround` is where a cut moves so no reader is handed a marker in part. `redact.Stream` redacts a text that arrives in pieces in one pass and BOUNDED MEMORY WHATEVER THE TEXT, writing exactly what `redact.Secrets` returns for the whole however it is cut, or REFUSING it (`redact.ErrDecidedEarly`, whose partial output the caller neither keeps nor shows): its keys' stage decides a window once the text has run the claims' lag past it (`holdBytes`, about 5.3 MiB) whatever its claims say, keeps the footprint of what each window so decided EARLY asked of the claims, and stops the stream where a claim then comes, or ends, otherwise over it, which only a password's key or an AWS secret's quoted key that its reader decides past the lag can, beside a key's armour (a Google token's end before dots that run on past the lag comes late too, set only once they stop, but a window that read the token open read it otherwise only over the dots, which is all that end is checked over); whether a text is refused is the text's alone, never how it is cut, and an ordinary text decides no window early: the passes `Secrets` makes run as stages, each STREAMED OVER ITS OWN INPUT in a window of `MaxReach` either side of a step, deciding with the whole-text code and trusting only what the window holds, carrying the rule's sequence and an open run across windows rather than holding the run, and the rules run a second time ahead of the keys' blocks for what they claim, each such stage holding markers that stand for more than a step of the text DECIDING UP TO THE LAST MARKER IT HOLDS, since no token's or AWS secret's read crosses a marker's `[` (the password's reader excepted, whose reads do); the stages are handed the text at its own offsets, a piece at a time, so what each holds is the text's alone however it is cut; its writes are cut at any byte, inside a rune too. `Secrets` itself reads its text WHOLE at any length, because its caller holds the text already and the stages' windows only cost it (3.4 to 4.4 times the time, and a copy of every window): the stream's tests hold the two alike, every window's edge at each byte around the shapes whose reads run furthest. |
| `gitref` | The grammar of git names the engine and its config share: repository names, refs, branches, protection patterns, path globs, remote URLs, commit text and tag objects, standard library only (ADR-0008). |
| `regexlit`, `regexlit/regexlittest` | WHAT A REGULAR EXPRESSION'S MATCHES CANNOT DO WITHOUT, read off the expression's own syntax tree, for every scan that skips a text the expression provably does not match. `Required` derives a `Prefilter` — CLAUSES, each a set of substrings every match contains one of — so a text failing a clause is skipped without running the expression, and the verdict is the same set of matches. Go's regexp jumps only to a literal PREFIX and a gate's pattern almost never has one (`\bADR-`, `(?m)^func`, `(?i)…`, an alternation), so the matcher stepped through every byte, at about a megabyte a second under the race detector, where a substring search is SIMD. `LineLocal` says when the expression may then run on the admitted lines alone. `Terms` hands each clause over with how each substring is compared, for a caller that looks them up in an index of what each text holds rather than in a text, and so makes `Admits`'s comparison itself. Folded literals are compared on the text's Unicode LOWERING, not an ASCII-only one, which misses `(?i)` across É and é; a rune the regexp folds onto an ASCII letter without lowering to it is an ESCAPE (`HasEscape`: DERIVED from the Unicode tables, U+017F alone today, while the Kelvin sign lowers to `k` and needs nothing) that admits a text outright whenever a clause compares folded; and a rune whose orbit lowers inconsistently (σ, ς, Σ) cuts a literal. The derivation may admit too much and never too little, which its suite certifies with matches GENERATED from each pattern's own syntax tree and by exhaustion over every rune. ONE derivation rather than a literal list beside each pattern, because a hand list drifts in the unsafe direction: the withdrawn-vocabulary gate's lowered a line with `strings.ToLower` and passed a withdrawn name spelled with a long s. A package of its own, moved out of `sourcetree`, because Gitlet's code search plans a pattern from the same substrings and production code does not import `sourcetree`, which imports `testing` and finds a source tree that does not exist where the engine runs — and a second derivation written for that caller is the copy ADR-0008 exists to prevent. `regexlittest` is the generator a suite certifying such a derivation draws on: expressions from the caller's own vocabulary, and strings an expression MATCHES, written from its own syntax tree — a member of a folded rune's case orbit, a class's rune or a byte that is not UTF-8, a branch, a count — because random text almost never matches a pattern, so a property checked over it holds vacuously. ONE generator, lifted out of this package's suite and shared with `codeindex`'s, because a copy that stopped writing the long s would leave its suite passing without meeting the case it exists for |
| `skipgate` | THE ENFORCEMENT BEHIND "a skip is not a pass", which was a convention held by memory and had already decayed twice. It reads `go test -json` on both test pipelines, renders it back so a CI log is unchanged, and fails on a skip nothing declared — plus it carries the run's own verdict, because in a pipeline `go test`'s exit status is gone by the time make looks. The allowlist is keyed on the test's own NAME with a reason per entry rather than being a count, because a count goes green when one skip is fixed and another appears. Two-sided where it can be: an `Always` entry that stopped firing is stale and fails, judged by its TEST and never by its package alone — its test reported a result that was not a skip, or a passing run of every test of its package never ran it (renamed or deleted; a package that failed may have died before it, or under it, which leaves a test that started and never reported, so a `run` record alone judges nothing) — because each half of the partition covers half the packages and a `-run` covers part of one, and judging by the package failed every targeted run of a package that declares an entry. Whether a run is narrowed is read from the command it runs (`-run`/`-skip`, and GOFLAGS): the stream names the tests that ran, never the ones a filter left out. `-timings FILE` writes how long each passing package took and the CPU its test binary used — read at the binary, since go test reports no package's CPU: with `-timings` the gate hands the `go test` it runs `-exec '<itself> exec'`, whose wrapper runs each binary, hands it go test's signals, appends its directory and CPU to a file the gate reads, and exits as the binary did (cpu.go) — (the partition's weights: CPU for the CPU-bound parallel half, wall time for the solo half), and under it each of its top-level tests that passed or skipped (the one record of which tests a package's time goes to; a subtest is inside its parent) — read from the records it already judges, and written whatever the verdict; a failed package's time, its tests' with it, is left out because it stops wherever the failure stopped it. Every run ends with the CPU the command and the test binaries it waited for used, against the wall time and the CPUs the process may run on, read from the exited command's own accounting: how a CI log tells a CPU-bound shard from one that waits; then the 25 slowest tests and subtests, the one place a subtest's time is seen |
| `fixedwait` | The gate that refuses a new fixed wait or elapsed-time reading in test code that no marker, bubble or inventory entry covers. |
| `clocktest` | Forges instants whose wall and monotonic clock readings have parted (a host that slept, a wall clock stepped), for tests of code that measures time across them. Imported only by tests |
| `solo` | Declares a test package must have the runner to itself; `solo/partition` computes `make test`'s and `make test-solo`'s halves and CI shards from the imports, and a roster guard fails both ways |
| `prereqtest` | Test support: one rule for a program the suite needs that a machine may lack (fail under the make targets, skip in a bare `go test`), plus pin-file reading. |
| `e2e` | The gates only a whole running node can answer: a real engine, API and scripted-model turn, with the dashboard's own protocol module reading the frames that come out; no exported surface, runs in `make test-solo` |
| `docsgate` | Test-only gates holding the tree's markdown links, anchors, docs index, coordination-bucket inventories and the always-loaded agent instructions to the files and code they name |
| `commentgate` | Test-only: holds every comment block outside a package doc to 12 lines and every row of this file to one line, each against an inventory of older code that only shrinks (#1474) |
| `wiregate` | Test-only: every flag and number a client receives is sent at zero or its field says why it may be left out, and the dashboard's wire types declare each as it is sent, each against an inventory of older code that only shrinks |
| `docslinks` | Test-only, like `docsgate`, and it certifies the links INTO the documentation site rather than between files: every address on the docs site that this repository carries — in the docs and the skill it publishes, the dashboard a person clicks through, the schemas an editor resolves, the code and the process files — names a page the site builds under its product and version segment, because the site forwards no path it used to serve and a link outside that shape is a 404 however it once resolved. It lives under `internal/` because the site copies `schema/` and `skills/` verbatim and serves them, so beside the skill it guarded it was itself published |
| `clientsource` | Finds a declaration the dashboard makes, by name and syntax rather than path or layout, so a gate can hold it against the engine's own copy |
| `sourcetree` | Where this repository's tree is for everything that reads its own files: the module root and a walk that skips VCS metadata, nested checkouts and `node_modules`; plus the module's one type-check loader for gates. |
| `sourcetree/decltype` | Types a Go expression from the declarations it reaches, read from source with no type checker: the one walk every static gate that judges a value by its type is built on |
| `scenario` | Test-only: SYNTHETIC TIMELINES of a company's work (who files, comments, asks, answers, assigns and moves which item, and which agent seats each step must wake), each reproducing the shape of an operating pattern measured on a running company and nothing else. Every handle, key and word in a fixture comes from a closed vocabulary that `CheckTree` holds every declared fixture directory to, so a measured pattern reaches this repository as a shape and never as a company's data. The package decides nothing and imports no engine package: a seam a timeline exercises drives it through its own real writers in its own tests (the tracker's `TestTheHealthyCompanyWakesEverySeatItsWorkNeeds` runs the healthy-company control, the hand-offs any routing change must keep waking, and `TestEveryEngineDetectorFiresOnItsOwnTimelineAlone` holds each of the advisor's evidence detectors to firing on its own pattern's timeline and on no other). `DocumentVocabulary` holds the advisor's company document fixtures to a closed word list the same way. |
| `conflictmarkers` | A test-only package — what it certifies is content rather than code, as `internal/docslinks` does for the docs' links — that FAILS THE BUILD on a file in this tree still carrying a merge conflict's markers. A Go file with one fails to compile, the one place a marker is loud; everywhere else it is silent — a docs page renders it as prose and the site publishes it, a YAML or SQL comment swallows it, the committed dashboard bundle ships it — and a long branch resolves the same page one commit at a time, each commit permanent history. It reads the DISK through `sourcetree.Walk` rather than the index, because the `.orig` copy a merge tool leaves beside a resolved file carries the conflict whole and is one `git add -A` from a commit, and it fails a walk that read under five hundred text files, since a root resolved to the wrong place passes with no offence found. The seven-`=` divider is refused inside a conflict and everywhere outside Markdown, and allowed in Markdown directly under a line of text, where it is a setext heading — a stated gap rather than a hidden one: a divider left behind with both of its markers deleted reads there exactly like a heading |
| `adr` | The gate behind `adr/`: checks every decision record's fields, Authority, Enforced-by and citations, and that the index lists each |
| `version` | What the binary calls itself: the tag goreleaser stamps at link time (for the image of `main`, `main-` and twelve hex digits of the commit, stamped the same way), falling back to the module's own build info and then to `dev`, so a binary built outside the release path names itself honestly rather than claiming to be a release it is not. `IsDevelopment` is the one PREDICATE over that, and it is an ALLOWLIST of the two unversioned spellings (`dev`, `(devel)`) rather than a test for a release shape — its caller is the development principal, which authenticates every request with no credential, so a spelling this build does not recognise, `main-<commit>` among them, must refuse the bypass rather than enable it. Split as `IsDevelopmentFor` for `resolve`'s reason: the process's own version is a `sync.OnceValue` over link-time state, so no test can put a release in front of the predicate that decides whether a bypass exists — and that predicate is certified beside its caller, in `internal/api/auth`'s development-principal suite. The package's own tests cover the package and nothing else now: four cases over the stamp/build-info/`dev` fallback. They used to be ten times its code and to assert the RELEASE SURFACE instead — the ldflags stamp, the pure-Go build, the image's `${TARGETPLATFORM}` copy, the catch-all notes category, the pre-release flags, the single tag trigger and every Dependabot entry — because each of those fails silently and has no other symptom |
| `testbinary` | WHETHER THIS PROCESS IS A TEST BINARY, for the few rules shipping code holds only a test to — a broker and Gitlet in a test declare their sizes, a node on an external broker reads its account's limit, and a test's broker keeps its store in a `brokertest.TempDir` (`jetstream.ErrUndeclaredSize`, `engine.ErrUndeclaredCodeCache`, `engine.ErrUndeclaredBrokerLimit`, `jetstream.ErrStoreOutsideTempDir`). That last is told by the NAME `brokertest.TempDir` gives its directories (`StoreDirPrefix`, `InStoreDir`), kept here because jetstream cannot import brokertest (it imports `testing`) and brokertest cannot import jetstream (whose tests import it), and a name rather than a record of the directories made, because a case's broker can run in a child test binary on a directory its parent made. Read off the testing package's own flags, which `go test`'s generated main registers before any test runs, and NEVER through `testing.Testing`, the documented answer, because that links the testing package into the crewlet binary, which `cmd/crewlet`'s `TestTheBinaryLinksNoTestScaffolding` refuses. Read at every call rather than once, since a reading taken before the flags were registered would be wrong for the rest of the process. |
